# Invariants

This ledger records invariants of this repository whose change is a policy
change. The workspace-level guide is `INVARIANTS.md` at the workspace root.
When a change adds, removes, relaxes, or reinterprets an invariant here,
update this file in the same change and name the test that checks it.

## Remote task creation

| Invariant | Status | Checked by |
| --- | --- | --- |
| NIP-HOST `task.create` from an enrolled device is an inert inbox submission: it records intent and grants no execution authority. | Relaxed on 2026-09-27 by the owner's auto-start policy ([#9735](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/9735)). Holds whenever the policy is absent, unreadable, or off. | `without_a_policy_creation_is_inert_and_unchanged` and `creation_is_inert_idempotent_and_bound_to_labels` in `crates/coder` |
| Only the host's owner, with a command on the host, turns auto-start on or widens it. A device sends only a workspace label, a title, a prompt, its own images, and at most the engine the person asked for, and cannot choose the routes, model, or limits: the engine only reorders the routes the owner's policy already admits. | New on 2026-09-27. Reinterpreted on 2026-09-29 ([#9969](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/9969)): a request on the host's local control socket (the desktop app's switch or `openagents connect`) is a command on the host, and runs the host's own `coder host autostart`; a device still cannot. The switch changes only whether the policy is on, its projects, and how many run (`on --keep-engine`); the engine the owner set up (controller, routes, full access, usage probes) stays, and removing a project takes it off the policy (2026-09-29). Reinterpreted on 2026-09-30 ([#10076](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/10076)): a start from the host's own chat (`thread.run`, the desktop handoff) puts first the engine that chat's typed `run_coder` offer named, through the host-only `Tasks::prefer`, and only among the routes the owner's policy admits; it never sets a model, a limit, or a route the policy does not admit, and a device's `task.create` still carries no engine. Reinterpreted on 2026-09-30 ([#10081](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/10081)): a device's `task.create` may carry `engine`, the typed engine of its chat's `run_coder` offer, and the host reads it exactly as `Tasks::prefer` (the host's own preference wins when both are present): first only among the policy's admitted routes, never adding a route, a model, or a limit, and the task's summary says plainly when it does not run. A device sends it only to a host whose presence advertises `task-engine`. | `the_command_line_turns_the_policy_on_and_off`, `policies_outside_their_bounds_refuse`, `the_desktop_switch_keeps_the_owners_engine_settings`; `auto_start_changes_run_the_hosts_own_command` in `crates/coder-host/tests/control.rs`; `a_requested_engine_starts_first_when_the_policy_admits_it`, `a_requested_engine_without_capacity_falls_back`, `a_devices_requested_engine_starts_first_when_the_policy_admits_it`, `a_devices_requested_engine_outside_the_policy_falls_back_and_says_why`, `a_devices_requested_engine_at_its_limit_says_until_when` in `crates/coder/src/task/autostart.rs`; `a_phone_runs_coder_from_a_host_threads_offer` in `crates/coder-host/tests/threads.rs`; `a_phone_asks_the_computer_for_the_engine_the_person_named` in `crates/coder/tests/phone_engine.rs` |
| An auto-started task runs only in a workspace the policy lists and the host admits, under a normal operator execution grant that the task owner admits with every usual check. | New on 2026-09-27. | `a_policy_starts_admitted_tasks_within_its_bounds_and_records_each` |
| At most `max_running` auto-started tasks run at once, and every eligible, started, skipped, refused, and no-capacity decision is appended to the host's `autostart.jsonl` before the next. | New on 2026-09-27; `no_capacity` added on 2026-09-28. | `a_policy_starts_admitted_tasks_within_its_bounds_and_records_each`, `turning_the_policy_off_stops_new_starts_and_cancelled_tasks_are_skipped`, `without_capacity_a_task_ends_as_no_capacity_with_the_reset` |
| An auto-started task generates only through a route (provider and model) the owner's policy admits. The host picks the first admitted route that is connected and has capacity; failover during a run picks only among the grant's admitted routes. Neither adds a model, widens a limit, or relaxes a spend bound. | New on 2026-09-28 ([#9831](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/9831)). It reinterprets one admission check: the task's requested model must be one of the grant's admitted routes, not only its primary one. A grant without `fallbacks` admits exactly as before, and a policy without `routes` means exactly what it meant. | `a_task_starts_on_the_first_connected_route_with_capacity`, `an_existing_policy_file_keeps_its_meaning`, `admission_accepts_the_task_model_on_any_admitted_route`, `a_capacity_refusal_fails_over_to_the_next_admitted_route_and_is_recorded` |
| A task that no admitted, connected provider has capacity for does not start a run; it ends as `no_capacity` with the earliest recorded reset, and a device's summary headline says so from typed host state. | New on 2026-09-28. | `without_capacity_a_task_ends_as_no_capacity_with_the_reset`, `with_every_route_exhausted_the_run_ends_as_no_capacity_with_the_earliest_reset` |
| An auto-started task never stays queued forever waiting for its owner process. When the owner exits without admitting it, or has not admitted it within 10 minutes, the host records `unadmitted` and either starts the turn once more (only for an unexplained stop or a refused admission, at most two starts per turn) or cancels it and records `not_started` with a typed cause. A device's summary headline says why from that typed cause, never from the owner's output. | New on 2026-09-29 ([#9985](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/9985)). Before, an unadmitted task was recorded once and left queued. | `an_owner_that_exits_without_admitting_ends_the_task_with_its_reason`, `an_owner_that_stops_without_a_reason_is_started_once_more_then_ends`, `an_owner_that_runs_but_never_admits_ends_the_task_at_the_deadline`, `a_task_an_earlier_host_left_unadmitted_ends_at_the_next_sweep` in `crates/coder`; `every_not_started_cause_survives_the_summary_disclosure_rules` in `crates/coder-host` |
| An auto-started task's commands run inside the filesystem boundary (writes only to the workspace and scratch, no external network, a cleared environment with the system `PATH`) unless the host's owner turned on full access with a command on the host (`coder host autostart on --full-access`). With full access, commands run as the host's user with no sandbox, network access, and the owner's login-shell environment and real `HOME`, `USER`, and `LOGNAME`, less variables named `*_API_KEY`, `*_TOKEN`, or `*_SECRET`; a Claude route's call adds `--permission-mode bypassPermissions` and still has every tool off. A device cannot ask for it, and every grant, right, workspace, route, limit, and concurrency check still applies. A container grant refuses it. | New on 2026-09-28, owner-directed: owner-host Coder tasks need network access and the owner's installed tools. It relaxes the boundary only on the owner's own hosts, for tasks the owner's admitted devices create; the task store and Git directory lose their protection from commands there. Off by default; a policy or grant without `access` keeps the boundary. | `the_owner_turns_on_full_access_on_the_host`, `an_existing_policy_file_keeps_its_meaning` in `crates/coder`; `a_login_listing_keeps_the_path_and_leaves_out_credentials`, `a_shell_that_cannot_answer_leaves_the_fallback_path`; `full_access_runs_as_the_owner_with_no_sandbox`, `full_access_is_refused_for_container_commands`, `a_full_access_call_bypasses_permissions_and_still_runs_no_tool` in `crates/microcoder` |
| A repository turn on a `devin` route runs the local Devin CLI (`devin acp`) with its own stored login, never Devin's cloud API or an API key the host holds. Devin runs its own tools, so the task owner's command boundary does not wrap them: under full access Devin runs in `bypass` mode with the owner's login-shell environment; under the boundary Devin runs with `--sandbox` in `accept-edits` mode, and the host answers every permission request with Devin's own `reject` option. Either way the Devin process gets no variable named `*_API_KEY`, `*_TOKEN`, or `*_SECRET`, runs in its own process group that the host stops when the turn ends or is cancelled, and opens its session with the engine mark in `_meta`. The session must report the admitted model (`default` admits Devin's own). | New on 2026-09-28 ([#9916](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/9916)), owner-directed. It relaxes the boundary row above for Devin routes only: the Devin process itself reaches Devin's service and writes its own session store under `~/.local/share/devin`, and under the boundary its workspace edits are limited by Devin's `accept-edits` mode, not by the task owner's filesystem boundary. A policy without a `devin` route is unchanged. | `a_devin_route_runs_the_recorded_turn_under_full_access`, `the_boundary_runs_devin_sandboxed_and_refuses_what_it_asks`, `a_model_other_than_the_admitted_one_is_refused`, `a_devin_route_is_closed` in `crates/microcoder`; `the_recorded_devin_turn_replays_through_a_session` in `crates/acp-client` |
| A repository turn on an `opencode` route runs OpenCode locally (`opencode acp`) with OpenCode's own stored logins or the owner's configured providers, never an API key the host holds. OpenCode runs its own tools, so the task owner's command boundary does not wrap them: under full access OpenCode's permission is `allow` with the owner's login-shell environment; under the boundary OpenCode may only read, search, and edit inside the workspace, every other tool asks, reaching outside the workspace and asking the user are denied, and the host answers every permission request with OpenCode's own `reject` option. Either way the OpenCode process gets no variable named `*_API_KEY`, `*_TOKEN`, or `*_SECRET`, runs in its own process group that the host stops when the turn ends or is cancelled, saves its session in the engine's own database (`OPENCODE_DB=openagents-coder-engine.db`), never the owner's `opencode.db`, and must report the admitted `provider/model`. A capacity refusal is recorded only from a typed `APIError` with HTTP status 429, read from the engine's database. | New on 2026-09-28 ([#9915](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/9915)), owner-directed. It relaxes the boundary row above for OpenCode routes only, as the Devin row does: the OpenCode process itself reaches the route's provider and writes its engine database under OpenCode's data directory, and under the boundary its workspace edits are limited by OpenCode's permission rules, not by the task owner's filesystem boundary. A policy without an `opencode` route is unchanged. | `an_opencode_route_runs_the_recorded_turn_under_full_access`, `the_boundary_lets_opencode_edit_and_refuses_what_it_asks`, `a_rate_limit_is_recorded_from_the_saved_message_and_ends_without_capacity`, `a_model_other_than_the_admitted_one_is_refused`, `an_opencode_route_is_closed` in `crates/microcoder`; `the_recorded_opencode_turn_replays_through_a_session`, `an_engine_session_runs_on_the_engines_database_with_the_routes_model` in `crates/acp-client`; `the_newest_message_error_is_read_with_its_status` in `crates/coder-history` |
| A repository turn on a `grok` route runs Grok Build locally (`grok agent stdio`) with its own stored login or `XAI_API_KEY`. Under full access the process starts with `--always-approve` and the owner's login-shell environment, with no sandbox. Under this computer's toolchains (`access: toolchains`, a person's local access when they name it; the default is full since [#10104](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/10104)) `--always-approve` is omitted and the whole process runs inside the task owner's own command boundary, built from the same policy as the loop's commands (`Host::engine_boundary`; `sandbox-exec` on macOS, `bwrap` on Linux, an AppContainer on Windows): it and every tool it runs write only the workspace and a private scratch, the task store and common Git directory stay sealed, reads stay confined to the system directories, the workspace, its Git directory, this computer's toolchains, and the resolved `grok` program, with the network; `HOME`, the temporary directory, and `GROK_HOME` are in the scratch, and that Grok home holds only a `0600` copy of the person's `auth.json` (or nothing, with `XAI_API_KEY` passed), removed with the scratch; the person's own Grok home is only read. A login whose earliest `expires_at` falls within the turn's time limit plus ten minutes is never copied: the turn is refused before it starts, since a refresh inside the copy could retire the person's refresh token. The host answers each permission request with the agent's allow-once option, except a file-writing tool (`edit`, `delete`, `move`) whose `locations` or `rawInput` name a path outside the workspace and the scratch, which gets the reject option; a command's writes are limited by the operating-system boundary alone. Under the boundary (`access: boundary`, no network) a Grok Build turn is refused before the process starts. Either way the process gets no variable named `*_API_KEY`, `*_TOKEN`, or `*_SECRET` except `XAI_API_KEY` when that source set it, runs in its own process group that the host stops when the turn ends or is cancelled, and opens its session with the engine mark in `_meta`; only a full-access turn reattaches an earlier turn's session. The session must report the admitted model (`default` admits Grok Build's own). The task transcript is the chat. A turn a whole coding agent (Devin, OpenCode, Grok Build) ended itself is never shown as cancelled or timed out: after the host refused a tool it asked for it ends `engine_stopped_after_refusal`, a `cancelled` it reported with no stop from the host ends `engine_cancelled`, and the adapter summary's `stopped` says in plain words what happened, which the chat shows. | New on 2026-09-30 ([#10052](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/10052)). Changed on 2026-10-01 ([#10092](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/10092)), owner-directed: outside full access, Coder's own boundary, not Grok Build's sandbox or a blanket refusal, is the limit, consistent with Codex and Claude Code runs; before, the host refused every ask, so at the default access a Grok Build turn that wanted a shell command ended "cancelled". It relaxes the boundary row above for Grok Build routes only in that the Grok Build process itself (not only its commands) reaches xAI and holds a copy of its login inside the boundary, where the tools it runs can read it. The boundary and toolchains guarantees of every other engine are unchanged. Model boundary: verified live on macOS with Grok Build 1.0.44; Linux and Windows use the same boundary and are not yet verified live. A policy without a `grok` route is unchanged. The terminal delegate door's provider order is unchanged. | `a_grok_route_runs_the_recorded_turn_under_full_access`, `toolchains_runs_grok_inside_the_boundary_and_allows_what_it_holds`, `the_boundary_refuses_a_grok_turn_before_it_starts`, `a_turn_grok_ended_after_a_refusal_says_so`, `a_model_other_than_the_admitted_one_is_refused`, `a_follow_up_reattaches_the_same_grok_session`, `a_grok_route_is_closed`, and the ignored live `live_grok_at_toolchains_writes_only_the_workspace` in `crates/microcoder`; `the_login_is_checked_without_reading_it`, `the_login_expiry_is_read_and_nothing_else`, `the_model_and_the_arguments_are_explicit`, `a_writing_tool_names_its_paths_and_a_command_names_none`, `permission_options_are_read_by_kind` in `crates/acp-client`; `a_login_listing_keeps_the_path_and_leaves_out_credentials` in `crates/coder`; `endings_map_to_their_events`, `an_agent_that_stopped_after_a_refusal_says_so` in `crates/openagents-chat`; the release gate's `delegate-grok` |
| A repository task runs only in a workspace that is the top level of its own Git checkout; an empty or plain directory inside another repository is refused by `coder host autostart on` and at admission. | New on 2026-09-28, after a host's workspace was an empty directory inside the owner's workspace repository. | `the_owner_turns_on_full_access_on_the_host`, `an_empty_directory_inside_a_repository_is_not_a_workspace` |
| A usage probe reads a provider's OAuth access token only when the owner turned probes on (`coder host autostart on --probe-usage`), only in the `coder` host process, and sends it only to that provider's own usage endpoint (`api.anthropic.com/api/oauth/usage`, `chatgpt.com/backend-api/wham/usage`). The token is never written, logged, stored in `usage.json`, or sent elsewhere, and no credential store is modified. A probed reading is advisory: it only reorders the admitted, connected routes that have no holding refusal, supplies the reset of a refusal whose provider reported none, and lifts a usage-limit refusal that a reading observed after it contradicts (the provider does not say its limit is reached and no window is at 95 %), for every reader of the capacity book (`capacity::Book::load`); it never adds a route, never lifts a rate limit or a refusal a later reading does not contradict, and any probe failure falls back to refusal-only routing. | New on 2026-09-28 ([#9837](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/9837)), owner-directed. It widens which process reads the Claude credential: before, only Claude Code read it; now `coder` reads it too, while probes are on. `microcoder` still never reads a credential file; it reads only `usage.json`'s typed readings. Reinterpreted on 2026-09-28 ([#9892](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/9892)): a fresh reading's full window supplies an unreported reset in place of the flat 30-minute hold. Relaxed on 2026-09-30 ([#10073](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/10073)): Codex refused on 09-28 with a week's limit until 10-03, its probe read 8 % with a new reset two days later, and the start card still said Codex was limited; a later reading under the limit now lifts a usage-limit refusal, and the start card names no fallback the start passed over. Off by default; a policy without `usage_probe` means exactly what it meant. Reinterpreted on 2026-10-01 ([#10105](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/10105)): the owner signed out of an exhausted Claude account and into one with capacity, and Coder still passed Claude over; a start now has the probe read again, before it passes an admitted provider over for capacity on a reading 60 seconds old or older, and always for the engine the person asked for (`usage::refresh_with`'s fresh list, within five seconds once, never against a provider's `Retry-After`); a start on the person's own computer asks its host for that over the control socket (`engine_refresh`), so the token is still read only in the `coder` host process and only while probes are on. On macOS the Claude token is read from the keychain item before `~/.claude/.credentials.json`, and `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` is honored, as Claude Code itself reads them. | `routing_passes_over_a_provider_at_its_probed_limit`, `with_usage_probes_a_start_avoids_a_provider_above_the_threshold`, `a_failing_usage_probe_falls_back_to_refusal_only_routing`, `a_claude_credential_is_read_typed_and_never_printed`, `without_a_reported_reset_a_fresh_probe_reading_supplies_it`, `a_claude_refusal_without_a_reset_records_the_probed_one`, `a_later_reading_under_the_limit_lifts_a_usage_refusal` in `crates/microcoder-loop`, `codex_first_then_claude_code_with_the_reason` in `crates/coder/src/task/local.rs`, `malformed_answers_are_typed_failures`, `an_existing_policy_file_keeps_its_meaning`, `a_fresh_probe_skips_the_cache_but_not_a_retry_after` in `crates/microcoder-loop`, `a_stale_reading_is_probed_again_before_an_engine_is_passed_over` (`autostart`), `a_start_has_the_host_read_an_engine_again_before_passing_it_over` (`local`), `the_claude_credentials_file_is_where_claude_code_keeps_it` (`usage`) in `crates/coder`, `engine_refresh_reads_the_asked_engines_now_then_reports` in `crates/coder-host` |
| A usage reading in `usage.json` and a refusal hold in `capacity.json` apply only to the login they were observed on: each keeps a salted fingerprint of that login (`sha256(salt ‖ provider ‖ identity)` cut to 128 bits, the salt 32 random bytes in the task store's `account.salt`, `0600`), and every reader of the books (`capacity::Book::load`, `usage::Book::load`) drops a reading or hold whose fingerprint differs from the login signed in now; a dropped reading leaves its provider due for a probe. The identity is read only from non-secret metadata, never a token, in any process that reads the books, with probes on or off: Claude Code's `oauthAccount.accountUuid` (else `emailAddress`) and `organizationUuid` in `~/.claude.json` (or under `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`), Codex's `tokens.account_id` in `auth.json`, and Grok Build's per-login `user_id` (else `email`) in `auth.json`; the identity is never written, logged, or sent, and no credential store is modified. A login none of those name (an API key, a login without an id) has no fingerprint, and its readings and holds keep their earlier meaning; so do readings and holds recorded before fingerprints. The desktop reads the engine report on every screen while its window shows, every 60 seconds, at once when shown or focused, on a new chat, and after a run is passed over or refused for capacity. | New on 2026-10-01 ([#10105](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/10105)), owner-directed: a refusal hold and a usage reading outlived the login they were about, and the sidebar meter stayed full. | `each_engines_identity_is_read_from_metadata_only`, `a_fingerprint_is_salted_per_store_and_stable_within_one` (`account`), `a_hold_stops_holding_once_another_login_is_signed_in` (`capacity`), `a_reading_from_another_login_is_dropped_and_probed_again` (`usage`) in `crates/microcoder-loop`; `an_account_change_clears_the_hold_and_the_next_start_uses_the_engine` in `crates/coder` (`autostart`); `the_window_reads_the_engine_and_cannot_change_it` (`model`), `a_new_chat_and_focus_read_the_engine_again` (`shell`) in `crates/openagents-desktop` |
| Archiving removes a task only from device lists: an archived task's summary is never published again and its transcript lists as an archived chat, while its record, commands, evidence, and transcript stay in place. Only a finished or cancelled task archives, and only the owner restores one. | New on 2026-09-28. | `only_an_ended_task_is_archived_and_nothing_is_deleted`, `an_archived_task_leaves_the_current_list_and_only_an_ended_one_archives` in `crates/coder`; `a_task_the_owner_archived_lists_as_an_archived_chat` in `crates/coder-history`; `the_coder_list_leaves_out_archived_tasks` in `crates/openagents-mobile` |
| Once a repository turn's task stops (cancelled, or refused by its host), the Microcoder loop makes no further model call or command and ends as `stopped`. A model call in flight when the stop lands counts as interrupted, not failed: its cost stays unknown in the outcome and its trajectory records no failed reply for it. The turn's event stream ends with exactly one `stopped` and no `failure`, and its history shows no failed model call. | New on 2026-09-30 ([#10050](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/10050)). Before, a refused call after the stop was recorded as a failed reply ("The model call failed: the task cannot make this transition") until three ended the loop. | `a_stop_while_a_command_runs_ends_the_loop_with_no_further_model_call`, `a_model_call_cut_short_by_a_stop_is_interrupted_not_failed`, `a_stop_before_the_first_step_makes_no_model_call` in `crates/microcoder-loop`; `cancellation_reaps_the_current_command_and_dispatches_no_followup`, `interrupted_model_request_keeps_unknown_cost_and_starts_no_command`, `a_turn_stopped_while_its_command_runs_ends_once_as_stopped` in `crates/microcoder` |
| A Coder run for a person (a local run from the CLI, the desktop, or the phone through a host, a host's auto-started task, the issue flow, and the delegate door) has no step budget and no time budget. It ends only when Coder finishes or asks, when the person stops it (or its host refuses to go on), or when the loop's stuck guard ends it (`Ending::Stuck`): `STUCK_STEPS` (8) judged steps in a row, never fewer than `MIN_STUCK_STEPS` (4), in which Jev judged the recent steps repeating an approach that already failed (`repeating` at least 0.5) and the last one not moving the work forward (`progress` under 0.5), or, on a step Jev did not answer, every command of the last step had already run with the same exit and output. The loop's other guards (three unusable replies, three idle replies) and, for a whole coding agent, twenty minutes of silence stay; each command keeps its own limit; the delegate door keeps its spend cap. A policy, grant, or issue-flow file written before this that carries `max_steps`, `wall_seconds`, or `continue_turns` still reads without error, and the values are ignored; nothing writes them now. The running line shows the step, Jev's estimate of how much of the task is complete (a NIP-DEC Score asked in the step's one judgment request, carried as `complete` in the `progress` event), and the time, never a budget. | New on 2026-10-01 ([#10103](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/10103)), owner-directed. Before, chat and host runs stopped at 24 steps and 30 minutes, the delegate door at 40 steps (80 for an issue) and the executor deadline, and the issue flow at its policy's `max_steps` with up to two continuation turns (#10063). Model boundary: the guard is Jev's judgment, so a run Jev misjudges as progressing runs until the person stops it, and one it misjudges as stuck ends early. | `a_run_with_no_budget_goes_past_the_old_limits`, `a_repeating_run_without_progress_still_ends`, `the_stuck_guard_falls_back_to_the_rule_and_has_a_minimum_window`, `each_judgment_carries_the_completion_estimate` in `crates/microcoder-loop`; `a_run_past_the_old_step_limits_keeps_going_and_lands`, `a_repeating_run_is_ended_by_the_stuck_guard` in `crates/microcoder/src/repository/tests.rs`; `an_existing_policy_file_keeps_its_meaning`, `a_task_starts_on_the_first_connected_route_with_capacity` in `crates/coder/src/task/autostart.rs`; `a_policy_file_is_read_and_its_absence_is_the_safe_default` in `crates/coder/src/task/issue_run_tests.rs`; `the_running_line_names_no_budget` in `crates/openagents-chat` |
| A task's own owner process (its launcher, its admission, and every record it makes while it runs) waits a bounded two minutes for its task's write lock another process holds, instead of failing the task at the five seconds a device-facing write waits; a task still busy after that fails as before, and a running turn's stop check treats a busy store as no stop. A reader following a task (`openagents chat follow`, send-follow, and the chat issue flow's wait for its turn) takes no lock and never waits on a writer; only a store another process is initializing or migrating reads as nothing new yet, polled again, failing once it has stayed busy for the same two minutes; a reader's failure never records, stops, or cancels the task. | New on 2026-09-28 ([#9909](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/9909)), after slow disk syncs on a nearly full volume held the store past five seconds. It changes only how long an owner waits, not what it may do. Extended to readers on 2026-09-30 ([#10059](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/10059)), after a chat issue flow released its claim on a store busy past five seconds under heavy load. Narrowed on 2026-10-02 ([#10231](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/10231)): the store is one file per task, so a write waits only for a writer of the same task and reads take no lock. | `the_owner_process_waits_out_a_store_busy_past_the_lock_wait`, `a_follower_waits_out_a_store_another_process_holds_then_continues`, `a_follower_reads_a_store_held_for_migration_as_nothing_new_yet` in `crates/coder` |

Each task file replays its own commands and owner events and is refused when any materialized fact or receipt disagrees. A command identity names the same bytes forever, across tasks and actions; expected revisions are enforced under the task lock; a refused command reserves nothing; an exact retry is acknowledged only after a durability barrier. Nothing is pruned: `MAX_TASKS` and `MAX_COMMANDS` stay global, and the 8192 owner events are bounded per task. An admission or check intent holds `workspaces.lock` while it checks every other task, so two unresolved runs never share a tree. | Coder never writes the checkout its worktree was made from, even with full access. At admission (`coder::task::adapter`), a workspace that is a linked Git worktree gets a `coder_boundary::source::Guard`: writes are denied beneath the main working tree (`git worktree list`'s first entry, unless bare) and the common Git directory, and allowed back only for the worktree itself (when it lies inside), its own administrative directory (`.git/worktrees/<name>`), and the object store (`.git/objects`, content-addressed, so a commit in the worktree can store its objects). Everything else there stays unwritten: the checkout's files, index, and `HEAD`, every ref (branches, tags, remote-tracking refs, the stash), `packed-refs`, the configuration, and the hooks. Every full-access command and every whole coding agent's process (Grok Build, OpenCode, Devin, whatever it approves for itself) runs under the guard: on macOS in the `sandbox-exec` profile beside the privacy rules; on Linux under `bwrap --dev-bind / /` with the checkout and its Git directory bound read-only and the allowed paths bound writable over them, so everything else (devices, `/proc`, the network, the rest of the disk) is as full access always had it. Each guarded process gets `GIT_CEILING_DIRECTORIES` at the worktree's parent; `GIT_DIR` and `GIT_WORK_TREE` are not set, because they would retarget every Git command the run makes, test fixtures and clones included, at the worktree. A guard that can't be enforced (Linux without a working `bwrap`) refuses the run at admission; a workspace that is not a linked worktree has nothing to guard. Under the boundary and toolchains access the write boundary already denies the checkout. | New on 2026-10-02 ([#10247](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/10247)): in the shadow-baseline study (#10209) every routed `fix-git` run, 12 of 12, `cd`'d into the person's checkout and merged into its `master`. | An image reaches a Coder task only as its exact bytes on the computer that runs the task. A device sends it with NIP-HOST `artifact.put` (needs `operate`): sequential chunks of at most 32 KiB, each naming the image's SHA-256 digest, type, and size; only PNG and JPEG (by signature), at most 8 MiB an image and 4 a task, at most 8 unclaimed uploads a device, each dropped after a day. The host keeps uploads per device (`uploads/<device>/` in the task store), checks the whole image against its digest and type when the last byte arrives, and drops a mismatch. A chunk is idempotent where the host keeps it, so its reply is not retained (a resend answers what is held, a conflicting resend is refused). `task.create` names images only by digest; the host binds only complete images that same device sent (a retried create finds the task's copy), keeps them at `media/<task>/<hex>` (`0600`), and the task's intent names them, so the execution grant, which binds the intent's digest, admits exactly those bytes. A desktop chat's local run keeps them the same way (`Local::start_with_images`). The engine reads them back checked against their digests before any model call and passes them natively on every step: Codex as Responses `input_image` data URLs, Claude Code as `stream-json` base64 `image` blocks. Whole-agent routes (Devin, OpenCode, Grok Build) take no images: they are left out of an image task's routes with the reason recorded, and a task no remaining route can serve, or whose bytes differ, ends refused before any model call; a local run whose first route cannot take images is refused before anything is saved. Routes that carry words only (a running Coder task's chat, a computer's own thread, the host's `RunCoder` handoff) refuse a send whose draft holds images and keep the words and images. A draft's images leave the device only with an explicit start of Coder from that chat (**Run Coder**, or the desktop's coding reply that starts it), to the computer that runs it (the phone's ready host over NIP-HOST, the desktop's own local run), both built from `attachments::Drafts::uploads`, which refuses an image that is not PNG or JPEG or over 8 MiB before anything is sent. ## Coder change review and publication

| Invariant | Status | Checked by |
| --- | --- | --- |
| A Coder task's change is shown at exact revisions: its base commit, its worktree's `HEAD`, and the tree of the worktree's content when read, written through a private index so the worktree, its index, and its refs never change. File and line counts are Git's for that base and tree; a binary file's counts are unknown, never zero. A diff cut at its bound, or one Git could not write, says so (`Completeness`), and the diff is read with bounded memory. | New on 2026-09-30 ([#10067](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/10067)). | `a_review_names_exact_revisions_and_counts_on_a_scratch_repository`, `a_cut_diff_says_so_and_keeps_whole_counts` in `crates/coder`; `a_cut_or_unreadable_diff_never_reads_as_complete`, `the_card_names_exact_revisions_and_whole_counts` in `crates/openagents-chat-app` |
| A read naming another head than the shown one marks the view stale; the stale view stays on screen, cannot publish, and is replaced only by **Refresh**. | New on 2026-09-30 ([#10067](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/10067)). | `a_moved_worktree_makes_the_view_stale` in `crates/coder`; `a_moved_head_makes_the_view_stale_and_refuses_to_publish_it` in `crates/openagents-chat-app`; `a_reviewed_run_goes_stale_refreshes_and_publishes_once` in `crates/openagents-desktop`; `a_phone_reviews_a_change_refreshes_a_stale_view_and_publishes_once` in `crates/openagents-mobile` |
| Reading a change (`task.review`) needs `observe` and is never retained; publishing it (`task.publish`) is a mutation that needs `operate`, checked by the device and again by the host before the task owner sees it. Reading or opening a diff grants nothing. | New on 2026-09-30 ([#10068](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/10068)). | `publishing_a_reviewed_change_needs_operate` in `crates/coder-access`; `publishing_a_change_needs_operate_and_reading_it_needs_observe` in `crates/coder-computers` |
| A publication commits exactly the reviewed tree on the reviewed `HEAD` and pushes it once: fast-forward onto the repository's branch only when `.openagents/coder-issues.json` lands on `main`, otherwise to a branch of its own with a draft pull request. It never rebases, force-pushes, or changes the worktree. Its identity is the task and the reviewed revisions; a head the worktree has moved past is refused with nothing pushed; progress is recorded before each effect; an uncertain push is reconciled by reading the remote before any second push, and a pull request is looked up by branch before one is opened. | New on 2026-09-30 ([#10068](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/10068)). | `a_reviewed_change_publishes_once_as_a_draft_pull_request`, `a_policy_that_lands_on_main_pushes_onto_it_fast_forward_only`, `an_uncertain_push_reconciles_by_reading_the_remote_without_pushing_twice`, `an_uncertain_push_that_did_not_land_is_pushed_once_on_retry`, `a_stale_review_is_refused_and_nothing_is_pushed`, `a_forge_that_fails_leaves_the_branch_pushed_and_a_retry_opens_it_without_pushing` in `crates/coder` |

See [Coder on this computer](docs/desktop/local-coder.md#what-changed).

## Relay notification delivery

| Invariant | Status | Checked by |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Every stored event reaches each live subscriber of a relay process once, in `ingest_seq` order, never before its commit, and never skipped. Losing the Postgres notification listener, or a notification naming a sequence the database doesn't hold, does not stop the process: it keeps serving, replaces the listener with backoff, reads the high-water mark on the new listening connection, and catches up through it by sequence in reads of at most 4,096 positions. Ephemeral events sent while no listener was connected are not replayed. | Reinterpreted on 2026-09-29 ([#9947](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/9947)). Before, a lost listener, a gap over 4,096 positions, or a notification past the high-water mark stopped the process and closed every client; Cloud Run then restarted it. Delivery order and no-skip are unchanged. | `the_relay_keeps_serving_and_catches_up_when_its_listener_is_killed` in `crates/nostr-relay/tests/listener_postgres.rs`; `m4_two_process_gap_and_chaos_contract` in `crates/nostr-relay/tests/multiprocess_postgres.rs`; `a_long_outage_is_read_back_in_bounded_steps`, `a_notification_past_the_database_is_dropped_without_skipping_real_events` in `crates/nostr-relay` (`gateway::server`) |
| A cancelled history read fails only its own request. A statement cancelled on a relay worker's connection (SQLSTATE 57014) never stops the process, and a cancelled read waits for its statement to end before the connection runs another. | New on 2026-09-29 ([#9947](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/9947)): each of the four relay crashes on 2026-09-29 came 20 to 50 ms after Postgres logged a history read `canceling statement due to user request`. | `a_cancelled_read_never_fails_the_next_statement` in `crates/nostr-relay/tests/listener_postgres.rs` |

## Device commands

| Invariant | Status | Checked by |
| --- | --- | --- |
| A device command runs at most once: the host keys it by device and device-minted ID, records it before evaluating it, records the exact task-owner command before applying it, and applies those bytes again after a crash; a replay returns the recorded disposition. | New on 2026-09-28 ([#9835](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/9835)). | `follow_ups_continue_the_task_and_replays_never_run_twice`, `a_recorded_dispatch_is_applied_again_byte_for_byte_after_a_crash` in `crates/coder` |
| An interrupt supersedes only an older interrupt, never runs, queues, or steers anything, and never stops a turn newer than the one it was based on; commands expire 24 hours after the device minted them. | New on 2026-09-28. | `an_interrupt_supersedes_only_older_interrupts_and_never_a_past_turn`, `expired_and_revoked_commands_never_run` |
| A command that waits for a turn to end runs only while its sender still holds `operate` under the same grant and epoch. | New on 2026-09-28. Tightened later on 2026-09-28: another device's command or queue edit that lets a held command run rechecks that command's sender too, where before it was treated as admitted. | `a_held_command_rechecks_its_sender_before_it_runs`, `a_queue_listing_shows_a_device_only_its_own_text`, `a_task_command_reaches_the_owner_with_its_grant_and_epoch` in `crates/coder-access` |
| An `answer` starts the next turn only while the task's ended turn asked a question or (in a task recorded before [#10104](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/10104)) asked to approve a step, and only from a device that read that turn; the first answer wins and a later or competing one is refused. An answer is data for the engine: it never widens the task's grant, workspace, routes, boundary, or spend, and it is not a POL approval. A summary of a waiting task says only that it waits, never the question. | New on 2026-09-28 ([#9835](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/9835)). | `an_answer_continues_only_a_turn_that_asked_and_the_first_one_wins` in `crates/coder`; `a_question_ends_the_turn_waiting_and_the_first_answer_continues_it`, `a_question_ends_the_turn_only_when_someone_can_answer` in `crates/microcoder`; `a_waiting_question_or_approval_asks_for_attention_without_its_text` in `crates/coder-host` |
| Coder approves every step by default and never asks permission ([#10104](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/10104)). A repository turn's loop is told every step is already approved (committing, pushing, to `main` too, deleting, installing, any command the task needs) and never to ask for permission or confirmation; its step schema offers `ask` only as `none` or `question`, a question only for an answer only the person has, such as a choice between approaches. A step that still asks for approval (a reply naming `approval`, which still parses) never ends the turn: its commands run and the next step is told the approval stands, so a run ends with its result and no approval event; `asked_approval` and the approval event still read and render for tasks recorded before. A person's own local runs default to `coder.access: full`, so every engine's own permission asks are granted (Claude Code `--permission-mode bypassPermissions`, Grok Build `--always-approve`, Devin's bypass mode, OpenCode's allow rule, Codex's commands unsandboxed as the person) and Git commits and pushes with the person's own setup; a settings file that names `toolchains` or `boundary` keeps that boundary, under which Devin and OpenCode asks are still refused. The terminal delegate door's engines already run with `--dangerously-bypass-approvals-and-sandbox` and `bypassPermissions`. A person's **Stop** still stops a run, `coder.start: ask_first` still offers before starting when the person set it, and a device's auto-start policy, device enrollment, and spend requests keep their own grants. | New on 2026-10-01, owner-directed: "default approval of everything": no approval prompts or confirmation questions for pushing, committing, running commands, or other actions on the owner's own computer; Coder does the work end to end. It reverses the 2026-09-28 approval ask ([#9835](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/9835)), whose prompt told the model to ask "before a step with consequences ... such as deleting data or pushing" (the source of a Codex run asking to push to `main`), and the `toolchains` default of [#10045](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/10045) for a person's own runs: the task store and Git directory lose their protection from those runs' commands, as they already did under the owner's full access. | `an_approval_ask_is_granted_and_the_run_goes_on` in `crates/microcoder-loop`; `every_other_ending_is_its_own_event` in `crates/microcoder`; `full_access_is_the_default_and_a_named_boundary_still_reads` in `crates/coder/src/task/settings.rs`; `codex_first_then_claude_code_with_the_reason` in `crates/coder/src/task/local.rs`; `settings_change_the_local_run_and_the_defaults_change_nothing` in `crates/openagents-cli/tests/chat.rs`; `the_client_starts_at_once_and_approves_everything_by_default` in `crates/coder/src/task/chat_client.rs` and `a_coding_reply_starts_coder_at_once_and_streams_its_events` in `crates/openagents-chat` (the chat client `openagents chat` and OpenAgents Terminal share, #10108); the release gate's `push-main` |
| A waiting command never continues an archived task: once a task is archived, its held messages and any later send, queue, steer, or answer refuse as `conflict`. | New on 2026-09-28, after a live test's queued message started a turn on a task the test had archived. | `a_queued_message_never_revives_an_archived_task` in `crates/coder` |
| While a device holds a task's queue edit lease, no queued message is promoted; the lease lapses 60 seconds after its last renewal and another device's lease attempt refuses. A device edits, removes, or sends now only its own held messages, a reorder moves only the queued messages, and an edit keeps the original request so a replay still matches and never runs twice. | New on 2026-09-28. | `a_leased_queue_waits_and_an_edit_keeps_the_request_for_replay`, `a_lapsed_lease_lets_the_queue_run_and_a_removed_message_never_runs`, `a_message_sent_now_stops_the_turn_and_runs_ahead_of_the_queue` in `crates/coder` |
| The phone picks a command's action from the task's phase and the attention its summary asks for, or from a composer choice token it minted, never from the message text. A command that cannot reach its computer, because the transport failed or the computer is offline or out of date, stays in the outbox with its ID; only a signed refusal ends it. | New on 2026-09-28. The second sentence fixes the first stage, which dropped a command sent to an offline computer. | `the_composer_mode_follows_the_task_and_its_attention`, `a_long_press_offers_queue_or_stop_and_send_while_coder_works`, `an_unreached_computer_is_nudged_and_the_command_waits` in `crates/openagents-mobile`; `a_composer_answers_with_its_token_or_a_choices_token` in `crates/rust-native` |
| A nudge carries no command, text, or task and grants nothing. The host answers only a current nudge signed by an actively enrolled device, and only by evaluating held commands and publishing presence and hints to that device, at most once per nudge and every 30 seconds per device. | New on 2026-09-28. | `only_the_addressed_host_opens_a_current_nudge_from_its_signer`, `each_nudge_is_answered_once_and_a_device_at_most_every_interval`, `a_nudge_from_an_enrolled_device_brings_fresh_presence_at_once` in `crates/coder-host` |
| A follow-up turn is an inert submission unless the owner's auto-start policy admits it under the same workspace, model, route, and concurrency bounds as a new task. A follow-up never widens a grant or reuses an earlier turn's grant. | New on 2026-09-28. It extends the remote-task-creation rows to later turns. | `a_follow_up_starts_its_turn_only_within_the_policy_bounds`, `a_follow_up_runs_as_the_next_turn_and_carries_the_earlier_one` in `crates/microcoder` |

## Steering

| Invariant | Status | Checked by |
| --- | --- | --- |
| A host never treats a new turn, an enqueue, or a restart as native steering: native steering of a running turn is refused unless the engine steers mid-turn, and emulation (cancel and continue) runs only when the caller chose it. | New on 2026-09-28 ([#9836](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/9836)); restates NIP-SESS for the adapters in code. | `native_steering_an_engine_lacks_is_refused_unless_emulation_was_chosen`, `each_adapter_reports_a_steering_mode_that_matches_what_it_demonstrated`, `microcoder_states_that_it_steers_only_at_a_turn_boundary` |
| An accepted steer is not a consumed one: consumption is recorded only from the adapter's stated acknowledgment, as its own trace step, and a correction stays in the unconsumed ledger until a run's admission reads it. | New on 2026-09-28. | `a_steer_is_consumed_when_the_next_turn_starts_and_recorded_as_its_own_step`, `only_engine_evidence_confirms_consumption` |

## Linking devices

| Invariant | Status | Checked by |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Tailscale, SSH, a relay, an iroh connection, a QR scan, and nearby discovery only introduce devices. Rights come only from a host-signed NIP-HOST grant, and every invitation carries the rights its issuer set: a connect code from the desktop app, `openagents connect invite`, or `openagents connect --ssh`, and a nearby approval, carry the pairing grant: every right (`observe`, `operate`, `terminal`, `review`, `access_read`, `access_admin`), because the phone paired that way is the owner's own. An iroh `EndpointId` never admits anything: on `openagents/reach/1` the NIP-REACH handshake proves both Nostr keys and the grant check decides, as over TCP, and a key the host never enrolled reaches only `openagents/enroll/1`, where only a redemption of a retained invitation with its capability earns a signed reply. | New on 2026-09-27 ([#9731](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/9731)). Extended to iroh, QR codes, and nearby discovery on 2026-09-29 ([#9969](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/9969)). The old setup commands were removed on 2026-09-29 ([#9978](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/9978)); the row stands for its replacements, which introduce devices the same way. Owner-directed on 2026-09-29 ([#9965](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/9965)): every pairing grants full permission, and no screen or flag narrows it. The phone keeps only that grant: the narrower sets earlier codes carried are refused, owner-directed the same day since no computer still issues them. | `only_the_connect_code_rights_are_kept` in `crates/coder-host/src/client/iroh.rs`; `an_unknown_key_reaches_only_enrollment_and_a_grant_admits_only_its_device`, `a_phone_redeems_a_connect_code_over_iroh_and_a_second_phone_is_forbidden`, `a_terminal_opens_over_iroh_only_with_the_terminal_right`, `revocation_closes_an_open_iroh_channel_before_its_next_message` in `crates/coder-host/tests/iroh.rs` |
| A host with NIP-HOST tailnet admission on issues a single-use invitation, never a grant, only to a caller that its local `tailscale whois` names as the host machine's own untagged Tailscale user, with the rights the operator chose. Every other caller gets a refusal and no invitation. The invitation is issued from the access store with the host key from the same key source the serving host signs with (the keychain under `--keychain`, `DIR` under `--keys DIR`, else the store's key file), and admission starts only when that key is the serving host's key; otherwise it stays off. The control socket's `tailnet_status` reports whether it serves and on which tailnet address, or why it is off. | New on 2026-09-27, owner-approved. It relaxes the row above only when the operator runs `coder host serve --tailnet-admission RIGHTS`: Tailscale identity then admits the device to an invitation, and the host still signs the grant on redemption. Off by default. On 2026-09-29 the owner kept it as an optional path alongside QR pairing and iroh; it is not deprecated. Reinterpreted on 2026-10-01 ([#10125](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/10125)): a host serving with `--keychain` read the key from the store's file, found none, and left admission off; the key source and the serving-key check now hold for every key source. | `refuses_malformed_and_non_tailnet_callers_before_whois`, `parses_status_and_whois`, and `admission_uses_the_serving_hosts_key_source` in `crates/coder-host`; `the_socket_is_private_and_serves_only_this_user` (`tailnet_status`) in `crates/coder-host/tests/control.rs`; `live_tailnet_admission_adds_the_computer_and_its_chats` (ignored, live) in `crates/openagents-mobile` |
| A device key holds one NIP-HOST grant at a host: a new grant to a device (invitation redemption or approval) revokes that device's earlier grants in the same commit without advancing its epoch, and they leave the book once no retained invitation or enrollment names them; a request under a grant that left is refused and reads nothing. Grants other devices hold, including ones the device delegated, are untouched; an unredeemed invitation it issued moves to the new grant only from a grant live until then and only when the new grant holds `access_admin` and every invited right and outlives the invited grant, else it is refused as `revoked`. A dead grant never blocks a new one: while the book's 128 grants are full, the longest-dead unnamed grant leaves it; only 128 live or named grants refuse a new device. The one exception is renewal (the next row): a renewed grant stays admitted beside its renewal until its own expiry. | New on 2026-09-28 ([#9908](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/9908)), after live test runs filled a host's grants with one fixed key. It narrows authority (a re-enrolled device loses its earlier grant, and commands it queued under that grant no longer run) and never extends a delegation past what the new grant could issue. | `a_device_that_enrolls_again_holds_one_grant`, `revoked_grants_never_block_a_new_enrollment`, `delegations_survive_their_issuer_enrolling_again` in `crates/coder-access` |
| A paired device never has to pair again while it keeps connecting: when a direct channel (TCP, WebSocket, or iroh) opens or runs under a current grant that the host or its owner issued and that has a quarter or less of its lifetime left, the host signs a new grant with the same device, relay, rights, epoch, origin, and lifetime (at most 30 days), sends it on the channel (`openagents.host-grant-renewal.v1`), and the channel follows it. The renewed grant stays admitted until its own expiry, so nothing already signed is cut off. A revoked, expired, or stale grant, a grant that is not the device's newest, and a grant a device delegated never renew; a device accepts a renewal only with the same terms (`Access::renewed`). | New on 2026-09-29 ([#9969](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/9969)), owner-directed: a QR pairing lasts 30 days, and a monthly rescan is not acceptable. | `a_grant_renews_near_its_end_with_the_same_terms_and_the_device_accepts_it`, `a_revoked_device_never_renews_and_loses_both_grants`, `a_delegated_grant_does_not_renew` in `crates/coder-access`; `a_grant_near_its_end_is_renewed_on_the_channel_and_the_channel_follows_it` in `crates/coder-host/tests/iroh.rs` |
| A device clock up to 60 seconds behind or ahead of the host's pairs and is served: the host admits a request dated up to 60 seconds before the invitation or grant it names, and a device accepts a reply dated up to 60 seconds before its request. Expiry is still the host's clock, held strictly. | New on 2026-09-29 ([#9969](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/9969)); it reinterprets the existing 60-second skew, which a request dated before its invitation did not get. | `a_phone_whose_clock_is_off_by_thirty_seconds_still_pairs_and_works` in `crates/coder-host/tests/iroh.rs` |
| The local control socket is `0600` in a `0700` directory, and the host serves a connection only when the kernel reports the peer's user ID equal to its own (`getpeereid` on macOS, `SO_PEERCRED` on Linux), closing any other before reading a byte. On Windows the channel is the named pipe `\\.\pipe\openagents-control-<user SID>`, created first-instance-only and remote-rejecting with a protected DACL whose one entry is the host's own SID (no Administrators, SYSTEM, or Everyone), and the host serves a client only when the client process's token user equals its own; it serves the pipe with the same protocol and answers as the socket, and a host that starts itself again waits up to five seconds for its predecessor's pipe to close, never sharing the name. A caller it serves is the local operator: it creates and cancels invitations, lists and revokes devices, sets projects and the auto-start policy, and imports an owner key only through the grant store and the host's own commands. No device, grant, relay message, or channel reaches it. Once the desktop app manages the host, it is the only local path that changes access or auto-start. Its `chat` operation may name the caller (`router::Caller`, #10108), and a caller is only the desktop or a terminal on this computer (`Caller::local`); a phone or website caller is refused as `malformed`, and a chat with no caller is the desktop's. The caller only names the turn's surface and client word for the router; it grants nothing. A connect code it mints grants the pairing grant (every right); `invite_create` and `nearby_decide` carry no rights field, and one with a `terminal` or `rights` field does not parse. | New on 2026-09-29 ([#9969](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/9969)); the Windows pipe added on 2026-09-29 ([#9977](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/9977)) and served by the host from 2026-09-29 ([#9980](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/9980)). Extended on 2026-10-01 ([#10108](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/10108)): the `chat` operation's caller, so a terminal's turn through the host says `terminal`. | `control_pipe_dacl_names_only_the_user`, `control_pipe_admits_only_the_same_user`, `control_pipe_name_is_per_user`, `control_pipe_sid_shapes`, `control_pipe_admits_this_user_on_windows`, `control_pipe_names_are_local_and_single` in `crates/coder-host/src/control/windows.rs`; `the_pipe_serves_this_user_and_a_second_host_cannot_bind_it` (Windows) in `crates/coder-host/tests/control.rs`; `the_socket_is_private_and_serves_only_this_user`, `a_widened_directory_is_made_private_and_a_stale_socket_is_replaced`, `codes_are_minted_rotated_and_cancelled_over_the_socket`, `an_owner_key_is_imported_only_once_no_phone_holds_a_grant`, `auto_start_changes_run_the_hosts_own_command` in `crates/coder-host/tests/control.rs`; `a_terminal_turn_through_the_host_says_terminal` in `crates/coder-host/tests/threads.rs` |
| Outside the desktop app, the owner secret key stays in one private file on the owner's computer (`~/.openagents/coder-owner/owner.key`), never in an argument or a log line; `openagents connect --ssh --import-owner` reads it only to send its public half. Under the desktop app (`coder host serve --keychain`), the owner, host, and iroh secret keys live in the login keychain (the macOS keychain, the Secret Service on Linux, or Credential Manager on Windows as generic credentials named `<account>.com.openagents.desktop` and kept on this computer only; service `com.openagents.desktop`, accounts `owner-key`, `host-key`, `host-iroh-key`), read only by the host process and never in a file, an argument, or a log line; the host makes its own owner on first start. The one exception is a Linux desktop where no Secret Service answers (`coder host adopt detect` reports `"keys": "files"`): there the app's host serves with `--keys ~/.openagents/host-keys`, `0600` files in a `0700` directory as a CLI install keeps them, rather than not starting; the keychain is never silently swapped for files where it answers. CLI-only installs keep the files. `openagents connect owner import` reads the key from stdin, never an argument. | New on 2026-09-27. | Upgrading a host set up the old way (`coder_service::adopt`) is silent and verified, and keeps its identity and state: it copies the host key, and the owner key only when it is the access store's owner, into the keychain (`com.openagents.desktop`, or the Linux file keys of the row above), deletes a key file only after the keychain returns the same key and only under the access store's lock with the old agent reported stopped, never overwrites a keychain item that holds a different key, never writes the grants, epochs, serve settings (tailnet admission included), auto-start policy, or tasks, and never prints a secret. It also stops and removes every other user agent or unit that runs `coder host serve` or the old `coder-service ... run` launcher (never the app's own `com.openagents.desktop.host`, never `coder earn serve` or another program), and renames the old agent's `service.json` to `service.adopted.json` so the NixOS installer and updater (`os/bin/coder-host-install`, `os/bin/coder-update`) step aside; they also step aside whenever the app's own `com.openagents.desktop.host.service` is registered, and the NixOS module's installer unit carries both as `ConditionPathExists=!` so systemd skips it and no rebuild installs a second host. After adoption the owner key is in the keychain, not the file. Any failed check leaves the old setup running exactly as it was. Windows adoption moves key files only; it never had an agent. Tests never reach the real home: `Paths::under` and `coder host`'s home refuse it under `cfg(test)`. | New on 2026-09-29 ([#9973](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/9973)); relaxes the row above for adopted computers. | A host's chat threads reach a device only through NIP-HOST `thread.list` and `thread.read`, which the access layer admits only with `observe`, and `thread.send`, `thread.stop`, and `thread.run`, only with `operate`, on any link (iroh, the relay, CAP/CJ), under the same grant, epoch, and freshness checks as every other operation. They run the same chat service commands on the same store (`<host root>/basic-chats`) as the local operator socket, so a device never reaches the socket and a thread the desktop app or `openagents chat` started is the one the device reads. A thread answer carries titles, turns, the streaming reply, and a link to Coder work, and encodes in at most 48 KiB. Each turn may carry the router's offers, cards, and follow-up chips; the typed judgment (tier, answer, route, bank, and judgment text) stays on the host. A page over 48 KiB drops the oldest turns' extras before it drops turns. An older page with no extras means there are none. A send appends at most one message per send ID; other text under a held ID, a thread that is answering, and an archived thread refuse as `conflict`. A stop runs the chat service's own stop only while the thread is answering the message whose send ID it names, so it is idempotent per thread and send ID and a stale one never stops a later reply; the partial is kept as a reply marked stopped, and the words stay "Stopped receiving this reply. The hosted worker may still finish." A phone shows a stop control on a host's thread only after the host answered a no-op `thread.stop` (a fresh send ID) with `dispatched`: an older host, or a device without `operate`, gets no stop control, never a dead one. A tap on that stop, **Open Coder**, or **Stop Coder too** that names a view a newer render replaced still counts when the current view offers the same control, resolved against the current view; every other stale tap is ignored. Stopping a reply never stops a Coder task; that is offered separately, through the task's own stop. A thread's link to a Coder run `openagents chat` started on this computer (host `local`) names the host's own key only when the host's task owner holds that task and its local-run record for that thread, so the device opens, follows, and interrupts it through the normal task paths and grant checks; a run in another task store reaches the device only as `outside` (task, project, time), with no link, and the phone shows words and no Coder control for it. `outside` is omitted when absent. `thread.run` asks the host to start Coder for that thread through the same handoff the desktop uses, and its receipt names the task. It grants no execution authority beyond that handoff. An older host refuses it as `malformed` or `unsupported`. Thread reads are the one exception to reply retention: the host keeps no reply for them, so polling a streaming thread never grows the access store. | New on 2026-09-30 ([#10035](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/10035)); `thread.stop` on 2026-09-30 ([#10039](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/10039)); offers, cards, follow-ups, and `thread.run` on 2026-09-30 ([#10040](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/10040)); local runs through the host on 2026-09-30 ([#10043](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/10043)). | `a_paired_phone_reads_a_host_thread_and_continues_it`, `thread_reads_are_not_retained_so_polling_never_fills_the_store`, `a_phone_stops_a_host_threads_streaming_reply_and_the_owner_reads_it_stopped` in `crates/coder-host/tests/threads.rs`;
