`a_local_run_outside_the_host_is_named_only_when_present` in `crates/coder-access/src/thread.rs`; `a_local_run_outside_the_computers_host_is_said_plainly_with_no_dead_controls` in `crates/openagents-mobile/src/coder_tab_tests.rs` |
| Threads `openagents chat` kept without a host (`<chat home>/threads`, under the chat home's own device key) move into this computer's host store (`<host root>/basic-chats`, re-encrypted under the host's key) only when that host starts as this user's own host (root `~/.openagents/host`, or `OPENAGENTS_CHAT_HOME` set) or when `openagents chat` finds a running host and sends the same-user control operation `chat_migrate`. A move keeps every thread's ID, title, times, turns with their router metadata and send IDs, lane, archived and pinned state, and Coder link, so the thread's ATIF export is unchanged. It never loses a thread: each thread becomes a durable record in the host's store before the chat home gets its marker (`threads-migrated.json`), and only then is the old store renamed `threads-moved-<time>`, never deleted; a thread the host already holds is left as it is; a record that can't be read stops the move before the marker. A second move is a no-op. A scratch store, a relative path, or a home another user owns (or a link) is never moved. | New on 2026-09-30 ([#10037](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/10037)). Model boundary: a `--local` process writing to the old store while the host renames it may leave that one write in the renamed store, still readable with the chat home's key. | `threads_move_whole_and_their_export_is_unchanged`, `a_second_move_is_a_no_op`, `a_move_stopped_halfway_resumes_and_loses_nothing`, `a_thread_the_host_already_changed_is_left_as_it_is`, `scratch_stores_and_unreadable_records_are_never_moved` in `crates/openagents-chat/src/migrate.rs`; `threads_kept_without_a_host_join_it_at_start_and_when_the_command_asks` in `crates/coder-host/tests/control.rs` |
| A phone keeps each paired computer's thread list and each thread's settled turns as last read in the app's device-key encrypted store (`<state>/host-threads`: the lists, at most 32 threads of at most 160 KiB each, least recently read dropped first, and the follow-up outbox of at most 64), and shows them at once after a relaunch, marked with when they were read; a read the computer answers always replaces the kept copy, and a kept copy never grants anything, since every send, stop, and run still goes through the computer under the device's grant. A follow-up is written to the outbox with its send ID, minted once when it was typed, before any attempt; it leaves the outbox only when the computer accepts or refuses it, and an unreached attempt keeps it for the next one (the open thread, or the computer's next answered `thread.list`), so across crashes and relaunches the host appends it exactly once. One reader sends a given send ID at a time. Existing stores are untouched: the cache is a new directory with its own keys. | New on 2026-09-30 ([#10041](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/10041)). | `a_relaunch_with_the_computer_off_lists_and_opens_kept_threads`, `a_follow_up_queued_offline_goes_once_when_the_computer_is_back`, `a_follow_up_the_computer_took_before_a_crash_is_not_appended_twice` in `crates/openagents-chat-app/src/host_threads.rs`; `a_computers_threads_survive_a_relaunch_with_it_off_and_a_queued_follow_up_goes_once` in `crates/openagents-mobile/src/coder_tab_tests.rs` |
| A project picked on the local control socket that holds its own Git directory is admitted as a detached worktree of its current commit under the host root's `projects/`, made once and reused; Coder never writes in the person's own checkout, and the folder changes only by Git's record of the worktree. | New on 2026-09-29 ([#9974](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/9974)). | `a_project_change_is_recorded_and_asks_the_host_to_start_again` in `crates/coder-host/tests/control.rs` |
| A settings change on the local control socket (a project added or removed, the auto-start switch) never loses the host or leaves the settings half changed. The host starts again to serve changed projects by re-executing the path it was started from, even after a rebuild replaced the file there (Linux names the running file `PATH (deleted)`), else `argv[0]` resolved, trying for a few seconds; if it still cannot, it exits with a failure, and the Linux user unit (`RestartSec=1`) or the Mac login agent (`ThrottleInterval` 1) starts it again within a second or two. The Linux unit never runs `coder` out of a directory a build overwrites: outside an installed location (`/usr`, `/opt`, the Nix store, an AppImage) it runs a copy of `coder` and `microcoder` in `~/.openagents/host/bin/<sha256>/` (folder `0700`, programs `0500`), named by their contents, so a new build is picked up the next time the app starts and never replaced under a running host; the unit's arguments and key source are unchanged. The desktop waits up to 30 seconds for the host to answer again after each change, shows the chosen folder with **Saving…** at once, and says a setting did not change only when the host did not answer in that time or refused. Choosing another folder is idempotent and resumable: after a failure partway, the next answer from the host finishes it (one project shown, the auto-start policy naming the label the host gave the new one with the switch carried over, no label the host no longer has), and every policy change runs `coder host autostart on --keep-engine` or `off`, keeping the owner's engine. | New on 2026-09-29 ([#10001](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/10001)): on a Linux computer whose unit ran `target/release/coder`, a rebuild made the host's start-again fail with ENOENT, systemd brought it back 10 seconds later, and the window's 5-second retry left two projects and a policy naming the removed one. | `start_again_finds_the_program_a_rebuild_replaced` in `crates/coder-host/src/cli.rs`; `a_replaced_program_still_starts_again_after_a_project_change` in `crates/coder/tests/host_cli.rs`; `a_build_directorys_coder_runs_from_a_copy_named_by_its_contents`, `linux_unit_quotes_every_word`, `linux_login_agent_registers_and_unregisters` in `crates/openagents-desktop/src/platform/linux.rs`; `a_host_that_starts_again_after_each_change_still_swaps_the_project`, `a_swap_that_fails_partway_finishes_on_the_next_refresh`, `a_swap_is_given_up_after_its_tries` in `crates/openagents-desktop/src/model.rs`; `a_swap_whose_answer_was_lost_finishes_and_runs_again_harmlessly` in `crates/openagents-desktop/src/control.rs`; `auto_start_changes_run_the_hosts_own_command` in `crates/coder-host/tests/control.rs` |
| Nearby pairing admits a device only after the person clicks **Connect** on the computer while both screens show the same six-digit code; one pending request, five per ten minutes. The code is SHA-256 over both `EndpointId`s, both Nostr keys, and both nonces, the device's committed before it sees the host's; only the click signs a grant (origin `approval`, issuer the host) for the device key the exchange named, with the pairing grant (every right, as a QR code carries); **Don't connect**, a mismatch, a timeout, a busy host, or the rate limit gets no answer. The mDNS record (`_openagents._udp`) carries only the `EndpointId`, IP addresses, and a display label, and discovery never admits. | New on 2026-09-29 ([#9975](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/9975)). | `no_grant_without_the_click`, `a_click_needs_the_shown_request`, `one_pending_request_and_five_per_ten_minutes`, and `the_rate_limit_holds_over_the_wire` in `crates/coder-host/src/serve/nearby_tests.rs`; `the_click_on_the_computer_grants_the_nearby_phone`, `dont_connect_grants_nothing`, and `the_phone_keeps_only_the_nearby_computers_approval` in `crates/coder-host/tests/nearby.rs`; `a_tapped_computer_shows_the_code_to_compare_then_connects` in `crates/openagents-mobile`; `a_nearby_phone_is_connected_only_by_the_click_on_its_prompt` in `crates/openagents-desktop`; `a_click_signs_an_approval_grant_the_device_accepts` and `only_the_pairing_rights_and_only_a_device_key` in `crates/coder-access/src/host/nearby.rs`; `a_machine_in_the_middle_with_its_own_keys_gets_different_codes`, `every_key_and_nonce_changes_the_code`, `a_reveal_that_does_not_open_the_commitment_is_refused`, and `the_advertisement_carries_only_addresses_and_the_label` in `crates/openagents-connect/src/nearby_tests.rs` |

## Basic chat

The owner decided on 2026-09-28 that the OpenAgents app is conversation
first ([#9914](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/9914)): a
new user chats with the basic Coder, a hosted model behind the OpenAgents
chat worker, before connecting any computer. The serving path and its limits
are in `docs/deployment/chat-worker.md`.

| Invariant | Status | Checked by |
| --- | --- | --- |
| No model API key ships in the OpenAgents app. The basic chat reaches a model only as a NIP-CJ conversation job signed by the device key and encrypted to the chat worker, whose door key stays in the worker's environment on its host; the job carries the conversation, instructions, a client name (a closed word, `router::ClientWord`: `openagents-mobile`, `openagents-desktop`, `openagents-web`, and on the terminal surface `openagents-cli` for `openagents chat` or `openagents-terminal` for OpenAgents Terminal, the same through a host, #10108), the `opener` request for the worker's first response, the `router` request (`chat-router-v2`), a bounded `context` (`surface`, `computer_ready`, `app_build`, since #10077 `computer` and `project`, and since #10094 `coder_run`), and, in a chat with an open test-set draft, that `draft` (only when NIP-CJ's `parse_draft` reads it, at most 64 KiB) and its last try's `tried`, and `skip`, at most 32 public `3189` result IDs a check must not be offered (the trainer's own results and the ones it already checked, because the worker holds no trainer key; the worker reads it with `router::card::skip` exactly or drops it), and no credential, model choice, grant, key, host address, or amount, except the person's own provider keys in a sealed `payer.keys` envelope when they chose BYOK `mine` (the row below). `computer` names where Coder runs, this device (`here`, from the desktop app or `openagents chat`, with its coding agents' readiness as `ready`, `not_signed_in`, or `limited`) or the phone's paired computer (`paired`), by the label the person gave it (at most 64 characters); `project` is the chat's project folder by name (at most 128 bytes) and, only beside `here`, its absolute path (at most 1024 bytes); a value past its bound or with a control character is left out, and the worker reads a path only beside `here`. These are the person's own data: the worker gives them only to the chat model's instructions and to its own reviewed answers' `chat.*` slots, never to a seam (personalization, embeddings, or Jev). `coder_run` is the chat's Coder run once its turn ended (never while it runs or waits for an answer): `ending` (`finished`, `failed`, `stopped`), `turn`, `engine`, `model`, its `summary` (at most 4 KiB), at most 32 changed `files`, and its last 16 `commands` (each one line of at most 200 bytes); the worker gives the summary, files, and commands only to the chat model's instructions, and Jev reads only how the run ended, as a fixed line (`router::CoderRun::marker`). The phone's kept relay connection (one NIP-42 connection while the Coder tab shows) carries only routing: each job still checks every answer as below. | New on 2026-09-28. Reinterpreted on 2026-09-28 ([#9920](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/9920)): jobs ask for `opener`, and share one kept connection instead of one each. Extended on 2026-09-28 ([#9921](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/9921)): jobs ask for `router` with the bounded `context`. Extended on 2026-09-29 ([#9939](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/9939)): `chat-router-v2`, and the chat's `draft` and `tried`. Extended on 2026-09-29 ([#9941](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/9941)): `skip`, after a new trainer was offered their own result to check. Extended on 2026-09-30 ([#10077](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/10077)): `computer` and `project`, so a chat on a computer knows it is on one; the context now names the paired computer and the project folder, which it used to leave out. Extended on 2026-10-01 ([#10094](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/10094)): `coder_run`, so a follow-up after a run is the router's, with what the run did. Extended on 2026-10-01 ([#10108](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/10108)): the `openagents-terminal` client word, and a terminal's turn through the host keeps the terminal surface instead of the desktop's. | `a_coder_run_is_bounded_and_typed` (`router`), `the_model_is_told_what_the_finished_run_did` (`coder-worker`), `a_coder_run_context_matches_the_worker_fixture` (`openagents-chat`), `the_computer_context_is_bounded_and_typed` (`router`), `the_model_is_told_where_the_chat_runs` (`coder-worker`), `a_checks_add_to_the_gym_speaks_of_the_check`, `adding_the_first_result_names_the_menu`, `a_skip_is_exact_hex_ids_or_nothing` (`crates/coder`), `a_basic_job_carries_no_credential`, `a_basic_job_asks_for_the_first_response`, `the_context_names_no_host_and_bounds_the_build`, `a_turn_asks_for_routing_with_a_bounded_context`, `the_draft_round_trips_through_the_phone_and_each_step_is_a_tap`, `messages_reuse_the_kept_connection`, `the_payload_keeps_the_newest_turns_within_its_bound` in `crates/openagents-mobile`; `a_computer_context_matches_the_worker_fixture`, `a_phone_names_its_computer_and_never_a_path` in `crates/openagents-chat`; `a_paired_phone_reads_a_host_thread_and_continues_it` in `crates/coder-host/tests/threads.rs`; `settings_change_the_local_run_and_the_defaults_change_nothing` in `crates/openagents-cli/tests/chat.rs`; `a_send_streams_typed_events_and_says_the_terminal_sent_it` (`client`) in `crates/openagents-chat`; `a_terminal_turn_through_the_host_says_terminal` in `crates/coder-host/tests/threads.rs` |
| The openagents.com homepage terminal reaches OpenAgents only as the `web` surface: `POST /ask` sends a NIP-CJ conversation job to the chat worker through the relay, with the website's instructions (`basic_coder::INSTRUCTIONS_WEB`), `context.surface: web`, `computer_ready: false`, and no computer, project, Coder run, draft, credential, or key. It is signed with a key the server derives from the visitor's random `oa_visitor` cookie and a server secret, never sent to the browser, so the worker's usage log records each visitor apart. On that surface the worker's policy (`router::policy::for_web`) serves only refusals, knowledge, the model, and prepared answers on the answer routes, each without an offer or card, and turns work, command, screen, wallet, account, Gym, deck, and capability routes into the model told `WEB_NOTE` (the OpenAgents app does that; openagents.com/download); grounding never falls back to a Coder offer, and the command gate withholds every command. The site answers each visitor's questions one at a time (a second question while the first is answering is refused as `We're still answering your last question.`, never as a limit) and has no per-minute or waiting cap (#10120). The page draws only server-rendered Markdown (raw HTML as text). | New on 2026-10-01 ([#10106](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/10106)), owner-directed: the old site's terminal returns as product orientation only. Changed on 2026-10-01 ([#10120](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/10120)), owner decision: the per-minute and waiting caps are removed. Model boundary: a worker release older than this one reads `web` as no surface and answers as a phone with no computer ("Connect one…"). | `the_website_answers_and_never_offers` (`router::policy`) in `crates/coder`; `a_question_streams_its_answer_as_the_website_and_names_the_visitor`, `a_question_must_end_with_the_visitor`, `every_public_page_answers_in_development` in `crates/openagents-web` (`tests`); `a_visitor_asks_one_question_at_a_time_and_as_many_as_they_like`, `each_visitor_signs_with_a_key_of_its_own_that_the_salt_hides` (`ask`) |
| A chat on a computer knows it is on one ([#10077](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/10077)). When the turn's `context.computer` is `here`, the chat never tells the person to connect a computer unless they ask about adding another one: the model is told where the chat runs, the project folder, and that Coder runs here (`router::Context::note`); every prepared answer whose words assume no computer is `place = "away"` and its `.here` variant answers instead (`Bank::placed`, eligibility by place), so `dispatch.no_computer.here` says no coding agent can take the work here, with no offer; and a product answer tagged `off-computer` is never served whole. A question about the working directory or the project is answered from the context's project folder. A phone with a paired computer is told its name the same way; a phone with none still gets **Connect a computer**. The routes and their rubric are unchanged, so no routing reads the context's text. | New on 2026-09-30 ([#10077](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/10077)). | `here_variants_show_only_on_a_computer` (`router::bank`), `an_off_computer_answer_is_never_whole_on_a_computer` (`router`), `the_set_asks_independent_typed_questions_and_validates` (`router::judge`), `on_a_computer_the_working_directory_is_the_project_folder`, `on_a_computer_dispatch_never_asks_to_connect_one`, `the_model_is_told_where_the_chat_runs`, `with_no_computer_the_offer_is_to_connect_one` (`coder-worker`) in `crates/coder` |
| A computer's chat context names every coding agent on it ([#10113](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/10113)): `context.computer.engines` lists the engines the settings' `coder.providers` allow, in that order, each `ready`, `not_signed_in`, or `limited` from the same login probe (`capacity::probe`), capacity book, and usage readings a start reads, then each other engine installed or signed in here as `not_enabled` (`Local::engines`, and the host's chats through `engines_here`). Installation is read from where an agent lives (Codex's home folder, the `claude`, `grok`, `opencode`, or `devin` binary), never from a credential, and no token is read or sent. The wire carries at most 8 (`MAX_ENGINES`, client and worker); a worker leaves out a state it does not know, so an older worker reads the allowed engines and drops the rest. OpenAgents Terminal's welcome card names the ready ones. | New on 2026-10-01 ([#10113](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/10113)), owner-directed: the card said only "Codex ready" on a Mac with Codex, Claude Code, Grok Build, and Devin, because the list came from the run's prediction, which never probes engines after the one that would run and skips an engine the settings leave out. Widens the 2026-09-30 bound of 4 engines. | `every_coding_agent_here_is_listed_with_its_state` (`task::local`), `every_engine_and_its_state_reaches_the_note` (`router`) in `crates/coder`; `every_engine_here_goes_on_the_wire` (`router`) in `crates/openagents-chat`; `the_welcome_card_names_every_ready_agent_here` in `crates/openagents-cli/tests/terminal.rs` |
| A phone's chat names its paired computer's coding agents ([#10119](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/10119)). The host puts the same list its own chats send (`engines_here`, #10113) in its NIP-HOST presence as capabilities `engine-<state>-<engine>` (`coder_access::protocol::engine_flag`), at most 8, each engine once, with a closed state (`ready`, `not_signed_in`, `limited`, `not_enabled`) and the engine's bounded word only: no account, token, or usage figure. It reads them off its runtime at most every 15 seconds and republishes presence when they change. The phone reads them from the computer's newest presence (`coder_tab::engines_of`, leaving out a state it does not know) and sends `context.computer` as `{place: "paired", name, engines}`, omitting `engines` when the computer names none, so a phone paired with an older host sends what it always did; an older worker ignores the field, and an older phone ignores the flags as unknown capabilities. The worker's note names each one and its state, says each Coder run uses one of them and never other agents or tools, and that a question about which agents are connected or who the chat delegates to is answered from that list; with no list, it says the chat was not told rather than guess. | The chat worker never trusts a quiet socket. It renews the subscription every 45 minutes, before the relay's Cloud Run request timeout, on a new connection that is subscribed before the old one is closed, and it reads the old one for 5 seconds after, so a job published during the renewal reaches it and one delivered on both is answered once. | A seam sees only what its input type names. A personalization call carries the route, the answer id, the stem, and the latest message redacted of bounded secret shapes and cut to 600 characters; a retrieval or CLI call carries that message and the bounded transcript. A continuation is shown only after it passes validation (one sentence, at most 160 characters, no URL, no first-person singular, no claim of completion, no number the user did not type, no markup), appended after the stem, which is never retracted; otherwise the stem's reviewed generic end closes it. A possible secret turns personalization and offers off for the turn. | New on 2026-09-28 ([#9922](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/9922)). | `redaction_replaces_bounded_secret_shapes_and_bounds_length`, `continuations_are_validated_before_they_are_shown`, `the_default_seams_are_all_unavailable` in `crates/coder` (`router`); `a_routed_work_request_is_offered_to_coder_and_the_model_is_dropped`, `an_invalid_continuation_falls_back_to_the_generic_end` (`coder-worker`) |
| The T1 personalization seam (`coder::router::personalize`) sends its provider fixed instructions and only the router's `Ask`: the route id, the stem, and the latest message the router already redacted, cut again to 600 characters. It answers only a continuation that passes its own checks (one line, not a question, no "we", no button, no time, price, or guarantee the user did not write, not cut off by the token bound) and then `router::validate_continuation`, which the router applies again; an error it reports names a class of failure, never message or model text. The worker's seam is `NoPersonalize` unless `CODER_PERSONALIZE` names a provider, and the seam's `recipients()` names that provider. | New on 2026-09-28 ([#9927](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/9927)). Wired and deployed on 2026-09-28 ([#9920](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/9920)): the chat worker runs `CODER_PERSONALIZE=openrouter`, and the privacy answer names OpenRouter as a recipient. | `the_prompt_carries_only_the_route_the_stem_and_the_message`, `a_good_continuation_is_tidied_and_joined_by_one_space`, `every_rule_refuses_its_own_failure`, `the_seam_answers_a_checked_continuation_and_names_its_recipient`, `a_refused_or_failed_continuation_is_an_error_without_its_text`, `an_openrouter_failure_is_named_by_its_class_only`, `the_provider_variable_is_off_unless_it_names_one` in `crates/coder` (`router::personalize`) |
| The chat worker keeps no message text. It writes no message text to disk (its usage log holds caller keys, ids, counts, and times; an emergency brake's quota file holds caller keys and counts), and its per-turn router log line (a `router` record, in shadow mode or not) holds only ids from the bank, the route catalog, the CLI group list, and the tool catalog, probabilities, tier words, and the judge's time. The privacy answer names every service a message reaches, built from the worker's configuration: the model door, TypeSafe for Jev, and each configured seam's recipients, each named once, with Jev's fallback doors (the Vercel AI Gateway, OpenRouter) named beside TypeSafe when their keys are set. On the deployed worker those are, since #10109, OpenRouter for Space Bunny Alpha (an anonymous preview model, the primary every turn asks first; since #11040 every chat-model and personalization request asks its provider not to keep or train on it, which the answer says) and the Vercel AI Gateway for Gemini 3.8 Flash (and Gemini 2.5 Flash for Gym news replies, through the same gateway and key, since #9950) when the primary does not answer, OpenRouter (personalization), the Vercel AI Gateway (OpenAI embeddings, for the product and codebase knowledge bases and the Gym's records, which reuse the product knowledge base's embedder and so add no recipient), and TypeSafe; the CLI route fills free text through the same door and names no other service. Since #10077 the privacy answer (`meta.privacy@2`) also says that on a computer, or a phone paired with one, each message names that computer and the chat's project folder, which only the worker and the chat model read. Since #10094 it (`meta.privacy@3`) also says that after Coder runs in a chat, the next messages carry what it reported, the files it changed, and its commands, which only the worker and the chat model read. For a job on the caller's own keys (BYOK, #10176), the model answer names the door as theirs ("through OpenRouter on your own key") and the privacy answer names the same services, every one reached on the caller's keys and under their own accounts, with Jev through their providers that serve it, and no TypeSafe of ours. | New on 2026-09-28 ([#9922](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/9922)). `meta.privacy` cites this row. Extended on 2026-10-01 ([#10109](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/10109)): the primary and its provider's retention are named. | `the_model_answer_names_the_model_answering_now`, `a_payer_job_grounds_on_their_keys_and_says_so` (`coder-worker`), `the_shadow_record_never_carries_message_text`, `the_wire_matches_the_nip_cj_fixtures` in `crates/coder` (`router::wire`); `unfilled_slots_make_an_entry_ineligible` (`router::bank`) |
| The chat worker's model door asks its primary first and falls back per turn (`coder::generate::FallbackDoor`): every turn goes to the primary (`CODER_WORKER_PRIMARY`, unset is Space Bunny Alpha on OpenRouter at `reasoning.effort` `low` whenever `OPENROUTER_API_KEY` is set; `off` is none), and a primary that fails before the first words of its answer reached the caller (an HTTP error, an unknown model, a 429, a failure event, an empty stream, nothing at all within `PRIMARY_FIRST_WORD`, four seconds, or, for a primary streaming its reasoning, no answer text by `PRIMARY_THINKING`, eight seconds from the request) hands the same turn to the gateway door `CODER_WORKER_MODEL` names, which runs with its own retries and bounds; one that fails after its first words fails the turn rather than have a second model repeat it. The worker logs each fallback with the doors, the cause, and the wait, never message text. Every model reply's result names the model that wrote it, and the bank's model answers (`meta.model`, `meta.team`) name the model answering now: the fallback's while the primary's last turn failed before its first words. The model answer, grounded answers, Gym news, and the authoring interview all ask the primary first. | New on 2026-10-01 ([#10109](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/10109)). | `an_error_status_before_the_first_word_falls_back`, `a_primary_silent_past_the_first_word_wait_falls_back`, `a_primary_that_fails_after_its_first_words_is_not_redone`, `a_primary_that_answers_names_itself`, `a_primary_that_is_thinking_is_waited_for`, `a_primary_that_thinks_past_the_bound_falls_back` (`crates/coder/tests/gateway_stream.rs`); `the_primary_is_space_bunny_whenever_openrouter_is_reachable` (`generate`); `the_result_names_the_door_that_answered`, `the_primary_goes_in_front_of_a_gateway_door`, `the_model_answer_names_the_model_answering_now` (`coder-worker`) |
