## OpenAgents Terminal

OpenAgents Terminal (`crates/openagents-terminal`, `openagents terminal`,
[#10111](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/10111)) is a
screen over the shared chat client. Its user guide is `docs/terminal/README.md`.

| Invariant | Status | Checked by |
| --- | --- | --- |
| The screen adds no chat or Coder logic and reads no words. Every message goes to the chat router through `openagents_chat::client` as `Caller::TERMINAL`; the only parse of what the person types is a draft that is exactly `/word` against the closed `slash::Slash` list (new, threads, stop, export, settings, connect, plugins, help, quit), and any other text, including text that only starts with a slash, is a message. An answer to Coder's question is chosen by the run's typed state (a `question` event waiting), never by the words; a follow-up is picked by its key. | New on 2026-10-01 ([#10111](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/10111)). | `only_an_exact_word_from_the_list_is_a_command`, `everything_else_goes_to_the_chat_unread` (`slash`), `slash_commands_are_a_closed_list_and_other_text_is_a_message`, `a_question_from_coder_is_answered_from_the_composer` (`app`) in `crates/openagents-terminal` |
| A Coder run from the screen has no budget and one stop. Progress shows Jev's estimate as `step N · ≈X% done` and never a step or time limit ("of N"); every step is approved (#10104) and the screen draws no approval prompt. Esc (or `/stop`) while a run streams asks its task to stop (`client::Stopper`), and the run's own `stopped` event ends it. Quitting, opening the thread list, or sending while a run streams only stops following: the run is its own process and keeps going, and following it again (reopening the thread) replays from its first event without showing any event twice. | New on 2026-10-01 ([#10111](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/10111)). | `esc_stops_the_reply_then_the_run_and_never_quits`, `progress_is_one_live_row_and_a_replayed_event_shows_once` (`app`), `progress_is_an_estimate_never_a_budget` (`rows`), `the_screen_end_to_end` (`tests/pty.rs`) in `crates/openagents-terminal`; the progress tests in `crates/coder-terminal/tests/components.rs` |
| The screen draws Coder Noir’s neutral intensity ladder on near-black: `coder_ui::theme` through `coder_terminal::Ladder`, with the nearest palette entries on a 256-color terminal and no color at all under `NO_COLOR`; the cursor uses Coder Noir’s neutral content color (OSC 12 `#ededed`) and handed back (OSC 112) on every exit, a panic included. | New on 2026-10-01 ([#10111](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/10111)): the cursor was still amber. | `the_screen_end_to_end` (`tests/pty.rs`, which reads the bytes) in `crates/openagents-terminal`; the colorless and 256-color checks in `crates/coder-terminal/tests/components.rs` |
| `/connect` gets its code only from this computer's host over the control socket (the host stays the only issuer of access), shows it only as a text QR code, and cancels it once a phone connects, when it expires, on Esc, and when the screen closes. The Ctrl+S sync offer installs the host service only when the person presses it, never on its own. | New on 2026-10-01 ([#10111](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/10111)). | `text_qr_rows_fill_light_modules_without_escapes` in `crates/coder-connect`; `sync_leaves_a_running_host_alone`, `sync_installs_when_everything_is_here`, `sync_names_what_is_missing` (`screen`) in `crates/openagents-cli` |
| What the screen keeps of its own is `terminal.json` in the chat home (each folder's path and the last thread ID opened there, at most 256 folders, no message text) and the files `/export` writes on request. | New on 2026-10-01 ([#10111](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/10111)). | `each_folder_keeps_its_own_last_thread`, `the_oldest_folders_are_forgotten_first` (`last`) in `crates/openagents-terminal` |
| A Coder run's tool calls group the same way on every surface (OpenAgents Terminal, the desktop and phone Coder run view, `openagents chat` text), by the one module `openagents_chat::tool_groups`, from each step's typed `call` (`verb`, `target`) and each command's typed `output`, never from a step's words: consecutive calls that only look (read, search, list, fetch) fold into one row labelled by verb ("Read 3 files, Searched 2 patterns") with the thoughts between them, a call that acts (run, edit, delete, move, other) is one row, and a stretch's standalone rows past ten fold under one label. Condensed is the default: command output and file contents show only when the person expands (Ctrl+O or `/expand` in the terminal, a click or tap on the row in the apps). | New on 2026-10-01 ([#10117](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/10117)), owner-directed to match Grok Build ([design](docs/coder/design/2026-10-01-tool-call-groups.md)). Model boundary: a line written before `call` existed groups as one standalone call. | `crates/openagents-chat` `tool_groups` unit tests and `tests/tool_groups.rs` over real Grok Build, Codex, and Claude Code runs; `an_agents_tool_calls_are_typed`; `tool_calls_are_grouped_condensed_and_expand_with_ctrl_o` and `a_long_run_of_commands_folds_its_oldest` in `crates/openagents-terminal`; the pty `the_screen_end_to_end`; `tool_calls_show_grouped_and_open_to_each_call` in `crates/openagents-chat-app`; `tool_calls_show_grouped_and_a_click_opens_a_group` in `crates/openagents-desktop` |

## OpenAgents Terminal releases

`scripts/release/terminal.sh` publishes the `openagents` program and its
`microcoder` engine to `gs://openagentsgemini-cli-releases/openagents/`, and
`scripts/install/openagents.sh` and `.ps1` install them
([#10114](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/10114)). The
runbook is `docs/release/terminal.md`.

| Invariant | Status | Checked by |
| --- | --- | --- |
| A channel pointer (`openagents.<channel>`) moves only to a version for which the bucket holds both artifacts (`openagents-` and `microcoder-<version>-<platform>`) and the version's sums file names both, on all seven platforms. A partial publish leaves the channel where it was. | New on 2026-10-01 ([#10114](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/10114)). | `uncovered_platforms` in `scripts/release/terminal.sh`, tested by `scripts/test-release-terminal.sh` (a missing engine, a missing sums entry, and a longer version's artifacts are each a gap) |
| A published object is never replaced: the script refuses to build a version whose sums file or any artifact is in the bucket, and uploads with `--no-clobber`. It writes nothing outside the `openagents/` prefix. | New on 2026-10-01 ([#10114](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/10114)). | The pre-build check and `--no-clobber` uploads in `scripts/release/terminal.sh`; read back on the 1.0.0-rc.1 cut |
| The installers install nothing whose SHA-256 differs from the version's sums file, and verify both programs before replacing either. | New on 2026-10-01 ([#10114](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/10114)). | `a checksum mismatch installs nothing and keeps the installed version` in `scripts/test-release-terminal.sh` |
| A macOS artifact is published only after Gatekeeper (`spctl --assess -t install`) answers `accepted` from `Notarized Developer ID`; `--skip-notarization` is refused with `--publish`. | New on 2026-10-01 ([#10114](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/10114)). | `wait_for_gatekeeper` in `scripts/release/terminal.sh`; the verdicts in each published manifest |

## Hosted decision service

Coder on a computer with no TypeSafe key asks Jev through the hosted
decision worker (`decision-worker`, `crates/gateway`), deployed as described
in `docs/deployment/decision-worker.md`.

| Invariant | Status | Checked by |
| --- | --- | --- |
| No TypeSafe key ships to or is asked of a user's computer. A keyless Jev call is a NIP-CJ decision job (kind `25910`) signed by the computer's decision key and encrypted to the worker whose public key is compiled into clients (`jev_hosted::WORKER`); the worker's door key is read once from its environment file on its host and appears in no payload, log line, ledger, or receipt. A `jev::Client` that carries its calls through an exchange refuses to build with a key. | New on 2026-09-30 ([#10044](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/10044)). | `no_key_means_the_hosted_service_under_a_key_made_once` in `crates/jev-hosted`; `open_lane_answers_unprovisioned_keys_under_quota` in `crates/gateway/tests/relay_worker.rs` |
| The worker's open lane has no usage limit (owner decision, 2026-10-01, [#10121](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/10121), following [#10120](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/10120): "I never want to see a limit again"). It answers any caller key with no per-minute, per-day, or total count; the only bounds are the request size (`max_request_bytes`, 256 KiB of ciphertext, refused `limit_exceeded` before any door) and the jobs at once (`busy`). It forwards only models the lane names (`not_admitted`). The lane's `quota` counts (`per_key_minute`, `per_key_day`, `total_day`) are an emergency abuse brake only: each is unlimited unless named, the deployed `decision-worker.json` names none, and the startup log says `no usage limit`; when an operator sets one, a count that is set must be positive (a typo stops the worker), a job counts when admitted, a refusal (`rate_limited`, `quota_exhausted`, with `retry_after_ms`) counts nothing, reaches no door, and names no count ("This worker can't take this job right now. Try again later."), and the day's counts survive a restart. A client reads a brake's refusal as "Jev refused: busy …". | New on 2026-09-30 ([#10044](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/10044)) as a metered lane (2,000 per key and 20,000 in all per UTC day). Changed on 2026-10-01 ([#10121](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/10121)): no count by default, an off-by-default brake, neutral refusal text. | `with_no_brake_the_open_lane_admits_every_job`, `a_key_is_rate_limited_then_capped_and_the_total_caps_everyone`, `a_restart_keeps_the_day_and_a_broken_file_stops_it`, `loose_limits_stop_the_worker` in `crates/gateway/src/open_quota.rs`; `with_no_brake_the_open_lane_answers_every_job_and_records_it`, `open_lane_answers_unprovisioned_keys_under_quota`, `the_deployed_config_names_the_fallback_doors_in_order` in `crates/gateway/tests/relay_worker.rs`; `a_refusal_reads_as_jev_refused_with_its_http_status` in `crates/jev-hosted` |
| The decision worker records every decision job it reads as one JSON line in its usage log (`usage/YYYY-MM-DD.jsonl` under `jobs_dir`, the UTC day it arrived), answered or refused: time, caller key, lane (`open`, `principal`, `anonymous`), request id and attempt, the model asked and the model that answered, the door that answered, the doors' and the whole job's time, tokens and cost when the door reported them, outcome and code, and the request's size. A line holds no state, question, or key. `decision-worker usage [--since DATE] [--by key\|lane\|model\|door\|day\|outcome] [--json]` reads it. A retransmission of a settled pair is not a new line. | New on 2026-10-01 ([#10121](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/10121)), owner decision: record everything so usage stats are easy to pull. | `the_log_rotates_by_day_and_the_stats_read_it_back` in `crates/gateway/src/decision_usage.rs`; `open_lane_answers_unprovisioned_keys_under_quota`, `with_no_brake_the_open_lane_answers_every_job_and_records_it` in `crates/gateway/tests/relay_worker.rs` |
| Every answer the worker relays carries `service` (`{door, version}`), and a client accepts only an answer `decision::bind_answer` binds: signed by the worker, `e`-tagging this request event, `p`-tagging this key, with a receipt bound to this request's digest. | New on 2026-09-30 ([#10044](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/10044)). | `a_computer_with_no_key_is_answered_by_the_hosted_worker_until_its_quota_ends` |
| A decision request's `state` is a string or a JSON object, and every question entry (instructions, choice option descriptions, score levels, noul `true`/`false`) is an EntryType, bounded as [NIP-DEC](nips/openagents/NIP-DEC.md#bounds) says (32 KiB per question, 128 KiB and 32 levels of state, 8 levels per entry); a request outside those bounds is refused `invalid_request` before any door is called, and an admitted one reaches the door with `state` and `questions` unchanged. String-only requests, including the legacy string noul `criteria`, stay valid. A model alias (`typesafe/jev-1.13`) is admitted only where its canonical name is and goes to the door as that name. | New on 2026-09-30 ([#10047](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/10047)). | `every_structured_example_round_trips_the_wire`, `every_invalid_example_is_refused_invalid_request`, `entry_depth_is_bounded_at_exactly_the_limit`, `a_too_deep_state_is_refused`, `oversize_entries_and_state_are_refused` in `crates/nostr` (`decision`); `structured_entries_pass_through_and_model_aliases_admit` in `crates/gateway/tests/relay_worker.rs` |
| Every caller builds a decision with one shared model that is exactly NIP-DEC (`jev::DecisionRequest`: `State` text or object, `Questions` of Noul, Choice, and Score with EntryType fields and optional `NoulCriteria`; answers as `jev::Answer`; HTTP errors as `jev::Refusal`), and converts it to the TypeSafe and gateway body, OpenRouter's Decisions body, and the NIP-DEC job body from that one value. A question file read into the model keeps its bytes. The SDK's model-alias and status tables equal `nostr::decision`'s. | New on 2026-09-30 ([#10048](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/10048)). | `every_docs_example_round_trips_exactly_and_reads_typed`, `what_the_model_does_not_cover_stays_raw_and_keeps_its_bytes` in `crates/jev` (`nip_dec`); `every_nip_dec_example_becomes_the_same_job`, `the_sdk_and_the_wire_share_one_alias_and_status_table` in `crates/jev-hosted` |
| A client of TypeSafe's door is built only by `jev_hosted::resolve` (its local-key door), `jev_hosted::resolve_with_fallbacks` (the same door as the final backup, with Jev's other doors in front of it for decisions, for the chat worker's judge), or by the decision worker (its upstream): Coder's delegate door and judge, Coder One, Microcoder, the hands seam, the decision profile's keyed and relay doors, the Gym, external-eval graders, and Voyager's live door all resolve a local key first, then the hosted service, then no Jev with the reason; a local key for TypeSafe's door keeps Jev's other doors and the hosted service behind it for decisions (the Jev doors row). | New on 2026-09-30 ([#10048](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/10048)). | `each_profile_kind_builds_its_door` in `crates/coder` (`decision`); `no_key_means_the_hosted_service_under_a_key_made_once` in `crates/jev-hosted` |
| The gateway's `POST /v1/systemone` takes the Decisions API request (`model`, `questions`, `state`), refuses a body outside NIP-DEC's bounds before any door is consulted, asks a model alias's door by its canonical name, and answers its own refusals with NIP-DEC's HTTP status table (400 for request errors, 404 for an unbound model, 502 for a failed or mismatched door, 503 for no capacity); a code only the gateway defines keeps its status. | New on 2026-09-30 ([#10048](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/10048)); `idempotency_conflict` moved from 409 to 400 and `door_not_bound` from 403 to 404 with it. | `the_decisions_api_answers_the_typesafe_docs_examples` and the status assertions in `crates/gateway/tests/serve.rs` |
| Jev's doors are asked in a fixed order. The chat worker's router judge (`jev::doors::Failover` with `primary_last`) asks the Vercel AI Gateway's TypeSafe-compatible API (`typesafe-ai/jev`, the primary, which routes Jev to TypeSafe itself) → OpenRouter's Decisions API (`typesafe/jev-1.13`) → TypeSafe direct last, and sends every route but a decision to TypeSafe; the decision worker's open lane (`open.upstream_last` in `deploy/decision-worker/decision-worker.json`) asks the same order, its `backups` first and its TypeSafe upstream last, while a provisioned principal's jobs ask the upstream first and the `backups` after it. Each backup door is off unless its key variable (`AI_GATEWAY_API_KEY`, `OPENROUTER_API_KEY`) is set when the server starts, and the startup log names each on or off; a door's key goes only to that door and is never logged. A door is asked only after the doors before it timed out, could not be reached, answered 402, 408, 429, or a 5xx, or refused for their own reasons (`jev::doors::fails_over`), never after a refusal of the question itself, and a backup that refuses the question ends the chain. An answer from any door but TypeSafe names it in `service.door` (the chat judgment's `door`, the thread record's `service.upstream`); when no door answers, the first door's refusal stands. The chat judge and the decision worker bench a door that refused for its key or account (401, 402; `jev::doors::benches`) for `jev::doors::BENCH` (five minutes): it is skipped, the bench is logged once, and the door is asked again after it; the decision worker never benches the upstream under a principal's own key. The decision worker's journal names the open lane's door order at start and the door that answered each job. An extension eval's decision door (`ext_eval::run::DecisionPin`: the hosted eval runner and `openagents ext eval run`) asks the same order through its run's door proxy, the backups whose keys are set first and TypeSafe last, for the child's classifier and the `decision` graders alike; on the eval runner the gateway's key is `AI_GATEWAY_API_KEY`, else the chat door's key when the chat door is the Vercel AI Gateway, and its startup log's `decision` line names the doors in order. A computer's own TypeSafe key (`TYPESAFE_API_KEY`, else `~/.openagents/jev.json`) is not its only door: `jev_hosted::resolve`'s local-key client for TypeSafe's door (`jev_hosted::local_doors`, `jev_hosted::with_backups`) asks a decision at that key's TypeSafe door first, as the person's own, then the Vercel AI Gateway and OpenRouter when their keys are set on that computer, then the hosted decision service last, keyless (a carried door, `jev::doors::Door::carried`, the same door a computer with no key uses; off under `OPENAGENTS_JEV_HOSTED=off`), so a key out of credits (402) or refused (401) still gets answers; the refusing door is benched like any other, the hosted door reads or makes the decision key only when first asked, an answer the hosted service carried keeps its `service.door` and adds `service.exchange`, and its decision record says `via` `hosted`. This covers every local caller: Coder's repository judge (Microcoder), the delegate's credentials, the hands seam, the Gym, Voyager, extension-eval graders, and the decision profile's keyed door. A local key for any other door is that door alone, and `jev_hosted::resolve_with_fallbacks` (a server's keys) never falls back to the hosted service. The router's question set is the same whichever door answers. A repository run's judge (`crates/microcoder/src/repository/native.rs`) accepts an answer whose model is the admitted Jev as the answering door (`service.door`) names it (`jev::doors::Naming`: canonical at TypeSafe, `typesafe-ai/jev` at the gateway, `typesafe/jev-1.13` at OpenRouter) and refuses any other, failing the turn ([#10107](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/10107)). | New on 2026-09-30 ([#10048](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/10048)); extended to the gateway door, the order, and the chat judge on 2026-09-30 ([#10064](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/10064)). Reinterpreted on 2026-10-01 ([#10110](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/10110)): the chat judge asks the gateway first and TypeSafe last, at the owner's direction (the gateway handles provider routing), and benches a 401 or 402 door; the decision worker's open lane followed the same day ([#10112](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/10112)). The judge's door-aware check is new on 2026-10-01 ([#10107](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/10107)): before it, a failover answer from the gateway failed every Coder turn in about a second. The extension eval's decision door followed on 2026-10-01 ([#10122](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/10122)): before it, TypeSafe's account running out of credits failed the child's classifier in both arms, so the subject arm never ran its program and scored what the baseline did. The local-key path followed on 2026-10-01 ([#10123](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/10123)): before it, a computer whose `jev.json` key TypeSafe answered 402 had no Jev at all, though the hosted service would have answered. Model boundary: the gateway names no Jev version, so an answer from it is accepted as the admitted Jev on the door's word. | `the_backup_door_answers_only_when_the_upstream_cannot`, `typesafe_then_the_gateway_then_openrouter`, `on_the_open_lane_the_gateway_answers_first_and_typesafe_last`, `the_deployed_config_names_the_fallback_doors_in_order` in `crates/gateway/tests/relay_worker.rs`; `no_credits_at_typesafe_falls_through_the_gateway_then_openrouter`, `a_refusal_of_the_question_never_fails_over`, `when_no_fallback_answers_typesafes_refusal_stands`, `an_unreachable_or_hung_typesafe_falls_through`, `with_typesafe_last_the_gateway_answers_first`, `with_typesafe_last_it_answers_when_the_others_cannot`, `a_door_that_cannot_pay_is_skipped_on_the_next_call`, `a_benched_door_is_asked_again_when_its_bench_ends`, `a_benched_primarys_refusal_stands_when_no_fallback_answers`, `a_key_that_cannot_pay_falls_through_to_a_carried_door_and_is_benched` in `crates/jev/tests/doors.rs`; `a_local_key_with_no_credits_is_answered_by_the_hosted_worker` in `crates/jev-hosted/tests/hosted.rs`; `a_local_key_for_typesafe_falls_back_to_the_other_doors_then_the_hosted_service` in `crates/jev-hosted`; `a_door_never_shows_its_key` (`jev::doors`); `fallback_doors_are_on_only_with_their_keys_and_a_local_key` in `crates/jev-hosted`; `a_fallback_doors_judgment_names_that_door`, `the_privacy_answer_names_jevs_fallback_doors_when_they_are_on` (`coder-worker`); `the_router_record_names_the_jev_door_that_answered` in `crates/openagents-chat`; `a_backup_doors_name_for_the_admitted_jev_is_the_admitted_jev` in `crates/microcoder`; `the_subject_arm_gets_its_program_when_typesafe_is_out_of_credits` in `crates/ext-eval/tests/runner.rs`; `the_gateway_leads_the_decision_doors_and_typesafe_is_last` (`eval-runner` config) |
| Every recorded decision call (`openagents.decision-call.v1`) names its door, `via` (`direct`, `hosted`, or `local`), the relaying `service`, the served model, `latency_ms`, and, when the door reported them, `request_id`, `usage`, and `cost_usd`; its arguments hold the request body, or above 32 KiB the model, the questions when they fit, and the state's digest, size, and a 2 KiB excerpt. | New on 2026-09-30 ([#10048](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/10048)). | `every_decision_record_names_its_door_via_service_and_cost` in `crates/jev-hosted` |
| A decision-model call a Coder run records (`openagents.decision-call.v1` in the call's `extra`, such as Microcoder's `openagents.microcoder.judge.v1` judgment) stays in the task's ATIF trajectory and trace and never shows as a row of the person's transcript on the phone or the desktop: the Coder event mapper drops it and its result, and the history projection reads it as a `decision_call` record that conversation readers hide and never appends it to a message as a `Tool:` line. | New on 2026-09-30 ([#10073](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/10073)): the desktop showed a raw `openagents.microcoder.judge.v1 {…}` row. | `a_decision_call_is_not_a_transcript_row` in `crates/openagents-chat` (`coder_events`); `a_decision_call_reads_as_a_decision_record` in `crates/coder-history`; `a_decision_call_shows_no_row` in `crates/openagents-chat-app` (`conversation`) |
| The decision worker never trusts a quiet socket: a liveness probe (a `REQ` with `limit` 0) every 30 seconds, and one still unanswered at the next ends the connection, which reconnects and subscribes again. | New on 2026-09-30 ([#10044](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/10044)). | `a_silent_relay_is_noticed_by_the_probe` in `crates/gateway/tests/relay_worker.rs` |

## Chat router CLI route

The chat router's CLI route (`crates/coder/src/cli_route.rs`,
[#9926](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/9926)) proposes an
`openagents` command for a chat turn. The owner decided on 2026-09-28 that the
phone's chat offers only read-only commands.

| Invariant | Status | Checked by |
| --- | --- | --- |
| The command tree the route descends is generated from the `openagents` command's own help text (the top-level table and each group's `USAGE`), never written by hand. Every command declares its effect and where the phone runs it next to its `USAGE`; a command without a declaration, a declaration without a command, a usage line that does not parse, and a bundled `tree.json` that differs from the live help each fail a test. Commands that move money are labeled `spends`, the seed export `secret`, and enrollment, invitation, and revocation `grants`. | New on 2026-09-28 ([#9926](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/9926)). | `every_command_parses_and_declares_its_effect`, `the_bundled_tree_is_generated_from_this_help`, `money_and_secrets_are_labeled` in `crates/openagents-cli`; `an_undeclared_command_or_a_stray_declaration_fails`, `the_bundled_tree_parses` in `crates/coder` (`cli_route::tree`) |
| The descent is typed: each level is one Jev Choice over exactly that node's commands plus `none`, each described by its subtree, and the descent keeps at most two paths (the argmax and a second choice at p ≥ 0.20, from the router's group and at most two other likely groups); a path is proposed only when it ends at a command, the geometric mean of its edges reaches the level floor, and no edge is under 0.20, so `none` or an unsure level ends it. A value the device can enumerate (a host, a workspace label, a usage-line enum) is selected among candidates code lists; only free text is written by the model, and the assembled command must pass the command's own argument parser (`coder::argv::Args`, which `openagents` itself runs) with every required value present. A missing or unparsable value is asked for, never guessed. No routing reads message text by keyword. | New on 2026-09-28 ([#9926](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/9926)). | `each_level_lists_exactly_its_children_and_none`, `none_or_an_unsure_level_ends_the_descent`, `a_host_is_selected_from_the_devices_computers_or_asked_for`, `a_fill_that_does_not_parse_asks_rather_than_guesses`, `validates_with_the_commands_own_parser`, `free_text_is_written_by_the_model_and_checked` in `crates/coder` (`cli_route`) |
| The route never proposes a `spends` or `secret` command on any surface, and on the phone proposes only the owner's read-only list (`computer list`, `show`, `workspaces`; `verse who`, `quests`, `board`, `xp`; `kb search`; `cap`, `prg`, and `ext list`; `session list`). A command it may not offer costs no model call and reaches the router as no command. The route runs nothing. On the phone and the desktop every proposal is an offer that needs a tap; in a terminal (`openagents chat`, OpenAgents Terminal) the computer looks the command up in its own build's command tree, never the worker's word: a `read_only` command runs at once and the reply shows what it printed, one that changes something there runs only after Enter (or `chat run-command`), and a `spends` or `secret` command, or one the tree does not know or whose words its parser refuses, never runs from the chat. | New on 2026-09-28 ([#9926](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/9926)). The labeled evaluation found zero `spends` or `secret` offers. Read-only commands run at once in a terminal since 2026-10-02 ([#10170](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/10170)). | `no_surface_offers_money_or_secrets`, `the_phone_offers_nothing_but_its_list`, `the_phone_list_is_read_only_commands_in_the_tree`, `money_is_never_proposed_and_costs_no_model_call`, `the_phone_is_not_offered_what_is_off_its_list`, `a_command_here_has_its_tree_effect_only_when_its_words_parse` in `crates/coder` (`cli_route`); `a_read_only_command_a_reply_proposes_runs_at_once_and_shows_its_output`, `a_command_that_changes_something_waits_for_the_confirm_and_money_never_runs` in `crates/openagents-chat`; `a_proposed_command_shows_itself_and_enter_runs_it` in `crates/openagents-terminal` |
| A wallet request in the chat always means the user's built-in OpenAgents wallet: the router never asks which wallet, app, provider, or exchange, and never names another. In a terminal a sure `wallet` route descends the `wallet` command group (so a balance request runs `openagents wallet balance` there, whose answer is one plain sentence that names no node, network, chain server, channel, liquidity, or msat); elsewhere a wallet reply is a prepared wallet answer or the model told `WALLET_NOTE`; a request to send money is the wallet's own send answer. | New on 2026-10-02 ([#10170](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/10170)). | `recorded_wallet_requests_reach_the_built_in_wallet` (recorded Jev responses), `wallet_answers_are_plain`, and the ignored `live_jev` and `live_hosted_chat` in `crates/coder/tests/wallet_eval.rs`; `a_wallet_request_in_a_terminal_descends_the_wallet_commands` in `crates/coder` (`router::policy`) |
| The chat worker never learns the device's computers: the route selects a `HOST` only from candidates its caller supplies (a desktop or terminal chat's own device store), and without them asks which computer. The route's evidence records command words and each level's choice and probability, never the message or a free-text value. | New on 2026-09-28 ([#9926](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/9926)). | `a_host_is_selected_from_the_devices_computers_or_asked_for`, `free_text_is_written_by_the_model_and_checked` in `crates/coder` (`cli_route`) |

## Coder cloud fallback

The owner decided on 2026-09-28 that Coder's Vertex provider goes through
the OpenAgents cloud and is the no-setup fallback
([#9919](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/9919)). The
path, limits, and refusals are in `docs/coder/runtime/cloud-fallback.md`.

| Invariant | Status | Checked by |
| --- | --- | --- |
| No provider token is on a user's machine for the cloud fallback. Coder's `vertex` provider is connected from `CODER_CLOUD` alone (only `off` turns it off) and generates only as NIP-CJ conversation jobs signed by the host's own Nostr key and encrypted to the OpenAgents cloud worker, which holds the model credential. Coder reads no Vertex token file and no Google credential. | New on 2026-09-28; replaces the host Vertex token of #9891. | `the_cloud_fallback_needs_nothing_on_the_host` in `crates/microcoder-loop`; `the_default_worker_is_a_public_key_and_the_door_needs_no_token` in `crates/coder` |
| The cloud is always the last provider in Microcoder's lineup, so a host with no provider configured still answers a turn through it, and a host with a provider that has capacity never spends the cloud's quota: a signed-in Claude Code or Codex CLI is chosen over Microcoder on the cloud, and an own door key takes the cloud off the lineup. | New on 2026-09-28. | `the_cloud_is_always_the_last_provider_and_needs_no_configuration`, `the_cloud_answers_only_when_nothing_on_the_host_can`, `a_fresh_host_completes_a_turn_through_the_cloud_fallback` in `crates/coder` |
| The cloud worker's `rate_limited`, `busy`, and `quota_exhausted` are capacity refusals held until the worker's `retry_after_ms` (else one minute, one minute, and the next UTC midnight), so the loop fails over and the no-capacity sentence names the reset; any other refusal is the step's plain error and holds nothing. | New on 2026-09-28. | `the_cloud_worker_codes_are_capacity_refusals_with_their_waits` in `crates/microcoder-loop`; `a_quota_refusal_is_a_typed_capacity_refusal_with_its_wait`, `a_rate_limit_holds_a_minute_and_a_too_large_step_is_only_an_error`, `a_cloud_refusal_mid_turn_is_recorded_and_the_next_provider_finishes` in `crates/coder` |

## Chat history over the tailnet

| Invariant | Status | Checked by |
| --- | --- | --- |
| A host accepts a direct chat-history (SESS observer) connection only on its tailnet admission listener, bound to its own tailnet address and never a wildcard or LAN address, and only from a caller in Tailscale's ranges whose local `tailscale whois` names the host machine's own untagged user; every other caller gets a refusal and no read. | New on 2026-09-28, owner-directed: chats loaded seconds slowly through the public relay. It adds a transport to the tailnet admission row above; it admits no one that admission would not. It stays with tailnet admission as an optional path; a phone connected by QR reads chats through its NIP-HOST grant's `observe` instead. | `a_direct_connection_is_welcomed_only_for_the_hosts_own_user`, `the_listener_binds_only_a_tailnet_address` in `crates/coder-host` |
| A direct read is the same sealed observer request and reply as a relay read: the host runs every signature, grant, authorization, client, root, freshness, rate, and revocation check except the relay binding, a pairing redemption travels only through its relay, and the device verifies the reply exactly as a relay reply. | New on 2026-09-28. A transport change, not a permission change. | `direct_requests_pass_every_relay_check_but_the_relay_binding` in `crates/coder-connect` |
| A device key holds one chat-history (SESS observer) grant at a host: a new pairing revokes that device's earlier grants in the same commit, and they leave the book once no retained invitation names them. A revoked grant never blocks a new pairing: while the book's 64 grants are full, the longest-revoked grant leaves it. A request under a grant that left the book is refused and reads nothing; only live grants to 64 devices refuse a new device. | New on 2026-09-28 ([#9899](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/9899)), after live test runs and revoked grants filled a host's 64 grants. It narrows authority (a re-paired device loses its earlier grant) and changes only which refusal a revoked grant's request gets. | `a_device_that_pairs_again_holds_one_grant`, `revoked_grants_never_block_a_new_pairing`, `expired_admissions_are_pruned_by_the_invitation_flow` in `crates/coder-connect` |
| Replay protection is unchanged across routes: the host binds each request ID to its exact event before it reads for it, and reads it at most once whichever route carries it, a retry, or another host process sharing the store; an exact retry gets the same reply while the host keeps it, else a signed conflict, or no reply while another process that admitted it is answering. | New on 2026-09-28. Reinterpreted on 2026-09-28 for read speed: a read never rewrites the book. Bindings, relay replies, and read-window counts go to an append-only request log (`observer.requests`) under the store lock, without a sync per read, and independent reads run at once. The log survives a host process crash; an OS crash or power loss can lose its last unsynced lines, so a request replayed within its 60 seconds after one could be read again, and the rate window could undercount (model boundary). Grant changes still rewrite and sync the book. A direct reply's bytes stay in memory, and a relay retry of it gets a signed conflict. | `a_request_is_read_once_on_either_route`, `another_process_never_reads_a_bound_request_again`, `a_failed_request_log_write_returns_no_reply_and_a_retry_reads`, `expired_replies_of_every_grant_leave_the_log`, `request_expiry_rechecked_after_read_and_rate_is_durable` in `crates/coder-connect` |
| Relay replies keep their bounds (128 KiB bodies, 32 KiB pages, 32-chat catalogs) and inline bodies; only a direct reply may be larger, and a host refuses a direct-sized request that arrives through a relay. A direct reply's body travels beside its host-signed envelope, NIP-44 encrypted to the device under the same conversation key, and the device accepts only the exact bytes the envelope's digest and size name. | New on 2026-09-28. | `direct_bounds_apply_only_to_direct_replies`, `a_detached_body_must_be_the_bytes_its_envelope_names` in `crates/coder-connect`; `a_direct_backward_page_is_large_and_keeps_the_newest_records_that_fit` in `crates/coder-history` |
| A direct connection's nudge carries only a source ID already disclosed on that connection, or `catalog`, and no bytes, length, or title; it grants nothing, and a device reads again only with an ordinary sealed request. A device with no direct connection reads through the relay. | New on 2026-09-28. Filesystem notifications drive the nudges, a folder appearing under a root counts as a list change (inotify does not report a chat written into a new folder at once, #9989), and a catalog nudge covers every root the host serves; on `coder host` that is only the Coder task directory (#9920). | `a_direct_connection_reads_at_once_and_nudges_when_a_read_chat_grows`, `a_direct_connection_nudges_the_catalog_when_any_harness_starts_a_chat`, `a_coder_only_host_watches_only_the_coder_task_directory`, `a_host_without_direct_reads_falls_back_to_the_relay_route` in `crates/coder-connect` |
| `coder host` serves only Coder task chats: every read under any grant uses only the grant's Coder task roots, so a grant that also names Codex, Claude, OpenCode, or Devin history lists only its Coder chats (an empty list when it names no Coder root), a read of any other source is refused, and only the Coder task directory is watched. New invitations name only the Coder task directory. The host runs no copy of OpenCode's or Devin's session stores. A grant names one to eight sources (three before OpenCode); a device built before then refuses a grant that names more than three or an `opencode` or `devin` source. | New on 2026-09-28 ([#9920](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/9920)), owner-directed: the phone shows only Coder chats. It narrows disclosure and replaces the OpenCode and Devin mirror rows (#9915, #9916): the host no longer reads `opencode.db` or Devin's `sessions.db` on a timer. | `a_coder_only_host_serves_only_coder_chats_and_their_delegates_under_any_grant`, `a_coder_only_host_watches_only_the_coder_task_directory` in `crates/coder-connect` |
| A session a Coder task delegates to on an OpenCode or Devin route is copied, when each turn ends, only from the store the engine used (OpenCode's engine database, Devin's store for the agent's environment), opened read-only, into the task directory as `<task>.delegate.<agent>.<session>.jsonl`, never reading the agent's credentials. The copy only grows while the session only grows (a later turn appends), a rewritten session is written as a new file, and large fields are cut and marked. The Coder catalog lists it as the task's subagent (`harness` the agent, `native_id` the task ID, `subagent` true, archived with its task); no other file name in the task directory lists. | New on 2026-09-28 ([#9920](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/9920)). | `a_delegate_copy_lists_under_its_task_grows_by_appending_and_restarts_when_rewritten` (opencode), `an_engine_session_is_copied_beside_its_task_and_a_next_turn_only_appends` (devin), `a_delegate_session_beside_its_task_lists_as_the_tasks_subagent_and_reads`, `a_delegate_file_name_round_trips_and_refuses_other_shapes` in `crates/coder-history`; `the_delegate_copy_reads_devins_store_under_the_agents_data_home` in `crates/microcoder` |
| A transcript cursor binds the file's incarnation and the SHA-256 of every byte before it, and a page is served only when the host's hash of those bytes matches; a replaced file, a truncated or shortened file, a consumed-prefix rewrite that leaves the length unchanged, a rewrite that changes the file's first 4 KiB, and a forged record position refuse with `SourceChanged`. The host may hash onward from a remembered mark (the hash state at a record boundary, kept for the process's life per incarnation, at most 64 files) instead of from byte 0; marks are dropped, and the hash taken from byte 0, whenever the file is shorter than when marked, is the same length with a different last-write time, or has different first bytes. The bytes from the nearest mark to the cursor are hashed and compared on every read. | Relaxed on 2026-09-28 for transcript read speed: before, every page rehashed the whole prefix. For an append-only session file (how every harness writes them) a mark's hash is exactly the prefix hash, so nothing is weakened; the one case a mark can hide is a file rewritten in place, keeping its inode and its first 4 KiB, made longer between two reads, with the change more than one mark interval (1 MiB) before the cursor. | `rewritten_prefix_truncation_replacement_and_forged_cursor_refuse`, `marked_prefix_hashes_match_the_whole_prefix_and_a_rewrite_still_refuses` in `crates/coder-history` |
| The chat list's index (`catalog-index.json` in the observer's private directory, written `0600` and replaced whole) is a cache, never authority: it keeps only each source's first-record fields and first-prompt line with the file identity and length they were read at, an entry is used only while the file still has that identity and at least that length (exactly that length unless the read was complete), and a missing, unreadable, oversized, or unrecognized index is ignored. A first catalog page always scans every source. A later page is answered from the listing a scan built within the last 30 seconds only while every directory and index file that listing read is unchanged; its members are therefore checked on every page, a changed membership still refuses the cursor, and only order and times can be that old. | New on 2026-09-28. | `a_restarted_host_lists_from_its_index_and_ignores_a_bad_one`, `a_later_page_keeps_its_listing_until_a_directory_changes`, `an_unchanged_scan_reuses_its_listing_and_a_changed_title_does_not`, `catalog_cursor_ignores_titles_but_refuses_changed_membership` in `crates/coder-history` |

## Device identity key

| Invariant | Status | Checked by |
| --- | --- | --- |
| The OpenAgents app's device key is a random secp256k1 key the host creates on the device and keeps in a this-device-only store (Keychain `com.openagents.app.device`, `AfterFirstUnlockThisDeviceOnly`, not synchronized). It is not derived from a NIP-06 seed phrase, and the app says so. | New on 2026-09-28. | `keys_show_as_npub_first_with_hex_beside_it` in `crates/openagents-mobile` |
| The device's secret key reaches the interface only in the answer to an explicit `account` request with `reveal: true`, which the Identity Keys screen sends after the person taps **Reveal nsec** and confirms a warning. The app packet never carries it, and no log line, file, or setting records it. The screen drops it when it closes or the app leaves the foreground, and copies it only on an explicit tap, to this device's pasteboard alone, expiring after 60 seconds (on Android, a clip marked sensitive that the app clears after 60 seconds while it is still the clipboard's content, with screenshots blocked while the nsec shows). | New on 2026-09-28 ([owner request](bins/openagents-ios/README.md#account)). It adds the first export path for the device key; before, the key never left Keychain and the Rust library. | `only_an_explicit_reveal_returns_the_nsec`, `the_app_packet_never_carries_the_secret_key` in `crates/openagents-mobile` |
| The phone's iroh secret key, which its QUIC endpoint proves when it connects a computer, is a random 32-byte key generated on the device apart from every Nostr key and kept in the same this-device-only store as the device key (Keychain `com.openagents.app.iroh`, `AfterFirstUnlockThisDeviceOnly`, not synchronized; on Android its own Keystore-encrypted file, not backed up). It reaches Rust only in the launch configuration; no packet, log line, file, or setting carries it. An `EndpointId`, the phone's or a computer's, never authorizes anything: a scanned connect code's grant is kept only when it is signed by the code's host key, names this device, and carries exactly the connect-code rights, and a channel over iroh is admitted by the NIP-REACH handshake and the grant, as over TCP. | New on 2026-09-29 ([#9971](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/9971)). | `the_app_shows_connect_and_its_packet_never_carries_the_iroh_key` in `crates/openagents-mobile`; `an_answer_signed_by_another_host_key_is_refused`, `only_the_connect_code_rights_are_kept`, `a_phone_pairs_over_loopback_iroh_and_keeps_the_grant_and_route` in `crates/coder-host` |

## Trainer XP

| Invariant | Status | Checked by |
| --- | --- | --- |
| A trainer's XP and level are a pure function of public signed NIP-XP events, the reader's trust list, and the named curve `trainer-curve-v1`. The OpenAgents app trusts the OpenAgents referee alone, re-checks every award against the evidence it names, and never publishes quests, awards, or XP, spends, or converts XP; nothing pays. It publishes only the world key's trainer profile and trainer card, each after an explicit, confirmed tap. | New on 2026-09-28 ([#9847](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/9847)); the profile exception added the same day ([#9895](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/9895)). | `a_reproduction_shows_on_the_board_the_tag_and_the_card` in `crates/verse`; `the_preview_card_shows_level_three_under_the_named_curve` in `crates/openagents-mobile` |
| Under NIP-XP's `reproduce` rule, the reproducer is never the claimant, a reproduction's run record is never the claim's, and the referee signs an award only after checking the reproducer's run record file against the digest the reproduction names. | New on 2026-09-28. | `the_claimant_cant_reproduce_their_own_attempt`, `a_failed_copied_late_or_uncited_reproduction_is_refused` in `crates/nostr`; `a_claim_a_reproduction_and_its_award_level_up_the_reproducer` in `crates/microcoder` |
| A level shows over another player's head in the Grid, or on a board, only when that key's newest valid trainer profile (NIP-XP `13193`, signed by the key) says `shown: true`; without one, the tag is the key's prefix alone. The ledger still counts every key's awards. | New on 2026-09-28 ([#9895](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/9895)). | `a_level_shows_over_a_head_only_after_the_trainer_opts_in` in `crates/verse`; `only_a_shown_profile_advertises_a_trainer` in `crates/xp-ledger`; `a_level_shows_only_after_the_trainer_publishes_a_shown_profile`, `a_preview_profile_change_publishes_nothing` in `crates/openagents-mobile` |
| A key's XP counts toward a trainer only when both keys signed: the trainer's newest profile lists the key and the key's newest link (NIP-XP `13195`) names the trainer. Links are one level deep, move no XP between awards, and either side ends one. The phone adds or removes a listed key only on an explicit **Link a key** or **Remove**; the other key signs its link on its own device, so no secret key moves. | New on 2026-09-28 ([#9896](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/9896)). | `a_key_counts_toward_a_trainer_only_when_both_keys_signed`, `links_are_one_level_deep`, `a_linked_trainer_sums_xp_across_its_keys` in `crates/xp-ledger`; `a_linked_key_raises_the_trainers_level_over_both_heads` in `crates/verse`; `linking_a_key_lists_it_and_its_link_back_counts_its_xp` in `crates/openagents-mobile`; `a_computer_key_links_to_its_trainer_and_can_withdraw` in `crates/microcoder` |
| A trainer card (NIP-XP `30194`) is a claim, never trusted: `openagents xp verify-card` checks its signature, derives the ledger from the relay under the card's own trust list, and reports every difference in keys, awards, XP, and level, exiting 1 on any. The phone signs a card only from the ledger it read, names the trust list it read under, and publishes it only after **Export card** and a confirmation; a preview card is never published. | New on 2026-09-28 ([#9898](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/9898)). | `a_card_checks_clean_and_each_inflation_is_reported` in `crates/verse`; `a_card_refuses_another_signer_foreign_awards_and_tampering` in `crates/nostr`; `exporting_signs_a_card_that_checks_and_publishes_it_with_a_link`, `a_preview_card_is_signed_but_never_published` in `crates/openagents-mobile`; `a_card_verifies_against_the_relay_and_an_inflated_one_is_refused` in `crates/openagents-cli` (live, local relay) |
| A `per-awardee` quest version pays each distinct reproducer at most once and at most `max_awards` awards in all; only the reproducer's role carries XP, and a version with more live awards than its `max_awards`, or two live awards for one reproducer, counts for no one until the referee revokes the extras. | New on 2026-09-28 ([#9894](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/9894)). It relaxes one award per quest version for `reproduce` quests that opt in, so a tutorial can pay every newcomer once. | `a_per_awardee_quest_states_its_max_and_pays_only_the_reproducer`, `a_per_awardee_award_is_keyed_to_its_reproducer` in `crates/nostr`; `a_per_awardee_quest_pays_each_reproducer_once_up_to_its_max`, `a_per_awardee_quest_pays_one_reproducer_once` in `crates/xp-ledger`; `a_per_awardee_tutorial_pays_each_reproducer_once_up_to_its_max` in `crates/microcoder` |
| Playtest XP never feeds the trainer level: `playtest` awards are signed by a separate playtest referee key, the app trusts only that key for its playtest card and titles (and none while `PLAYTEST_REFEREE` is unset), and a reader keeps playtest XP apart from the `trainer-curve-v1` total even when it trusts both referees. No playtest referee secret is generated or stored by the app or the repository; the owner creates it with `microcoder xp playtest-keygen`. | New on 2026-09-28 ([#9885](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/9885)). | `playtest_awards_count_on_their_own_card_and_never_in_the_trainer_level` in `crates/verse`; `a_playtest_referee_accepts_a_testers_report_once` in `crates/microcoder`; `openagents playtest award` signs only with the trusted playtest referee key and refuses while `PLAYTEST_REFEREE` is unset ([#9906](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/9906)): `no_award_is_signed_without_the_playtest_referee_key` in `crates/playtest`, `without_the_playtest_referee_key_nothing_is_read_or_signed` in `crates/openagents-cli` |
| Under NIP-XP's `playtest` rule, the tester is never the triager, the moderator, or the referee; joining or installing earns nothing (no quest exists for it); one issue earns at most one report-class award per season from a referee; and a quest version over its `max_awards` counts none of its awards. | New on 2026-09-28 ([#9885](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/9885)). | `the_rule_refuses_contributions_that_do_not_qualify`, `a_moderated_session_needs_the_moderators_record` in `crates/nostr`; `one_issue_earns_one_report_class_award_across_quests`, `a_quest_version_over_its_max_awards_counts_none` in `crates/knowledge` |
| The Verse world key (the trainer key) reaches the interface only in the direct reply to a `trainer` request with `reveal: true`, which the Trainer screen sends after the person taps **Reveal nsec** and confirms a warning. The app packet never carries it; the screen drops it when it closes or the app leaves the foreground, and copies it only on an explicit tap, to this device's pasteboard alone, expiring after 60 seconds (on Android, a clip marked sensitive that the app clears after 60 seconds, with screenshots blocked while the nsec shows). | New on 2026-09-28; Android the same day. It adds the first export path for the world key, so a trainer can sign a reproduction with the key over their head in the Grid. | `only_an_explicit_reveal_returns_the_trainer_nsec`, `the_preview_card_shows_level_three_under_the_named_curve` in `crates/openagents-mobile` |
| Under NIP-XP's `eval-check` rule, a result earns credit when another trainer reruns it to protocol, whether the rerun confirms or disputes it: the checker is never the result's evaluator (a hosted run's requester) or the suite's author, neither verdict is inconclusive, the check is newer than the result, and both fall inside the season. Credit is for verification work, not agreement; a disputing check is paid like a confirming one, stays visible as a dispute, and confirms nothing, so it counts toward no candidate. Each role is paid once per suite version per season (`eval-check:<season>:<suite>:<role>:<key>`), a key holding two roles is paid once in the larger, and the OpenAgents referee also refuses a check whose key is linked, both sides signed, to the evaluator's or the suite author's trainer. The referee job signs only with the existing referee key, never creates one, and never signs a second award for a key that holds a live one. | New on 2026-09-29 ([#9938](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/9938)); disputes paid from 2026-09-29 ([#9956](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/9956)), which relaxed "only when a check confirms it". | `a_disputed_check_earns_credit_and_an_inconclusive_one_earns_nothing`, `a_confirming_check_pays_each_role_once_in_its_own_award` in `crates/nostr`; `a_confirming_check_credits_the_checker_the_evaluator_and_the_suite_author_once`, `each_role_is_paid_once_per_suite_version_and_a_new_version_pays_again`, `a_key_holding_two_roles_is_paid_once_in_the_larger`, `a_dispute_earns_credit_a_self_check_earns_nothing_and_the_dispute_stays_visible`, `a_revoked_eval_award_stops_counting_and_a_replacement_counts`, `the_ledger_replays_to_the_same_totals_in_any_order_and_with_repeats`, `an_untrusted_referee_is_ignored`, `linked_keys_of_one_trainer_are_one_trainer_to_the_referee` in `crates/xp-ledger`; `a_confirmed_check_earns_one_award_per_role_and_a_rerun_signs_nothing_twice`, `a_self_check_an_early_check_and_a_linked_key_are_refused`, `a_closed_season_and_a_suite_that_isnt_a_starter_are_left_alone` in `crates/microcoder` (live, local relay) |
| Adoption into Coder's defaults is never automatic. A tool is a candidate only when a result on it is Better, checks by at least three distinct trainers (linked keys counting once, the result's trainer and the suite author not counting) confirmed it, and at least one Better result on a second suite externally validates it: a suite whose release is signed by someone other than the tool's author and created after the tool's release, on the same task distribution, naming the result with the `validates` marker; a reader that doesn't hold both releases assumes no independence. An admission that admits cites at least one such validation or doesn't parse; one that cites a result whose subject identity is `unresolved` is refused, and the admission's default lifetime follows the identity strength (365, 90, or 14 days). Only an operator's `microcoder xp adopt --subject`, signed with the `coder-defaults` key on the referee host, writes the admission and publishes the release. `eval-adopt` pays each role once per tool release, needs a confirming check and a validation, and a reader counts an `eval-adopt` award only when it holds the manifest and admission bytes the release pins. | New on 2026-09-29 ([#9938](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/9938)); external validation required from 2026-09-29 ([#9956](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/9956)). | `an_adoption_needs_an_externally_validated_result`, `an_unresolved_subject_is_never_adopted`, `a_second_suite_validates_transfers_or_isnt_independent` in `crates/nostr`; `the_admission_lifetime_follows_the_subjects_identity_and_the_record_agrees`, `a_better_result_is_a_candidate_after_three_distinct_trainers_confirm_and_one_validates`, `an_adoption_credits_the_tool_author_the_suite_author_and_the_evaluator` in `crates/xp-ledger`; `an_operator_adopts_a_candidate_and_the_referee_credits_the_adoption` in `crates/microcoder` (live, local relay) |
| Evaluation credit is XP and a name, never money: no code path in the ledger converts, spends, or transfers XP, a credit carries only XP and who earned it, playtest XP stays off a reader that trusts only the trainer referee, and no quest template, package record, or command help promises a payment. | New on 2026-09-29 ([#9938](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/9938)). | `no_code_path_converts_xp_and_no_copy_promises_money`, `playtest_xp_stays_off_a_reader_that_trusts_only_the_trainer_referee` in `crates/xp-ledger`; `the_built_in_templates_parse_and_promise_no_money` in `crates/microcoder` |
| A runtime admits an adopted extension only under the same checks a ledger makes before crediting the adoption: the newest valid `coder-defaults` release whose manifest it holds, and for each dependency a held admission whose decision is `admit`, whose subject is that dependency's release, and whose `expires_at` hasn't passed; a dependency without one admits nothing and is named as lapsed, and a release whose documents aren't held admits nothing. The hosted runner admits the defaults it holds in both arms (a marginal report naming the release in `meta.ext_eval.defaults`), Coder on a computer admits only the programs and skills the sync wrote beside a lock, and neither widens the operator's effects ceiling. Every run records the defaults lock's digest. A hosted validation (`validates`) runs a published second suite on the same tool and never a draft, and is refused when the cited result ran the same suite or another tool. | New on 2026-09-29 ([#9961](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/9961)). | `the_defaults_admit_a_dependency_only_under_a_live_admission_and_the_lock_round_trips` in `crates/xp-ledger`; `a_validation_names_the_result_it_validates_and_never_a_check_too` in `crates/nostr`; `a_second_suite_validates_a_result_and_an_adoption_makes_the_next_run_marginal` in `crates/eval-runner`; `the_lock_grants_the_programs_that_are_here_and_names_the_rest` in `crates/coder` |

## Phone wallet

The owner decided on 2026-09-28 to replace the phone's Mutinynet test wallet
with Breez's Spark wallet on Bitcoin mainnet
([#9854](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/9854),
[docs/breez/](docs/breez/README.md)). On 2026-10-02 the owner decided that
computers run the same Spark wallet from the same seed
([#10202](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/10202)); the
shared code is `crates/spark-wallet`, and `ldk-node` stays only as the x402
receiver, under `openagents x402 node`.

| Invariant | Status | Checked by |
| --- | --- | --- |
| The OpenAgents app's Wallet tab runs Breez's Spark SDK (`breez-sdk-spark`) on Bitcoin mainnet only, a network fixed in `crates/openagents-mobile/src/wallet.rs`. No request, configuration, or stored value switches it to another network. Real-time sync to Breez's server, cross-chain sends, and Stable Balance are off. It never opens the x402 node's directory or any treasury wallet. Regtest is used only by tests. | Rewritten on 2026-09-28 (was Mutinynet signet on `ldk-node`). | `the_phone_wallet_runs_on_mainnet_with_the_committed_key`; live: `a_mainnet_wallet_connects_with_the_committed_key` and `a_regtest_wallet_connects_and_reads_its_balance` (ignored, run by hand) in `crates/openagents-mobile` |
| The Spark seed is BIP39 entropy (16 bytes when the phone creates it; 16 or 32 when restored) in its own Keychain item on iOS (`com.openagents.app.spark`, this device only, when unlocked, not synchronized) and, on Android, encrypted with AES-GCM under its own Android Keystore key (`com.openagents.app.spark-v1`) in `noBackupFilesDir`, excluded from backup and device transfer; either way separate from the device and world keys and the x402 node's key. Rust receives it with `wallet_open` and keeps it and its mnemonic in memory only: neither is written to disk, logged, or put in the app packet or an error; the entropy leaves the phone only sealed to a computer the owner approved (the computer wallet row below). The recovery words leave Rust only in the direct reply to `wallet_words`, which the host sends after the person confirms a warning, and a restore's entropy only in the direct reply to `wallet_restore_check`. The words sheet copies them, as one space-separated line, only on an explicit tap after they are shown, to this device's pasteboard alone, expiring after 60 seconds; Android marks the clip sensitive and clears it after 60 seconds while it is still the clipboard's content, and blocks screenshots and recording (`FLAG_SECURE`) while the words show or a restore is typed. | Rewritten on 2026-09-28; the copy was added on 2026-09-28 at the owner's request. | `failures_are_typed_and_never_carry_the_key`, `recovery_words_restore_and_replace_the_wallet` |
| A payment is sent only for the quote on screen, after the person confirms its amount and fee, once, with a fresh idempotency key; a stale quote ID pays nothing. | New on 2026-09-28. | `send_quotes_the_fee_and_pays_only_the_confirmed_quote_once` |
| A purchase page opens only if its URL is `https`, and only once. A MoonPay page the SDK cannot get signed (Breez's server answers `SignUrl` with `Unimplemented`) is built unsigned from the SDK's key and parameters for the wallet's own deposit address, and only for a plain alphanumeric address. An on-chain deposit is claimed automatically at maturity up to the network's recommended fee rate plus 1 sat/vB; a claim the person starts is sent only after its quote, with the quoted fee as its ceiling. | New on 2026-09-28 ([#9865](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/9865)). | `buying_opens_the_provider_page_once_and_deposits_are_claimed_at_the_quoted_fee`, `moonpay_opens_unsigned_for_the_deposit_address` |
| A Lightning address or LNURL-pay code is quoted only within the recipient's advertised range, in whole base units (BIP 177 bitcoin; LNURL's msat bounds rounded inward); a comment is sent only to a recipient that takes one and only within its length. Text a recipient supplies (its description and its success message) reaches the screen as plain text, and a success action's URL is shown, never opened. The phone registers no Lightning address (`register_lightning_address` is never called) until a self-hosted OpenAgents LNURL server has its own service issue. | New on 2026-09-28 ([#9859](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/9859)). | `lightning_addresses_and_lnurl_codes_quote_within_the_recipients_terms`, `lnurl_terms_read_ranges_comments_and_metadata`; live: `a_mainnet_lightning_address_quotes_without_paying` (ignored, run by hand) |
| A deposit refund is sent only after the person reviews its destination (a mainnet Bitcoin address other than the wallet's own deposit address), its rate, and its estimated fee, once, at the reviewed rate; a rate whose fee would take the whole deposit is refused, and a deposit with a refund already broadcast offers no claim or refund. An on-chain withdrawal pays at the speed shown as chosen on the confirm screen, and choosing a speed sends nothing. | New on 2026-09-28 ([#9862](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/9862)). | `a_refund_is_sent_once_to_a_reviewed_address_at_a_chosen_speed`, `each_deposit_state_reads_plainly_and_only_open_ones_can_be_acted_on`, `an_onchain_withdrawal_shows_each_speed_and_pays_the_chosen_one` |
| The unilateral-exit state is saved after each sync only in the app's store encrypted with the device key, and reaches the interface only in the direct reply to an explicit `wallet_exit_export`, never in the app packet; a restore to other recovery words clears it. The offline wallet fixture is compiled only into debug builds (`#[cfg(debug_assertions)]`; a release build has no fixture code), runs only when the host asks, and uses its own folders. | New on 2026-09-28 ([#9862](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/9862)); compiled out of release on 2026-09-29 ([#9955](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/9955)). | `the_exit_state_is_saved_after_each_sync_and_exported_on_request`, `a_large_exit_state_round_trips_encrypted_in_parts_and_clears` |
| An npub is paid only at a destination its own key signed: the Spark address in its newest valid NIP-A3 payment targets (kind 10133) first, else the `lud16` Lightning address in its newest valid kind-0 profile, else the payment is refused. Events with a bad ID or signature or another author, and values without a mainnet Spark or Lightning address shape, are ignored. The confirm screen names the person, the address, and where it came from, and the payment still needs the person's tap. Paying an npub is never a zap. The phone publishes its Spark address as a kind-10133 event signed by the device key only while the person's **Publish my Spark address** setting is on, keeping other published targets, and removes it when turned off. | New on 2026-09-28 ([#9860](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/9860), [docs/breez/paying-people.md](docs/breez/paying-people.md)). | `an_npub_resolves_to_its_spark_address_then_its_lightning_address_or_is_refused`, `only_the_owners_newest_valid_events_count`, `publishing_replaces_only_the_spark_target`, `an_npub_is_paid_at_its_published_address_after_the_person_confirms`, `the_spark_address_is_published_only_by_the_setting_and_can_be_removed`, `scanned_and_pasted_codes_route_by_kind`; live: `a_throwaway_key_publishes_and_resolves_its_spark_address_on_live_relays` (ignored, run by hand) |
| Every bitcoin amount the app shows or reads goes through the shared `bitcoin-amount` crate: BIP 177 integer base units (`₿12,345`, spoken "12,345 bitcoin") by default, or legacy BTC (`0.00012345 BTC`) when the person chooses it in the Wallet. The choice is saved on the phone and applies to every surface at once. Amounts are `u64` base units internally and in storage, never floating point; typed amounts above 21,000,000 BTC, below one base unit, or of zero are refused. Product copy does not say "sat" or "sats"; protocol and machine-readable names (`_sats` and `_msat` fields, BOLT11 and LNURL msat, x402 `asset: "BTC"` amounts, CLI flags) keep their names ([docs/breez/amounts.md](docs/breez/amounts.md)). | Owner decision, 2026-09-28 ([#9881](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/9881)). | `crates/bitcoin-amount` tests; `the_legacy_btc_format_shows_and_reads_decimal_btc`, `the_amount_format_is_saved_and_reaches_the_wallet`, `the_choice_persists_and_defaults_to_bip177` in `crates/openagents-mobile` |
| The Wallet's main surface is the balance, Receive, Send, recent activity, the large-balance warning, and a backup card; every other wallet control is under Advanced, and nothing is removed. The backup card shows until the person taps **I wrote them down** on the recovery words sheet (or restores from words), which saves only a salted SHA-256 fingerprint of the seed, never the seed or its words, so a different wallet on the phone asks again. Moving a control never skips its confirmation: spending still goes through the confirm screen, and showing the words still asks first. | New on 2026-09-28 (the owner's simplification of the Wallet screen). | `the_main_screen_is_plain_and_the_rest_waits_under_advanced`, `the_wallets_advanced_section_opens_from_the_host_and_is_remembered` in `crates/openagents-mobile` |
| The Breez API key is committed in source (`crates/spark-wallet/src/spark.rs`, shared by the phone and computers). The owner confirmed with the Breez team that it is a basic validation key that every shipped app carries and none can hide; it authorizes no spending. It is the only API key exempt from the repository's no-keys-in-source rule. | Owner decision, 2026-09-28. | `the_phone_wallet_runs_on_mainnet_with_the_committed_key` |
| `openagents wallet` on a computer runs the same Spark wallet as the phone, on Bitcoin mainnet only (`openagents_spark::computer::NETWORK`; no flag or stored value switches it), with Breez's records in a JSON file under `~/.openagents/spark/wallets/` and the seed as hex entropy in `~/.openagents/spark/seed`, a 0600 file in a 0700 folder that a different seed never replaces without `--replace`. The seed arrives only by `openagents wallet link` (the phone seals it with NIP-44 to a one-time key that only the waiting command holds, after the owner approves, with Face ID or the passcode, a request on the phone showing the computer's name and the same six-digit code the computer prints) or `openagents wallet restore` (recovery words read with echo off). No command prints the seed or the words. Its answers are plain: a balance is one sentence and an address one line, naming no node, network, chain server, channel, liquidity, or msat; `send` pays only the quote it showed, after the person types yes or passes `--yes`, once, with a fresh idempotency key. `ldk-node` is only the x402 receiver, under `openagents x402 node`. | New on 2026-10-02 ([#10202](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/10202)). | `the_seed_is_a_private_file_and_never_silently_replaced`, `a_damaged_seed_file_is_reported_without_its_contents`, `only_the_requester_opens_the_envelope`, `both_screens_show_the_same_six_digits`, Breez's storage conformance suite (`crates/spark-wallet/tests/store.rs`), `a_balance_is_one_plain_sentence` and `every_answer_is_plain` in `openagents-cli`, `wallet_answers_are_plain`; the link: `the_operations_need_operate_and_check_their_fields` (`coder-access`), `an_ask_is_listed_until_answered_and_the_answer_is_read_once_forgotten` and `declines_expiry_and_unknown_asks` (`coder-host`), `a_listed_ask_shows_the_computer_and_its_code`, `approve_seals_the_seed_to_the_asking_computer`, `deny_answers_with_nothing`, and `without_a_wallet_nothing_is_answered` (`crates/openagents-mobile`); live: `a_computer_wallet_connects_reads_and_reopens_from_its_file` (ignored, run by hand) |

## Agent spending

Phase 1 of agent spending ([#9863](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/9863)):
an agent on one of the owner's computers asks, and the owner approves each
payment on the phone. The later phases ([#9864](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/9864))
add standing grants, which pay payees the owner trusted without a tap, and
allowance wallets on computers. The formats are in
[docs/breez/spend-protocol.md](docs/breez/spend-protocol.md).

| Invariant | Status | Checked by |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Nothing pays without the owner's tap, except what a standing grant the owner made admits (below). The phone pays an agent's spend request only when the owner taps Approve on that request's sheet (after Face ID or the passcode above ₿1,000), and then once, with a wallet idempotency key derived from the request ID; a repeat tap or retry never pays again. A request the checks refuse is answered with its refusal code without a tap, and a refusal moves nothing. | New on 2026-09-28; the standing-grant exception added on 2026-09-28 ([#9864](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/9864)). | `a_request_waits_for_the_owners_tap_and_approve_pays_once`, `approve_through_the_app_pays_in_the_background_and_delivers`, `deny_refuses_as_declined_and_nothing_pays`, `what_the_grant_refuses_is_answered_without_asking_the_owner`, `the_idempotency_key_is_a_uuid_fixed_by_the_request`, `large_payments_ask_for_face_id_and_the_ledger_survives_a_restart` in `crates/openagents-mobile` |
| The phone checks every request against its own grant for that computer and its ledger, and the first failing check names the refusal code. Approve checks again and reserves the amount plus the fee ceiling before the wallet is called; a pending payment stays reserved; only the wallet's own evidence releases one; the same request ID with different bytes is a `conflict`. The ledger and grants live in the app's encrypted store, so a restart never resets a period. | New on 2026-09-28. | `every_check_refuses_with_its_own_code`, `a_reservation_holds_the_amount_and_fee_until_settled_or_released`, `a_retried_request_reserves_once_and_a_reused_id_conflicts` in `crates/coder-access`; `wallet_failures_release_the_reservation_with_their_code`, `a_pending_payment_stays_reserved_until_the_wallet_settles_it`, `large_payments_ask_for_face_id_and_the_ledger_survives_a_restart` in `crates/openagents-mobile` |
| The approval sheet shows the payee, amount, and description as the phone decoded them from the invoice, and the wallet's own fee quote; the agent's note is shown as its words, never as the payee. Only mainnet BOLT11 invoices are paid, and a request never outlives its invoice. | New on 2026-09-28. | `a_request_waits_for_the_owners_tap_and_approve_pays_once`; `a_request_carries_its_exact_invoice_and_never_outlives_it` in `crates/coder-access`; `a_wallet_invoice_may_describe_itself_inline_but_not_for_x402` in `crates/nostr` |
| A `request` grant, issued by the phone for one computer, bounds only what that computer can ask for; it moves nothing by itself. Stopping a computer's requests advances its epoch: what waits is refused as `revoked` or `stale`, the computer keeps no grant, and it cannot ask again until the owner allows it; a grant at a lower epoch is refused. | New on 2026-09-28. | `the_default_grant_asks_only_and_validates`, `revocation_advances_the_epoch_and_old_requests_are_stale` in `crates/coder-access`; `blocking_a_computer_advances_its_epoch_and_refuses_what_waits` in `crates/openagents-mobile`; `a_newer_epoch_makes_waiting_requests_stale_and_an_old_grant_is_refused` in `crates/coder-host` |
| A host records a spend request only under a current grant a phone gave it, and asking again for the same ID and invoice returns the same request. `spend.list` needs `operate` and the sender's own grant naming this host; `spend.settle` is accepted only from the device whose grant the request draws on, a `paid` receipt only when its preimage hashes to the invoice's payment hash, and the first final receipt wins. A request that expires unanswered is refused as `phone_unreachable`, except that a proven payment still records over it. | New on 2026-09-28. | `a_host_asks_only_under_a_grant_a_phone_gave_it`, `the_host_refuses_what_the_grant_forbids_before_asking`, `the_first_final_receipt_wins_and_a_paid_one_must_prove_itself`, `an_unanswered_request_expires_as_phone_unreachable_unless_proven_paid` in `crates/coder-host`; `spend_lists_carry_the_senders_own_grant_and_need_operate`, `only_a_matching_preimage_proves_a_paid_receipt` in `crates/coder-access` |
| `openagents x402 --pay-with phone` pays only after the computer's own x402 policy admits the payment, only mainnet invoices, and only with a receipt whose preimage proves the payment; a denial fails the call with the phone's reason. | New on 2026-09-28. | `an_approved_payment_returns_the_phones_preimage_as_proof`, `a_denied_payment_fails_with_the_phones_reason` in `crates/openagents-cli` |
| A standing grant pays without a tap only a request its grant's checks admit whose amount plus fee ceiling is at most the automatic ceiling for one payment (₿1,000 by default, never above the grant's), whose purpose and decoded payee the owner listed, and which keeps the computer's automatic payments in the rolling window within the automatic, purpose, and payee ceilings; it is paid once with the approval's idempotency key, reserved and marked automatic in the ledger, and receipted like an approval. Everything else goes to the approval sheet; nothing is refused for an automatic ceiling. Only the owner makes a grant standing, by trusting a payee on the sheet after Face ID or the passcode; automatic ceilings never exceed the grant's; a replaced grant never refills the window; blocking a computer stops automatic payments with the rest. | New on 2026-09-28 ([#9864](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/9864)). | `a_standing_grant_names_automatic_payments_within_its_own_bounds`, `only_trusted_payees_and_purposes_within_their_caps_pay_automatically`, `a_replaced_grant_keeps_its_requests_and_never_refills_a_window` in `crates/coder-access`; `a_trusted_payee_is_paid_without_a_tap_within_its_ceilings`, `a_fee_above_the_ceiling_or_no_wallet_sends_an_automatic_payment_to_the_sheet`, `renewing_a_standing_grant_keeps_its_trusted_payees` in `crates/openagents-mobile`; `a_standing_grant_is_held_and_asked_under_as_a_request_grant_is` in `crates/coder-host` |
| A computer wakes the phone for a new spend request with a spend wake (`openagents.spend-wake.v1`): a host-signed private artifact to the device whose grant the request draws on, on a mailbox only the two keys derive, carrying no request, amount, payee, or task, and granting nothing. Each request wakes its phone once, and only a device that still holds `operate`. The app registers a push lease only in a build configured for push, and reads every computer's requests at once when it comes to the foreground. | New on 2026-09-28 ([#9864](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/9864)). | `a_spend_wake_names_only_the_pair_and_opens_only_for_its_device` and `a_new_spend_request_wakes_the_phone_whose_grant_it_draws_on` in `crates/coder-host`; `coming_to_the_foreground_reads_requests_without_waiting` and `push_wakes_are_off_unless_the_build_names_a_relay_and_gateway` in `crates/openagents-mobile` |
| `spend.list` and `spend.settle` travel over the CAP/CJ binding with the same admission and answers as the artifact binding: the sender's own grant naming this host, `operate`, and a receipt only for a request the host recorded. | New on 2026-09-28 ([#9864](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/9864)). | `spend_lists_and_receipts_travel_over_cj_as_over_the_artifact_binding` in `crates/coder-host`; `checked_in_manifest_is_the_one_a_host_builds_and_validates` in `crates/coder-access` |
| Tests never move the owner's funds: every spend test pays through a fake wallet, and the only live check creates a request and denies it. | New on 2026-09-28. | The fakes in `crates/openagents-mobile/src/spend/tests.rs`; no live test approves |
