| Each OpenAgents Nostr kind has exactly one owning specification under `nips/openagents`, listed once in `crates/nostr/src/kinds.rs` and the Kind registry in `nips/openagents/README.md`; no claim takes an official or Block NIP kind. | New on 2026-09-28 ([#9900](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/9900)), after NIP-EVAL and NIP-XP both claimed `3195`; the playtest report moved to `3197` before any was published. | `no_kind_is_claimed_twice`, `the_readme_registry_lists_exactly_the_registry`, `every_kind_a_nip_declares_is_registered_to_it_or_names_its_owner`, `no_claim_takes_an_official_or_block_kind` in `crates/nostr` |

## Playtest reports and playtest logging

| Invariant | Status | Checked by |
| --- | --- | --- |
| A playtest report's text travels only as a NIP-17 gift wrap (NIP-44) addressed to the OpenAgents triage key, signed by the tester's Verse world key; no readable report text or device is on the wire, and no other key can open it. Until a build carries the triage key (`playtest::TRIAGE_KEY`), reports wait on the phone and are sent nowhere. | New on 2026-09-28 ([#9882](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/9882)); reinterpreted the same day ([#9904](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/9904)): the build and tester key are now public in the report's NIP-XP record, below. | `a_report_seals_to_the_triage_key_and_only_it_opens_it` in `crates/playtest`; `a_report_is_sealed_to_the_triage_key_and_listed_with_its_code`, `without_the_triage_key_a_report_waits_on_the_phone_and_sends_later` in `crates/openagents-mobile` |
| After its private report is accepted by a relay, and only then, the app publishes the report's public NIP-XP playtest record (kind `3197`), signed by the same world key: the build, the platform, the report kind, and the SHA-256 of the private report's exact content, never its text, device, screenshot, session log, or task. The form says so before sending. Without the triage key nothing is published. | New on 2026-09-28 ([#9904](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/9904)). | `the_public_record_commits_to_the_opened_report_and_holds_no_text` in `crates/playtest`; `a_sent_report_publishes_its_content_free_record_signed_by_the_world_key`, `without_the_triage_key_no_public_record_is_published` in `crates/openagents-mobile` |
| No screenshot is taken or sent from the Wallet tab or a screen that can show a key (Identity keys, Trainer), whatever the host sends. | New on 2026-09-28 ([#9882](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/9882)). | `no_screenshot_leaves_the_wallet_or_a_key_screen`, `wallet_and_key_screens_are_sensitive` in `crates/playtest`; `no_screenshot_is_taken_from_the_wallet_or_a_key_screen` in `crates/openagents-mobile` |
| A chat's text leaves the phone for evaluation only inside a playtest report, sealed to the triage key like any report, never in its public record, and only by the tester's explicit choice: **Share this chat** on Report a problem is off by default, shows the whole chat first, and attaches it only from the Chat tab and only when its digest equals the previewed one; **Wrong answer** under a prepared answer shows what it sends and, only after **Send**, files a report with that question, the prepared answer (`id@version`), and the worker's judgment, nothing else of the chat. A chat with an `nsec` in it is refused. | New on 2026-09-28 ([#9921](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/9921)), the owner's decision on the router design's evaluation data. | `a_shared_chat_rides_only_inside_the_sealed_report`, `a_long_shared_chat_keeps_its_newest_messages_to_fit` in `crates/playtest`; `a_shared_chat_leaves_only_as_previewed`, `a_wrong_answer_report_carries_the_exchange`, `a_prepared_answer_offers_followups_and_a_wrong_answer_report` in `crates/openagents-mobile` |
| The playtest log holds only closed structural values (time, tab, screen, event code): it has no field for message text, prompts, transcripts, keys, recovery words, invoices, addresses, amounts, balances, or other players' keys, and refuses unknown fields and names. | New on 2026-09-28 ([#9883](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/9883)); renamed from the session log the same day. | `the_session_log_holds_only_closed_structural_values` in `crates/playtest` |
| **Playtest logging** is on for every tester, with no switch in the app, in every build unless the build was made with `OPENAGENTS_PLAYTEST_LOGGING=off` (release mode); an install that had the old opt-in **Playtest session** off records after the update. A build with logging off records nothing and deletes any log already on the phone. Either way the log stays on the device and leaves it only inside a report whose preview showed the whole log: the report attaches it only when the tester ticks it and its digest equals the previewed one. The Playtest screen says in one line whether logging is on in this build. | New on 2026-09-28 ([#9883](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/9883)) as opt-in; relaxed on 2026-09-28 by the owner: on by default for everyone, off only by the release-mode build switch. | `nothing_is_recorded_while_the_session_is_off` in `crates/playtest`; `playtest_logging_is_on_by_default_and_the_log_leaves_only_as_previewed`, `an_install_that_had_the_session_off_logs_after_the_update`, `a_build_with_playtest_logging_off_records_nothing_and_deletes_the_log`, `the_build_switch_turns_playtest_logging_off_only_when_set_to_off` in `crates/openagents-mobile` |

## Verse shared bodies

| Invariant | Status | Checked by |
| --- | --- | --- |
| A client applies a shared body's report or rest pose only when its stamp (epoch, then revision, then owner key) outranks the one it holds, or, for the same stamp, when it ends the episode last reported. Every client therefore converges on one owner and one rest pose per body, and a reset's higher epoch outranks every earlier claim, online or stored. | New on 2026-09-28. | `stamps_order_by_epoch_then_revision_then_owner`, `the_last_toucher_takes_the_ball_and_the_old_owner_yields`, `simultaneous_claims_resolve_to_the_same_owner_everywhere`, `a_reset_outranks_every_older_stamp_and_reaches_everyone`, `a_late_joiner_finds_every_rest_pose_from_the_snapshots` in `crates/verse`; `bare_world_players_share_the_ball_its_rest_and_the_reset` in `crates/coder-mobile` |
| A bare-world presence session publishes at most `EVENT_BUDGET` (54) durable events in any minute; pose frames go on the relay's per-second pose lane at the moving cadence, uncounted by that budget (2026-10-06, #10589). | New on 2026-09-28. | `a_moving_presence_player_keeps_five_frames_a_second_for_minutes` and `a_bare_world_player_stays_within_the_event_budget_while_playing` in `crates/verse`; `bare_world_players_share_the_ball_its_rest_and_the_reset` in `crates/coder-mobile` |
| Shared-body reports outside the world's walls, below its floor, faster than 60 m/s or 60 rad/s, of unknown bodies, or of another body set change nothing; body reports never reach the crowd of drawn players. | New on 2026-09-28. | `implausible_reports_change_nothing`, `body_reports_reach_the_inbox_and_never_the_crowd`, `a_body_snapshot_round_trips_and_is_checked`, `only_a_shared_body_carries_a_stamp` |
| A rigid-body step's result never depends on the machine's threads: detection and small islands split over threads give the one-thread result bit for bit, and an island of 1,024 or more contacts without joints solves by four slabs in an order fixed by the island alone (#10937). | New on 2026-10-09. | `a_step_split_over_threads_is_the_step_on_one_bit_for_bit` in `crates/physics` |
| Stamps order claims; they do not authorize them. Any participant may claim any shared body, so no shared body may decide combat, trade, scores, or anything else with stakes without a separate validating authority. | Model boundary, new on 2026-09-28. | Documented in `nips/openagents/NIP-MV.md#shared-bodies` |

## Published benchmark results

| Invariant | Status | Checked by |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Every number in the Gym's published leaderboard is computed by `gym-leaderboard` from a committed evidence file whose SHA-256 the board records; a source adapter refuses to build when its recomputed verdicts or tallies disagree with what the study recorded. A study that is only a descriptor and rows (`openagents.gym.study.v1`, `openagents.gym.attempt-row.v1`) gets the same cross-checks, and every number in its headline and caveats is a placeholder the code fills. | New on 2026-09-28 ([#9840](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/9840)). | `the_delegate_board_says_what_the_9776_report_says`, `the_tb21_board_says_what_the_essay_says`, `a_recorded_verdict_the_numbers_dont_support_refuses_to_build`, `tallies_that_disagree_with_the_rows_refuse_to_build`, `the_development_board_says_what_the_9746_report_says`, `a_development_row_whose_verdict_the_numbers_dont_support_refuses_to_build`, `the_shared_fact_board_says_what_the_showcase_and_the_gym_say`, `a_study_table_the_numbers_dont_support_refuses_to_build`, `reference_boards_are_labeled_snapshots_and_never_merged`, `a_study_that_names_an_unknown_rule_or_disagrees_with_its_rows_refuses` in `crates/gym-leaderboard` |
| An attempt whose cost is unknown never counts as a beat, and a beat whose knowledge was written from the same task carries `in_sample`. | New on 2026-09-28. | `the_delegate_board_says_what_the_9776_report_says` |
| A trace bundle reads only retained files whose digests match `retention.json` (or, for a Microcoder run record, its host directory's `MANIFEST.json`), is scrubbed of credential shapes and personal paths, and fits 256 KiB; a bundle that matched a credential rule fails `gym-leaderboard check` unless a person recorded the inspected match in `bench/terminal-bench/leaderboard-reviewed-redactions.json` with the retained file's SHA-256, and that review lapses when the file or the match count changes. The committed publication is byte-identical to regeneration from the evidence. | New on 2026-09-28; the manifest check and reviewed matches added the same day ([#9841](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/9841)). | `a_trace_that_changed_after_retention_refuses_to_bundle`, `a_record_that_differs_from_its_manifest_refuses_to_bundle`, `a_planted_credential_is_redacted_and_fails_the_check`, `a_review_holds_only_while_its_source_is_unchanged`, `every_bundle_fits_its_bound_and_matched_no_credential_rule`, `the_committed_publication_matches_the_evidence` |
| The Gym results client accepts a leaderboard only when the digest recomputed over the served JSON equals both the file's own and the index's, and a bundle only when its SHA-256 equals its `TraceRef`; a refused or unreachable publication leaves the cached one in place, responses are capped while read, and requests carry no credential, cookie, or `User-Agent`. | New on 2026-09-28 ([#9846](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/9846)). | `a_changed_byte_or_an_unexpected_digest_refuses`, `a_leaderboard_that_doesnt_match_the_index_is_refused_and_the_cache_kept`, `an_oversize_body_is_refused_while_reading`, `offline_shows_the_cache_or_says_it_needs_a_connection`, `requests_carry_no_credential_cookie_or_identity` in `crates/gym-leaderboard` |

See [the Gym leaderboard spec](docs/verse/gym-leaderboard.md).

## The Gym in the Verse

The Grid Gym's EVALS board and the notes trainers' agents trade there
([#9942](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/9942)).

| Invariant | Status | Checked by |
| --- | --- | --- |
| The EVALS board shows only extension eval results (`3189`, `oa:ext-eval:v1`) that pass `eval_ext::parse_publication` on the phone, grouped by the test set's release and never pooled across test sets. A check counts on the result it checks only when `eval_ext::linkage` confirms or disputes it; any other publication is its own row. Credit is the trainer's XP from `eval-check` and `eval-adopt` awards the XP reader counted. The real app reads `relay.openagents.com` and shows no fixture; labeled fixtures reach only a `ws://` relay on this machine, in tests and debug simulator checks. | New on 2026-09-29 ([#9942](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/9942)). | `results_group_by_test_set_with_names_checks_and_credit`, `forged_and_foreign_events_never_reach_the_board`, `names_fall_back_to_the_references_without_releases` in `crates/verse`; `the_evals_board_reads_inside_and_the_agents_compare_notes` in `crates/coder-mobile` |
| A player's agent speaks in the Gym only while the player stands in it with **Compare notes** switched on (off by default), and another player stands there too. A note cites at most one result, the author's own; carries only public event IDs and text computed from them, never chat content, files, or keys; and is at most 500 characters. An agent opens at most once in 15 minutes of silence, answers an opening note once and only while it's under 10 minutes old, answers the same trainer at most once in 15 minutes, never answers an answer, and sends at most four notes an hour, counting its own notes on the relay across restarts. | New on 2026-09-29 ([#9942](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/9942)). | `two_agents_exchange_one_opener_and_one_answer_then_wait`, `an_agent_is_quiet_when_off_alone_or_without_results`, `the_hourly_cap_and_restarts_hold`, `two_agents_in_the_gym_compare_notes_over_the_relay` in `crates/verse`; `the_evals_board_reads_inside_and_the_agents_compare_notes` in `crates/coder-mobile` |
| A reader never shows a note's own text. It shows a note only when every result it cites is a valid result by the note's author (and, for an answer, the quoted note is an opening note whose result is its author's, on the same test set), and it renders the text itself from those results. A note that cites anything else is held back. | New on 2026-09-29 ([#9942](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/9942)). | `readers_render_their_own_text_from_verified_results`, `ungrounded_notes_are_held_back`, `two_agents_in_the_gym_compare_notes_over_the_relay` in `crates/verse` |

## Product knowledge base

The chat router's `product.kb` route answers from `knowledge/openagents/`
([#9923](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/9923),
`crates/knowledge/src/product.rs`, `crates/coder/src/product_kb.rs`). See
[the chat router design](docs/coder/design/2026-09-28-chat-router.md#knowledge-routes-product-and-codebase)
and [the measurement](docs/coder/measurements/2026-09-28-product-kb.md).

| Invariant | Status | Checked by |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Only `admitted` entries of kind `product` under `knowledge/openagents/` are served. Every entry cites at least one file that exists in this public repository, by a relative path that is not a URL, not absolute, does not leave the repository, and names no private repository; its `answer` and body speak as "we", never in the first person singular (code spans and bold interface labels aside); its `answer` is at most 600 characters. A corpus with a problem does not load. | New on 2026-09-28. | `the_committed_corpus_is_sourced_public_and_plural`, `every_cite_is_an_existing_public_repository_path`, `answers_speak_in_the_plural_and_stay_short`, `an_entry_must_be_a_product_under_the_prefix` in `crates/knowledge` |
| Retrieval is typed. Candidates are the entries nearest the latest message by embedding cosine similarity, with no word-matching fallback: without embeddings the lookup fails and the router answers as if there were no knowledge base. What is kept is Jev's `relevant_N` Noul at or above the router's relevance floor; a passage carries its entry's reviewed answer only when Jev's `answer` Choice picked that entry at `KB_ANSWER_CONFIDENCE` or more, so the router's T0 gate needs both judgments. | New on 2026-09-28. | `a_sure_entry_carries_its_reviewed_answer`, `an_unsure_pick_grounds_the_model_instead_of_answering`, `nothing_relevant_keeps_nothing`, `the_questions_ask_relevance_for_each_candidate_and_one_answer_choice` in `crates/coder` |
| A lookup sends the latest message to the embedding provider, which the seam names in `recipients`, and sends Jev only the latest message, at most four earlier turns of at most 400 characters each, and the candidate entries. A failed lookup's error names no message text. | New on 2026-09-28. | `the_state_carries_the_latest_message_earlier_turns_and_the_candidates`, `a_failed_judge_is_a_failure_that_names_no_message_text`, `a_sure_entry_carries_its_reviewed_answer` in `crates/coder` |
| A grounded reply's instructions carry only the kept entries, with the rule to answer from them alone, cite each by id, and say when they do not answer; with no kept entry they are the no-documented-answer note. A reply's citations are read against the entries it was given, and an id it was not given is reported as invented. | New on 2026-09-28. | `instructions_hold_only_the_kept_entries_and_the_citation_rule`, `a_reply_cites_only_the_entries_it_was_given` in `crates/knowledge`; `a_grounded_reply_is_checked_against_the_passages_it_was_given` in `crates/coder` |

## Gym knowledge and eval routes

The chat router's `gym.news` and `eval.*` routes answer from the Gym's
verified records ([#9936](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/9936),
`crates/coder/src/gym_kb.rs`, `crates/coder/src/router/gym.rs`,
`crates/coder/src/router/card.rs`). See
[the chat router design](docs/coder/design/2026-09-28-chat-router.md#gym-and-eval-routes-2026-09-29)
and [the measurement](docs/coder/measurements/2026-09-29-chat-router-v2.md).

| Invariant | Status | Checked by |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Only verified records reach the Gym corpus. A published result or check is admitted only when `nostr::eval_ext::parse_publication` accepts its signed `3189` (signature, both markers, the inline report against its digest and `x` tag, the report under the ext-eval profile); a check counts toward a result only as `nostr::eval_ext::linkage` reads the two publications (a confirming or disputing check by a different trainer on the same suite, subject, and lock). Test sets are read from their releases only through a reader that checks the release: the starter test sets are the `3184` releases signed by `STARTER_PUBLISHERS` (the hosted runner's key, which the starter quests name as the suites' publisher), admitted only when `nostr::ext::parse_record` accepts the signature and marker, `nostr::eval_ext::parse_release` the manifest's bytes against the release's digest, and `nostr::eval_ext::check_suite_package` the suite and case manifest against the manifest; each file is fetched by its digest from the runner's public bucket and refused unless its bytes hash to it. The package `<signer>:<slug>-tests` is the catalog tool with that slug, named in an offer by the starter catalog's reference, and its size is the case manifest's count. A test set is also read from the verified results that ran it; adoptions are not read yet. The app's builds come from the compiled-in `CHANGELOG`, and the tool catalog and Gym notes from the checked product corpus. | New on 2026-09-29; starter releases read on 2026-09-29 (#9943). | `an_unverified_or_digest_mismatched_publication_never_reaches_the_corpus`, `checks_are_counted_as_the_profile_links_them`, `the_changelog_reads_as_builds`, `the_catalog_and_notes_come_from_tagged_product_notes`, `a_starter_release_reads_as_its_tools_test_set`, `a_release_that_does_not_check_is_refused`, `the_starter_publishers_are_the_quests` in `crates/coder` (`gym_kb`) |
| Numbers are real. Every card is built from records the Gym seam verified, and every number in it (tests passed without and with the tool, totals, checks, XP) is a field of one of them, copied; every card body is written and checked by NIP-CJ's own writer (`nostr::cj_conversation::card_feedback`), and one it refuses is not sent. A bank line's `{tool}` and `{tests}` come from the same records. With no record, the reply is a bank line that says so, or the model told it has no records (`router::gym::NO_RECORDS_NOTE`), never a number. | New on 2026-09-29 (the wireframe's `CHK-13`). | `a_cards_numbers_equal_its_records` (`router::card`), `an_items_numbers_are_its_records`, `a_tool_with_a_test_set_is_offered_to_run` (`router::gym`), `a_tool_test_is_offered_from_the_records_with_its_card`, `without_records_a_check_turn_says_nothing_is_waiting` (`coder-worker`) in `crates/coder` |
| A `gym.news` reply is grounded: its first words are the bank's `gym.news.lead` line, sent with the news card as soon as the items are kept and before any model text (#9950), and the model is restarted with only the kept items, told to answer from them alone, in the app's plain words and none of the banned ones, and cite each by its `gym:` id, and the news card lists those items with their sources (an event or a repository path). A reply's citations are read against the items it was given, and an id it was not given is reported as invented. The phone never sees an id: `router::gym::Tidy` takes the citations out as the reply streams and from its result, and the worker logs the post-check (`router::gym::post_check`: banned words and raw ids) as counts. Every news item's title and line, from the catalog, the Gym notes, the changelog, and each kind of record, carries no banned word (the wireframe's list, plurals included) and no raw id (`CHK-02`). Nothing relevant: the bank's `gym.news_empty`, no card. The grounded model runs on the news lane (`router::gym::NEWS_MODEL`, Gemini 2.5 Flash, reasoning off, at most `NEWS_MAX_TOKENS`) through the chat door's own gateway and key, so it adds no service a message reaches; since #10109 a worker with a primary asks the primary first and the news lane takes any turn it does not answer; the privacy answer names it beside the chat model, and `CODER_GYM_NEWS_MODEL=off` keeps news on the chat model. | New on 2026-09-29; ids and banned words kept off the phone on 2026-09-29 (#9944); lead line and news lane on 2026-09-29 (#9950). | `a_grounded_reply_may_cite_only_its_items`, `news_is_the_kept_items`, `a_news_reply_shows_no_ids_and_no_banned_words`, `every_news_items_words_are_the_phones` (`router::gym`), `news_is_grounded_in_the_gyms_records`, `the_news_lane_is_named_where_it_is_used` (`coder-worker`) in `crates/coder` |
| News retrieval is typed. Candidates are the items nearest the latest message by embedding cosine similarity and the newest dated records (a record's time, not a word); what is kept is Jev's `relevant_N` Noul at or above the router's relevance floor, at most five. Without embeddings or the judge the lookup fails and the router answers as if there were no records. A lookup sends the embedding provider the latest message (redacted, as for the product knowledge base, and named in `recipients`), and Jev the latest message, at most four earlier turns of at most 400 characters, and the candidates' record texts. The worker's relay read asks only for public `3189` publications with the `oa:ext-eval:v1` marker and the `3184` releases of the starter publishers; the bucket read fetches public files by digest and sends nothing of a conversation. | New on 2026-09-29. | `news_is_what_jev_keeps_from_the_candidates`, `the_questions_ask_relevance_for_each_candidate`, `the_relay_filter_names_the_profile` in `crates/coder` (`gym_kb`) |
| The authoring interview's words come from its seam, checked, or from the bank; never from the chat model, whose call an `eval.author` turn drops. A step is shown only when its text is non-empty, at most 1,200 characters, and plural; its draft only when NIP-CJ's `parse_draft` accepts it; its offer only when it is `start_eval` on the draft, `publish_eval`, `run_coder` (the handoff when a tool needs new code), or opening the test set or Add to the Gym. A request's `draft` and `tried` (a try's result, read by `router::card::tried` exactly or dropped) are data for the seam, never instructions, and only its presence lets a short reply continue the interview; a refusal still comes first. Without the interview's door and judge, `eval.author` answers with `eval.author.soon`. | New on 2026-09-29. Reinterpreted on 2026-10-02 ([#10177](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/10177)): a plugin being made on a computer also continues, recovered from our last message's fixed step line (`router::policy` rule 0b), and its steps are fixed lines, never the model's words. | `an_interview_step_is_checked_before_it_is_shown` (`router::gym`), `an_open_draft_continues_the_interview` (`router::policy`), `a_draft_is_what_the_nip_cj_parser_accepts`, `a_tried_result_is_read_exactly_or_dropped` (`router::card`), `the_interview_is_the_seams_or_the_banks_never_the_models` (`coder-worker`) in `crates/coder` |

## Chat router as a capability claim

The chat router is a decision service whose evaluation is an evidence
record on a claim key, judged by a written policy
([#9959](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/9959),
`crates/coder/src/router_claim.rs`, `crates/coder/src/router/calibration.rs`,
`crates/gym/gates/router-v1.json`). See
[the essay](docs/essays/2026-09-29-test-time-capabilities.md#the-thesis-capability-is-something-you-can-acquire-at-test-time),
[NIP-EVAL's decision-service record](nips/openagents/NIP-EVAL.md#reports),
and [the measurement](docs/coder/measurements/2026-09-29-chat-router-claims.md).

| Invariant | Status | Checked by |
| --- | --- | --- |
| The question set is named by its digest wherever it is named. A NIP-CJ `judgment`'s `set` is `chat-router-v2@<digest>` and its `bank` `chat-answers-v1@<digest>`; the worker logs both at start and in every `router` line, with the calibration map applied or `null`. The digest is SHA-256 of the canonical JSON of the `route` question, computed as the Gym digests a question set, so the committed `crates/gym/questions/chat-router-route-v3.json`, a Gym row, and a running worker name one question by one digest, and a changed route list or rubric is a changed set. | New on 2026-09-29. | `the_set_is_named_with_its_digest`, `the_shadow_record_never_carries_message_text` (`router::wire`) in `crates/coder`; `the_wire_names_the_question_set_by_the_gyms_digest` in `crates/coder/tests/router_suite.rs` |
| A published router eval is an `openagents.eval-report.v1` under NIP-EVAL's extension profile whose subject is the router as a decision service (`configuration` pins `chat-router-v2@<digest>` and `chat-answers-v1@<digest>`), whose suite `cases` is the committed Gym suite and whose partition is its locked rows; every artifact it cites is written beside it by digest, and the record is built from ids, labels, probabilities, tiers, and timings only: no message text. It carries per-route precision and recall, canned and dispatch precision, the abstention rate, ECE and Brier per question raw and calibrated, and the operating-point table, and it parses under `nostr::eval_ext::parse_report`. A record with no baseline is `inconclusive`, with the gate's floors written into its limitations. | New on 2026-09-29. | `the_record_is_a_publishable_report_whose_artifacts_are_beside_it`, `a_case_passes_on_the_route_the_tier_and_no_harm` (`router_claim`), `observations_and_operating_points_read_each_question_against_its_label` (`router_eval`) in `crates/coder`; the `decision-service` fixture in `crates/nostr/tests/eval_ext_fixtures.rs` |
| The Gym suite `chat-router-v2` names the `router-v1` gate, which declares its primary outcome (canned precision, higher) and the measures it holds non-inferior (route accuracy, canned recall, dispatch precision, ECE) inside its digest. The product floors (canned precision ≥ 0.98, dispatch precision ≥ 0.90) are judged with or without a baseline; against a baseline nothing may fall past `max_decrease`, and Better needs the primary outcome to rise past it. The gate reads a Gym door comparison as route accuracy and ECE. | New on 2026-09-29. | `the_router_gate_declares_its_primary_outcome_inside_the_digest`, `a_router_record_without_a_baseline_is_judged_on_its_floors_alone`, `a_router_is_better_only_past_the_margin_and_worse_on_any_measured_loss`, `the_router_gate_reads_a_door_comparison_as_routing_and_calibration` (`gate`), `every_committed_gate_loads_and_validates` in `crates/gym`; `crates/gym/tests/gate_digest.rs` over every shipped gate; `the_gym_suites_are_the_labeled_sets` in `crates/coder/tests/router_suite.rs` |
| Serving calibrated probabilities is the operator's call. The worker applies the committed maps only with `CODER_WORKER_ROUTER_CALIBRATION=on` (off and unset serve raw probabilities), refuses to start with a map fitted for another question set than it asks, and a map rescales only the `route` and `answer` probabilities: never the argmax, the runner-up, or any other reading. The committed map is fitted on the calibration partition and scored on the held-out rows, with `probability-v2`'s verdict recorded beside it. | New on 2026-09-29. | `the_committed_record_is_this_builds`, `a_record_for_another_question_set_refuses`, `applying_the_map_moves_only_the_probabilities` (`router::calibration`) in `crates/coder` |

## Codebase knowledge

The chat router's `codebase.kb` route answers from an index of this public
repository at a pinned commit
([#9924](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/9924),
`crates/knowledge/src/codebase.rs`, `crates/coder/src/codebase.rs`). See
[the index's design](docs/coder/design/codebase-kb.md).

| Invariant | Status | Checked by |
| --- | --- | --- |
| The index holds only this public repository, read from Git objects at one commit (never a working tree): Markdown and Rust doc comments, without retained transcripts, benchmark evidence, upstream NIP copies, the coding knowledge base, fixtures, or vendored code. No private repository is ever indexed. Every chunk keeps its path and line range at that commit, and the index names the commit. | New on 2026-09-28. | `only_first_party_docs_and_sources_are_read`, `markdown_is_chunked_by_heading_with_lines_and_titles`, `doc_comments_are_chunked_with_the_item_they_document`, `an_index_round_trips_through_its_file` in `crates/knowledge` |
| Retrieval is typed: candidates are the chunks nearest the latest message by cosine similarity, with no word-matching fallback; what is kept is Jev's `relevant_N` Noul at 0.5 or more, and a question goes to Coder (the grounding's `needs_dispatch`) when Jev's `needs_live` Noul is at least 0.6, when it did not answer, or when nothing is kept. The seam names its embedding provider in `recipients`. | New on 2026-09-28. | `the_judge_keeps_relevant_excerpts_and_a_missing_reading_escalates`, `passages_are_named_by_their_line_range_at_the_commit` in `crates/coder` |
| A codebase answer cites `path:line` ranges; a citation is listed as a source only when it lies inside an excerpt the model was shown, and the sources line names the index's commit. | New on 2026-09-28. | `only_citations_inside_the_shown_excerpts_become_sources`, `an_answer_with_no_valid_citation_lists_its_excerpts`, `citations_are_read_from_prose_and_brackets` in `crates/coder` |

## Eval authoring interview

The authoring interview drafts a test set with a person, in chat (the
`eval.author` route) and in a terminal (`openagents ext eval init`)
([#9937](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/9937),
`crates/ext-eval/src/author/`, `crates/coder/src/eval_author.rs`,
`crates/openagents-cli/src/ext_eval_init.rs`). See
[Authoring a suite](docs/extensions/evaluation.md#authoring-a-suite).

| Invariant | Status | Checked by |
| --- | --- | --- |
| The steps and gates are a typed machine the model can't move: only an explicit approval at a gate passes it (a tap on **Looks good** that Jev reads as `approve` at 0.8 or more, or `y` in a terminal), anything else at a gate is a change request that asks the step again, and a proposal is applied only to the step the machine asked for. A chat turn recovers its step from our own last message's fixed gate line (an exact comparison against the machine's closed set of lines) and the resent draft; a line the draft can't be at falls back to the step the draft implies, so a gate may be asked again but never skipped. No route, pick, or approval reads a message by keyword. | New on 2026-09-29. | `every_gate_refuses_to_advance_without_an_explicit_approval`, `a_proposal_never_moves_past_a_gate_and_approvals_need_a_gate`, `resume_recovers_the_step_from_our_line_and_never_skips_a_gate`, `the_floor_holds_after_any_sequence_of_proposals`, `every_line_is_recovered_exactly_and_lines_are_distinct` in `crates/ext-eval`; `a_chat_walks_the_whole_interview_one_gate_at_a_time` in `crates/coder`; `the_terminal_walks_every_gate_and_never_writes_into_the_extension` in `crates/openagents-cli` |
| A tool made in chat is a skill: at the start, Jev's typed `build` question (structured `{question, context, focus}` instructions and a `{what, not_for, examples}` rubric for `skill` and `code`, `coder::eval_author::rubric`) sends a tool to Coder on a computer only when it chooses `code` with 0.7 or more; anything else stays in the interview and reaches a draft, and its gates still pass only on a tap. No rubric example is a request the live check measures. | New on 2026-09-29 ([#9945](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/9945)). Reinterpreted on 2026-10-02 ([#10177](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/10177)): this holds in the phone's and the desktop app's chat; in a terminal on the computer Coder runs on, a new plugin, skill or code, is made by the plugin-creation flow below. | `a_skill_shaped_tool_stays_in_the_interview_and_reaches_a_draft`, `making_a_tool_is_a_skill_and_code_goes_to_coder`, `every_option_is_a_rubric_and_no_example_is_a_live_request` in `crates/coder`; live: `live_make_a_tool_requests_are_skills_unless_they_need_code`, `the_issue_requests_make_a_skill_and_a_test_set_live` in `crates/coder/tests/eval_author_live.rs` |
| Every draft the interview returns holds the floor, whatever the model proposed: each test parses as a case the runner accepts with no `TODO` line; no test prompt names the tool, its declared operations, or a catalog tool it turns on (such a test is left out, never rewritten); each test has a scored check of its outcome (added when missing); every test runs at least three times per arm; at least one test is one where the tool should stay out of the way (added when missing); at most 8 tests in chat and 16 in a terminal; the draft passes the `openagents.eval-draft.v1` parser. Every run offered compares with and without the tool. | New on 2026-09-29. | `the_floor_holds_after_any_sequence_of_proposals`, `the_floor_catches_each_violation`, `an_empty_draft_gets_a_quiet_test_and_every_test_an_outcome` in `crates/ext-eval`; `a_draft_from_the_phone_is_data_and_the_floor_still_holds` in `crates/coder` |
| The interview runs, spends, and publishes nothing by itself: a try, the full run, and **Add to the Gym** are NIP-CJ offers the phone renders as taps (a terminal asks `y`), and numbers in a reply come from a result record. It reads the tool and never writes into it: a try runs from a temporary copy, and the only write is the finished tests, at the last step, to the eval directory or `--out`, never overwriting a test. A draft's tests write out and read back byte-for-byte. | New on 2026-09-29. | `a_chat_walks_the_whole_interview_one_gate_at_a_time` in `crates/coder`; `the_terminal_walks_every_gate_and_never_writes_into_the_extension`, `a_read_only_extension_without_out_writes_nothing_and_says_why`, `answers_that_end_early_write_nothing` in `crates/openagents-cli`; `a_draft_writes_out_and_reads_back_byte_for_byte_and_the_engine_loads_it` in `crates/ext-eval` |

## Making a plugin on a computer

When the chat is a terminal on the computer Coder runs on, a request for
a new plugin is made through typed steps: what it should and shouldn't do, Coder's
draft, its tests for approval, a run of them, and publishing and turning it
on ([#10177](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/10177),
`crates/openagents-chat/src/plugin_flow.rs`,
`crates/coder/src/eval_author/plugin.rs`).

| Invariant | Status | Checked by |
| --- | --- | --- |
| The flow starts only from typed readings: the `eval.author` route, then Jev's `tool` reading `make` on a turn from a terminal on the computer Coder runs on, and its `scope` reading (`stated` at 0.8 or more skips the question). Each later step is recovered from our own last message's fixed step line (an exact comparison against `plugin_flow::Step::line`), the chat's Coder run (its typed ending and the bounded paths it changed), and Jev's typed `reply` and `publish` readings (an approval at 0.8, a publish choice at 0.7, else the step is asked again); no step reads the person's words by keyword. The worker's reply carries its step as the result's typed `plugin` field, which the client reads exactly or drops. | New on 2026-10-02. | `a_request_for_a_new_plugin_starts_the_flow_on_a_computer`, `each_step_moves_on_a_typed_outcome_or_reading` (`eval_author`), `an_open_plugin_flow_continues_through_work_and_commands` (`router::policy`), `a_plugin_step_rides_the_result_and_an_open_flow_continues` (`coder-worker`) in `crates/coder`; `every_line_is_recovered_exactly_and_lines_are_distinct`, `the_wire_field_is_typed_and_bounded`, `a_draft_is_read_from_the_paths_coder_changed` in `crates/openagents-chat` |
| Nothing runs, publishes, installs, or turns on without the person's answer to the step that names it: Coder drafts only after the request (or the scope answer) and never installs, enables, publishes, or runs the plugin (its brief says so); the tests run on this computer only after a `reply` reading approved them; publishing, installing, and turning it on run only after a `publish` reading chose them, each as the `openagents` command for that step, and publishing runs only when this build's own command tree has `plugin publish` (otherwise the reply says how to publish it later). Everything runs in the Coder task's own worktree, never the person's checkout. | New on 2026-10-02. | `a_plugin_is_drafted_tested_and_turned_on_through_typed_steps`, `this_computer_advances_a_step_only_on_its_outcome` in `crates/openagents-chat` |
| A package record need not name a program or background rules; resolving it (`Package::resolve`) refuses one that carries neither and has no skill (`skills/*.md`), so a skills-only plugin installs and tests and an empty one never does. | New on 2026-10-02 ([#10177](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/10177)): before, a record needed a program or background rules. | `a_skills_only_package_resolves_and_an_empty_one_is_refused` in `crates/coder` (`package`) |
| A background rule made from words (the chat's `standing.rule` route, `/background WORDS`, `openagents background add` or `edit` with `--message`) is compiled once into a typed rule by Jev choices over the host's catalogs (no keyword matching; each choice acted on at `background.compile` 0.6 or more, else one clarifying question) and bounded fields code reads afterward, kept as a draft, and saved only when the person confirms it (`background apply`); an edit applies only to the rule as it was when drafted, and a draft older than a day is refused. Such a rule only uses the host's built-in triggers, conditions, and actions (delete from the candidate classes inside the host's own roots, notify, fast-forward a clean checkout that has nothing its upstream lacks); there is no command or shell action, and a plugin's rule may never update a checkout. It is on once confirmed, and an edit from words turns an off rule on (the card shows it); only plugin rules start off. | New on 2026-10-02 ([#10157](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/10157)). Model boundary: Jev's readings are not verified beyond the threshold; the card's summary and dry run are what the person confirms. | `an_unsure_reading_asks_instead_of_guessing`, `a_draft_is_saved_only_when_applied`, `an_edit_drafted_against_an_older_rule_is_refused`, `a_conversation_rule_cleans_only_where_the_host_does`, `a_checkout_is_fast_forwarded_only_when_clean_and_not_ahead` in `crates/background` (`phase2_tests`); `a_standing_rule_is_drafted_here_and_saved_only_on_confirm` in `crates/openagents-chat` |

## The Gym in chat on the phone

The phone's side of the chat-first eval loop
([#9939](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/9939), wireframe
revision 3): cards and sheets in chat, runs, Add to the Gym, credit, the Gym
menu, and the opt-in Gym intro. The code is
`crates/openagents-mobile/src/gym.rs`, `eval_cards.rs`, and `first_run.rs`.

| Invariant | Status | Checked by |
| --- | --- | --- |
| A card is drawn only from a `card` feedback body that `nostr::cj_conversation::parse_card` accepts, and a `start_eval` or `publish_eval` offer only when `parse_offer` accepts it, from the chat worker's own signed answer to this device's request. The phone names every label itself and never shows a worker's `label`. Every number on a card, sheet, or the menu comes from a record: the worker's card, the report of a run this phone started (read with `nostr::eval_ext::parse_report`, each test's pass by the engine's majority rule over its `case.<id>.runs_passed` measurements), or the phone's own XP ledger; a value the phone hasn't read is left out (the menu draws a gray bar), never a zero or a guess. The worker's `credit` card is not drawn: `CARD-07` comes from the phone's ledger. The playtest card on Account > Playtest is drawn only once the playtest referee's awards are read, never as zeros while its key is unpublished. The recorded Gym (`gym_fixture`), the offline chat (`chat_fixture`), and the Computers fixture are compiled only into debug builds (`#[cfg(debug_assertions)]`), run only when the host asks, and keep their chats and runs in their own stores (`basic-chats-fixture`, `gym-fixture`), so their numbers never reach the real app; the iOS host compiles its fixture screen and copies `conversation.json` only into debug, simulator, and bench builds, and Android only into debug and bench. | New on 2026-09-29; fixtures compiled out of release and the playtest card gated on 2026-09-29 ([#9955](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/9955)). | `every_worker_card_renders_in_the_phones_words`, `a_report_reads_each_test_by_the_majority_of_its_runs`, `credit_comes_from_the_phones_own_ledger`, `the_menu_shows_no_number_it_hasnt_read`, `see_your_result_shows_the_phones_newest_result` in `crates/openagents-mobile`; release builds are checked for fixture strings (`docs/extensions/measurements/2026-09-29-build-23-launch-audit.md`), a model-boundary exception since a test build has debug assertions |
| Nothing runs, stops, or publishes without its tap. A Gym button does something only when its ID is one the last view minted, and what it does is the phone's own action, decided when the view was drawn. A run starts only from a card's button (one run per card at a time); stopping one asks first; **Add to the Gym** only opens `SCR-20`, which lists exactly what becomes public (the tests, the result's counts, and the trainer name) and says Coder's full work stays private; only its own button publishes. | New on 2026-09-29. | `only_a_cards_button_sends_a_request`, `a_hosted_run_is_followed_again_after_a_relaunch` in `crates/openagents-mobile` |
| The test-set draft is the person's: it is the chat's newest `draft` card, kept on the phone, sent back only as the chat worker request's `draft` (with `tried`, the result of the phone's newest run of that same draft), and public only through **Add to the Gym**. Every interview step moves on only on a tap: **Looks good** sends the approval with the draft, **Change it** puts "Change: " in the composer, and **Looks good** shows only on a reply that ends with one of the interview's own gate lines, compared exactly. | New on 2026-09-29. | `the_draft_round_trips_through_the_phone_and_each_step_is_a_tap`, `the_gate_lines_are_the_interviews` in `crates/openagents-mobile` |
| Where a run goes is the phone's decision from typed state, never the offer's `where`: the hosted runner (`nostr::eval_ext::hosted::RUNNER` on its relay) when the offer fits its bounds, with the `25920` built by `hosted::request_body` and `run_input` from the offer the card showed, signed by the trainer's world key so credit names the trainer, sent again (the same signed event) on each new connection so the runner answers what it recorded and never runs twice, each answer checked with `execution::bind_worker_event`, and the report read from the `3188` sealed to the trainer; else, for a published test set, Coder on a ready computer as a NIP-HOST task that runs `openagents ext eval run` and publishes nothing; checks and chat drafts wait for the hosted runner (`check` publishes as it ends, which would skip `SCR-20`); otherwise the card says why in plain words, with **Connect a computer** when one would help. A computer run's card never states a count it didn't read: it opens the Coder chat instead. | New on 2026-09-29. | `a_tap_sends_the_runners_own_request`, `refusals_read_plainly`, `the_desktop_runner_is_the_phones_with_its_own_wake` in `crates/openagents-chat-app/src/hosted.rs` (the runner both apps share since [#10060](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/10060)); `without_a_runner_the_card_says_why_it_cant_run`, `a_computer_run_follows_its_coder_task` in `crates/openagents-mobile`; `with_no_computer_a_test_runs_hosted_and_its_result_survives_a_reopen`, `a_hosted_run_still_going_is_followed_again_after_a_reopen` in `crates/openagents-desktop` |
| The trainer's world key reaches the Rust app through `gym_world` (on the desktop, from its own protected storage; see the Desktop Play row) and stays in its memory only: it names the trainer ("Trainer 7KQ", from the key's `npub`, never the key), reads their XP, and signs their hosted requests. No packet carries it. | New on 2026-09-29. | `a_trainer_is_named_not_keyed` in `crates/openagents-mobile` |
| Chat first. A new install opens the Chat tab on a chat with OpenAgents, with the tab bar, and the chat volunteers nothing of the Gym until the person opts in: no intro step, no card, no `start_eval` offer the person didn't ask for, and no menu (the `eval.*` routes still answer a question). **Profile** and the previous chats are in every chat's header. The opt-in is **Train Coder** (`gym_train`, from the Verse's Gym board or Account), kept in the phone's Gym state beside the intro's step; **Not now** on the intro's first step opts out again. | Chat first on 2026-09-29 ([#9958](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/9958)), replacing the guided first run of the same day. Relaxed on 2026-09-29 ([#9963](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/9963)), owner-directed: a new chat's suggested questions include Gym questions (What's new in the Gym?, Test a capability, Check a result) before the opt-in; each is only a question to send. | `a_fresh_install_opens_on_the_chat_with_nothing_of_the_gym` in `crates/openagents-mobile/src/gym/tests.rs`; `a_new_install_opens_on_the_chat_and_train_coder_opens_the_intro` in `first_run.rs` |
| Once opted in, the Gym intro is three taps to a test starting (**CHOOSE CODER**, **LET'S GO**, **START THE TEST**), keeps its furthest step across a relaunch and an opt-out, shows the Gym menu only after its first result (or **Skip for now** when the test can't start) and then only from the chat header's **Menu**, never at launch. No label on the menu, the intro, a card, or a sheet uses a word on the wireframe's banned list, and "tool" is on that list as an umbrella word: the word is "capability" ([#9957](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/9957)); a model's tool call in a transcript is the model's text. The chat router's Gym replies and news items keep the same list. | New on 2026-09-29; "tool" banned and the intro made opt-in the same day (#9958). | `the_gym_intro_starts_a_test_in_three_taps_and_resumes`, `no_label_uses_a_banned_word` in `crates/openagents-mobile`; `every_news_items_words_are_the_phones`, `a_news_reply_shows_no_ids_and_no_banned_words` in `crates/coder` |
| The phone's XP reader counts `eval-adopt` awards only with the `coder-defaults` manifest and admissions the release pins, fetched over HTTPS from the NIP-94 locators the relay holds and kept only when their bytes match the digest; without them the award is refused, as `xp_ledger::derive` refuses it. | New on 2026-09-29. | `an_adoption_release_asks_for_its_documents` in `crates/verse`; `an_adoption_credits_the_tool_author_the_suite_author_and_the_evaluator` in `crates/xp-ledger` |

## Extension evaluation

`openagents ext eval run`, `publish`, and `check`, and the runner the
hosted runner shares
([#9934](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/9934),
`crates/ext-eval/src/{run,sandbox,child,proxy,arms,trust,signal,live,replay,publish,check,blob}.rs`,
`crates/openagents-cli/src/ext_eval.rs`). See
[The run sandbox](docs/extensions/evaluation.md#the-run-sandbox) and
[Publishing and the Gym](docs/extensions/evaluation.md#publishing-and-the-gym).

| Invariant | Status | Checked by |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Every run's child runs inside `coder-boundary` (`sandbox-exec` on macOS, `bwrap` on Linux). Where the host has no backend, every run refuses as `unconfined_host` before any run directory is made or any child is spawned (the command refuses before it starts); nothing runs unconfined. Windows refuses the same way: its boundary is an AppContainer, which cannot reach the loopback door proxy. | New on 2026-09-29; the Windows refusal kept when the Windows boundary landed ([#9983](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/9983)). | `an_unconfined_host_refuses_every_run_and_spawns_nothing` in `crates/ext-eval` |
| The child starts from an empty environment: `HOME`, `OPENAGENTS_HOME`, `TMPDIR`, `TMP`, and `CODER_TRACE_DIR` point inside its run directory, it sees no `XDG_*` variable and no `CODER_*` variable of the operator's shell (only the harness's own), and a case's `run.env` key outside `OA_EVAL_*` fails the run as `env_var_rejected` before anything is spawned. | New on 2026-09-29. | `the_child_sees_nothing_of_the_operator`, `an_env_key_outside_oa_eval_is_rejected_before_spawning` in `crates/ext-eval` |
| The child reads only the system's program directories, its run directory, and the agent binary: a file in the operator's real `$HOME` is unreadable. It writes only `home/.openagents/`, `out/`, and `tmp/`, and its workspace only when the operator granted `write`. | New on 2026-09-29. | `the_child_sees_nothing_of_the_operator`, `the_workspace_is_writable_only_with_the_write_grant` in `crates/ext-eval` |
| The door key never reaches the child: the child's door is a per-run loopback proxy and a random token the proxy swaps for the key. The key appears nowhere under the results directory; tokens in the child's output and files are redacted, and a results directory that holds the key is removed and the run fails. | New on 2026-09-29. | `the_child_sees_nothing_of_the_operator` in `crates/ext-eval` |
| On macOS the child's network is the door proxy alone unless the operator granted `network`. On Linux, `bwrap`'s network namespace would hide the host's loopback proxy, so the network stays open there and the report records `open-door-proxied`; the key is still never in the child. | New on 2026-09-29; the Linux network is an explicit exception. | `the_network_is_the_door_alone_where_loopback_survives_offline` in `crates/ext-eval` |
| The subject arm admits the extension's program (and the Wasm guests it carries) under `CODER_PROGRAMS` with the effects the operator's grants allow, and its skills as digested guidance Coder refuses to run with when the bytes don't match; the baseline admits none of them. Both arms get the same agent binary and question sets, and each arm's run lock digests exactly what it admitted. | New on 2026-09-29. | `both_arms_run_and_the_gate_says_better_twice` in `crates/ext-eval`; `guidance_must_match_its_stated_digest`, `guidance_is_appended_to_the_instructions` in `crates/coder` |
| A run is one headless turn under the case's deadline: a passed deadline stops the child's process group as `timeout`. `SIGINT` and `SIGTERM` stop every live child (`SIGTERM`, a two-second grace, then `SIGKILL`), start nothing more, write a `partial` report, and exit 130 or 143; a second signal exits at once. | New on 2026-09-29. | `a_deadline_stops_the_child_as_a_timeout`, `stopping_a_run_stops_its_child_and_marks_it_cancelled` in `crates/ext-eval`; `sigint_stops_live_children_and_exits_130` in `crates/openagents-cli` |
| A directory target runs only when the operator owns it, others can't write it, and the operator trusted it (a yes on the terminal, remembered, or `--trust`); without a terminal an untrusted directory refuses. Trust grants nothing beyond `read`. | New on 2026-09-29. | `a_yes_is_remembered_and_a_no_refuses`, `a_directory_others_can_write_is_refused` in `crates/ext-eval`; `an_untrusted_directory_without_a_terminal_refuses` in `crates/openagents-cli` |
| Nothing leaves the machine until `publish`. `publish` signs the suite's NIP-EXT release only with the suite author's key and the `3189` only with the report's evaluator key, publishes each once (a second publish reuses both), and every event it sends parses with `nostr::eval_ext`. `check` verifies every fetched byte against the release manifest, refuses an extension whose package record or run lock differs from the result's, publishes only when the rerun's suite is byte-for-byte the published one, and reports confirm or dispute from the two signed events alone. | New on 2026-09-29. | `a_published_result_is_checked_and_confirmed_on_a_local_relay` in `crates/openagents-cli` (needs `NOSTR_RELAY_TEST_DATABASE_URL`) |
| A `module` step's guest call is recorded in the trajectory under the step's name with its operation and what it returned, and a `receipt` grader passes only when rerunning the same guest bytes on the run's workspace returns the recorded outcome; an output it can't read is `unverifiable`, never a pass. | New on 2026-09-29. | `a_module_step_records_its_guest_call` in `crates/coder`; `a_recorded_guest_call_replays_exactly_and_a_changed_one_fails` in `crates/ext-eval` |
| A `module` step reads only its granted scope: the paths its binding's `read` names, each `read_present` path that exists, and, under `read_named`, the workspace files the run's request names and the files a named saved log (`.log`, `.txt`, `.out`) names in turn, at most 16 and only those that exist inside the workspace (`plugin::scope::resolve`); a named path that leaves the workspace by `..` or a symlink is never added, and a `pure` step that names any of these is refused at admission. A step whose binding names `request` gets the run's request in that input field, held to 32 KiB with both ends kept, and `<field>_truncated` says whether it was cut. A run whose last answering `module` step returned a `markdown` string leads its reply, and the trace's final message, with that text (at most 16,000 characters), then the run's summary; a guest that returns none leaves the reply the summary. The replayer rebuilds the same scope and input from the trajectory's first user message, and a step that takes the request replays `unverifiable` when the trajectory holds none. | New on 2026-10-01 ([#10086](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/10086)), so a plugin's tool works on what the person asked about and its answer reaches them; it narrows nothing that existed and widens no step that doesn't name the new fields. | `a_module_step_takes_the_request_and_reads_what_it_names` in `crates/coder`; `paths_come_out_of_compiler_and_runtime_output`, `a_long_request_keeps_both_ends`, `the_scope_keeps_only_files_inside_the_workspace` in `crates/plugin`; `a_step_that_takes_the_request_replays_from_the_recorded_request` in `crates/ext-eval` |
| The verdict is the Gym gate `ext-eval-v2`: **Better** only when the subject arm passes more compared cases than the baseline, keeps every should-not-fire case, raises the mean score by more than the spread between repeats, and neither cost nor time per run rises past half again the baseline's plus the spread. Cost and time never make **Better** on their own; they are reported as separate notes (`Faster`, `Cheaper`, …). `ext-eval-v1` stays committed, with its digest unchanged, so older results keep their meaning, and a suite naming either gate parses. | New on 2026-09-29; replaces `ext-eval-v1`'s keep-on-time-or-cost rule. | `a_faster_run_that_passes_no_more_is_better_under_v1_and_not_under_v2`, `more_tests_passed_beyond_the_spread_is_better_under_v2`, `more_tests_passed_at_three_times_the_time_is_not_better_under_v2`, `the_increase_ceiling_identifies_v2_and_leaves_v1_as_it_was` in `crates/gym`; `a_time_only_improvement_is_no_clear_change_with_a_faster_note` in `crates/ext-eval`; `a_suite_parses_and_refuses_another_purpose_or_gate` in `crates/nostr` |

### The hosted runner

The hosted eval runner
([#9935](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/9935),
`crates/eval-runner`, `nostr::eval_ext::hosted`). See
[Where runs execute](docs/extensions/evaluation.md#where-runs-execute) and
[the runbook](docs/deployment/eval-runner.md).

| Invariant | Status | Checked by |
| --- | --- | --- |
| The runner answers only a request that opens: a valid NIP-01 signature, exactly one `p` naming the runner, execution v1, and `created_at` inside the freshness window. A request that doesn't open is dropped with a log line and no answer, because nothing proves who sent it. A retransmission gets the recorded answer and never a second run. | New on 2026-09-29. | `bad_requests_are_dropped_or_refused_and_the_quota_holds`, `a_hosted_run_is_streamed_sealed_and_published_only_on_request` in `crates/eval-runner` |
| It admits only its own `ext-eval` target and requirements, catalog tools (by the extension's DefinitionRef or a guest's), and chat-made tools built from a skill and catalog tools; at most 8 tests, 3 runs per arm, both arms, a 64 KiB draft; no test that asks for `exec` or `network`; a published suite only when it reproduces the release's suite digest byte for byte; and a check only of a published result of that suite. Anything else is refused `not_admitted` or `too_large` before anything is reserved or run. | New on 2026-09-29. | `bad_requests_are_dropped_or_refused_and_the_quota_holds`, `a_chat_made_tool_is_built_from_catalog_parts_only` in `crates/eval-runner`; `the_bounds_refuse_as_too_large` in `crates/nostr` |
| Every hosted run has the fixed grant, read and sandbox write, in both arms, and never `exec` or `network`, so the child's shell stays off. | New on 2026-09-29. | `a_hosted_run_is_streamed_sealed_and_published_only_on_request` in `crates/eval-runner` |
| The runner has no usage limit (owner decision, 2026-10-01, [#10121](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/10121), following [#10120](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/10120)): a trainer runs as often as they like, and everyone together has no daily turn ceiling; the bounds are the request's own (tests, runs, arms, draft size) and the suites at once, which queue and never refuse. `EVAL_RUNNER_RUNS_PER_DAY` (runs per trainer per UTC day; a check never counts) and `EVAL_RUNNER_TURNS_PER_DAY` (tests × runs × 2 arms per UTC day for everyone, checks included) are an emergency abuse brake only: unset is unlimited, the env example and the deployed environment set neither, a zero or a typo stops the runner, and the startup log and `eval-runner check` say `no usage limit`. When an operator sets one, the counts are written before a run is acknowledged and survive a restart, a run refused or failed before it ran gives its share back, and the refusal (`over_quota`) names no count ("the hosted runner can't take this run right now; try again later"). | New on 2026-09-29 as a quota (3 runs per trainer and 2,000 turns per UTC day). Changed on 2026-10-01 ([#10121](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/10121)): no count by default, an off-by-default brake, neutral refusal text. | `with_no_brake_a_trainer_runs_as_often_as_they_like`, `an_emergency_brake_holds_and_survives_a_restart` in `crates/eval-runner/src/quota.rs`; `a_trainers_fourth_run_of_the_day_runs`, `bad_requests_are_dropped_or_refused_and_the_quota_holds` in `crates/eval-runner/tests/runner.rs` |
| The runner records every request it answers, admitted or refused, as one JSON line in its usage log (`usage/YYYY-MM-DD.jsonl` in its state directory, the UTC day it arrived): time, trainer key, request event id, action (`run`, `check`, `validation`, `publish`), the tool's DefinitionRef id, the test set (its release id or `draft`), tests, runs per arm, turns, outcome and code, verdict and pass counts with and without the tool, the published result's id, the time from arrival to the end, and the request's size. A line holds no test text and no key. A retransmission answered from the ledger is not a new line. `eval-runner usage [--since DATE] [--by key\|action\|subject\|day\|outcome] [--json]` reads it. | New on 2026-10-01 ([#10121](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/10121)), owner decision: record everything so usage stats are easy to pull. | `the_log_rotates_by_day_and_the_stats_read_it_back` in `crates/eval-runner/src/usage.rs`; `a_hosted_run_is_streamed_sealed_and_published_only_on_request`, `a_trainers_fourth_run_of_the_day_runs` in `crates/eval-runner/tests/runner.rs` |
| Nothing becomes public until the trainer who asked sends a publish request naming the run's report: the report is sealed to them as a `3188`, another trainer's publish request is refused, and the publish releases the suite (once, signed by the runner as its author) and the `3189` (once, signed by the runner, naming the trainer with `p` and `request`). | New on 2026-09-29. | `a_hosted_run_is_streamed_sealed_and_published_only_on_request`, `a_hosted_run_over_a_local_relay_credits_its_trainer` (needs `NOSTR_RELAY_TEST_DATABASE_URL`) in `crates/eval-runner` |
| A hosted result carries the trainer's signed `25920` inline in `meta.ext_eval_request`, because relays keep no `25920`; a reader credits the trainer only when that request is the one the report names, its signature holds, and its one `p` is the result's signer. The referee and the phone's ledger credit hosted trainers from it. | New on 2026-09-29. | `a_hosted_result_carries_its_request_inline` in `crates/nostr`; `hosted_results_credit_their_trainers_from_the_inline_request` in `crates/microcoder` |
| A cancel control from the trainer stops the live run's children and answers `cancelled`; a run the runner was restarted during answers `unknown` and is never run again. The admission switch (a `closed` file in the state directory) refuses every new run `not_admitted` while runs already admitted finish. | New on 2026-09-29. | `a_cancel_stops_a_live_run_and_an_admission_switch_closes_the_door`, `a_run_interrupted_by_a_restart_answers_unknown_and_never_reruns` in `crates/eval-runner` |
| The runner never trusts a quiet socket. It proves its requests subscription live with a probe (a `REQ` with `limit` 0 that the relay answers with `EOSE` or `CLOSED`) every 30 seconds, and a probe still unanswered at the next one ends the connection: the runner reconnects with backoff and subscribes again. It renews the subscription every 45 minutes, before the relay's Cloud Run request timeout, on a new connection that is subscribed before the old one is closed, and it reads the old one for 5 seconds after, so a request published during the renewal reaches it, and one delivered on both is handled once and never run twice. Under systemd, each answered probe sends `WATCHDOG=1`, and the unit's `WatchdogSec=120` restarts a runner that stops. | New on 2026-09-29 ([#9946](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/9946)): the chat worker's protection, shared through `coder::relay::liveness`. | `a_relay_that_goes_silent_is_noticed_and_rejoined`, `a_relay_that_answers_its_probes_keeps_the_connection`, `the_subscription_is_renewed_on_an_overlapping_connection` in `crates/eval-runner/tests/liveness.rs`; `an_answered_probe_pets_the_systemd_watchdog` in `crates/eval-runner/tests/watchdog.rs` |
| No door key or decision key appears in any event the runner sends, any answer the trainer decrypts, or any file under its state directory. Keys live in the host's environment file and the runner key file (both mode 0600), and nowhere in the repository. | New on 2026-09-29. | `a_hosted_run_is_streamed_sealed_and_published_only_on_request` in `crates/eval-runner` |
| The catalog test sets (`crates/plugin-*/evals/`: the three starter sets and the example plugins' sets, #10086) load as `openagents ext eval run` loads them, fit the hosted runner, ask for `read` and `write` only, include a should-not-fire test, and grade every should-fire test on what the run found (its trajectory), because a program turn replies with its run summary. A hosted result names a catalog tool by the NIP-EXT release the runner published for it, so the referee can open a quest for it. | New on 2026-09-29. | `the_starter_test_sets_load_and_fit_the_hosted_runner`, `the_starter_extensions_resolve_to_their_guests` in `crates/eval-runner`; `a_hosted_run_over_a_local_relay_credits_its_trainer` in `crates/eval-runner` |

## Process ownership

| Invariant | Status | Checked by |
| --- | --- | --- |
| A supervised job owns its whole process tree until its cleanup ends: a deadline, a dropped future, a stop, and the direct child's own exit each end every process the job started. On Unix the tree is the child's process group; on Windows it is a job object that the child joins while suspended, before it runs a single instruction, and that kills whatever is left in it when its last handle closes. Windows has no signal that asks a program with no window to stop, so there a deadline ends the tree without a grace period, and a job's memory cap is the job object's limit on the tree's committed memory, reported as `JobObject`. A blocking wait on Windows puts the child in its job object when the wait starts. | New on 2026-09-29 ([#9980](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/9980)); the Unix rule is the one `supervise` has kept since it was written. | `a_deadline_ends_the_grandchild_too`, `a_descendant_does_not_outlive_a_child_that_exits`, `a_cancelled_job_takes_its_tree_with_it` in `crates/supervise/tests/tree.rs` and `crates/supervise/tests/windows.rs`; `a_stopped_live_job_leaves_its_job_object_empty`, `a_blocking_wait_ends_the_tree_at_its_deadline`, `on_windows_a_capped_job_is_held_by_its_job_object` (Windows); `a_job_object_counts_and_ends_its_process` in `crates/supervise/src/windows.rs` |
| A terminal's processes end with the terminal: on Unix the child leads a session and process group of its own; on Windows it is a ConPTY console whose program joins a kill-on-close job object before it runs, and close, idle expiry, and host shutdown end the job. A `command` launch runs only a program by absolute path on the host's own platform, and a working directory that names a drive, a root, or a parent refuses as `malformed`. | New on 2026-09-29 for Windows ([#9980](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/9980)). | `host_shutdown_takes_the_terminals_whole_tree`, `close_ends_the_terminal_and_reports_why` in `crates/coder-pty/tests/windows.rs`; `a_program_is_a_unix_or_a_windows_drive_path` in `crates/coder-pty/src/wire.rs` |

## macOS privacy prompts

| Invariant | Status | Checked by |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Nothing OpenAgents runs triggers a macOS privacy prompt. The host's own walks (background measuring, planning, globs, cleaning, session import) never enter a place macOS guards with a privacy prompt (`coder_boundary::privacy`: Desktop, Documents, Downloads, Music, Movies, Pictures, the system apps' data, app containers, iCloud Drive, Apple apps' private caches, `/Volumes`), and a report-only walk also skips every `com.apple.*` and `CloudKit` folder. Every command the host runs on macOS is under a Seatbelt profile that denies those places, Apple Events, the camera, and the microphone: a `Boundary` profile ends with those rules, and a full-access command, a Grok Build, OpenCode, or Devin process, or a terminal's shell runs under a privacy-only profile that allows everything else (but, for a full-access command or a coding agent, writes to the checkout its worktree came from: see Remote task creation, #10247), allows back its workspace, and lets only `/usr/bin/sandbox-exec` start outside it so nested boundaries still work. A `Boundary` profile never has that escape. | New on 2026-10-02 ([#10214](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/10214)): the disk cleanup rule's report-only walk of `~/Library/Caches` made macOS ask the owner whether Coder may access Apple Music. Model boundary: prompts not tied to a path (Contacts or Photos through their frameworks, location, screen recording, local network) are not denied by path; none is reached by a shell command or a walk. Doc: `docs/coder/runtime/privacy-prompts.md`. | `privacy_protected_folders_are_never_walked_or_cleaned` in `crates/background/src/paths.rs`; `crates/coder-boundary/tests/privacy.rs` (macOS: EPERM in a made-up home's `Music`, a checkout allowed back, a boundary nested inside, every boundary denies the real places with no escape); the `privacy` unit tests in `crates/coder-boundary`; `sessions_behind_a_link_into_a_protected_folder_are_not_read` in `crates/coder-host` |

## App versions

| Invariant | Status | Checked by |
| --- | --- | --- |
| The desktop app ships at the phone app's version, in lockstep. `MARKETING_VERSION` in `bins/openagents-ios/host/project.yml` (the TestFlight version) is the one source of truth: the Android build reads it, the desktop packaging scripts (`bins/openagents-desktop-macos/bundle.sh`, `scripts/desktop/package-macos.sh`, `package-linux.sh`, `package-windows.ps1`, `package-windows.sh`) read it, and `crates/openagents-desktop`'s Cargo version (the updater's running version), the Mac `Info.plist`, and Android's fallback `versionName` must equal it. A version change moves them together; the packaging scripts refuse a desktop crate that differs. | New on 2026-09-29: the desktop app went from 0.1.0 to the phone's 1.0.0. Installed 0.1.0 apps take 1.0.0 through the signed update manifest. | `crates/openagents-desktop/tests/version_lockstep.rs`; `a_0_1_0_install_upgrades_to_1_0_0` in `crates/openagents-desktop/src/update.rs` |

## Desktop updates

| Invariant | Status | Checked by |
| --- | --- | --- |
| The desktop app installs an update only when a manifest signed by a key compiled into `TRUSTED_KEYS` names it by size and SHA-256, the signature is checked over the payload's bytes before they are parsed, and the version is newer than the running one. A manifest is for one platform: the Mac's names the OpenAgents Team ID and no platform, and the new bundle must pass `codesign`, notarization, and carry that Team ID; a Linux manifest names `"platform": "linux"`, no Team ID, and each artifact's `format` (`appimage` or `deb`); a Windows manifest names `"platform": "windows"`, no Team ID, and each artifact's `format` (`msi` or `zip`); and each app refuses the others'. An AppImage replaces its own file with one rename in its folder, after the copy there is checked against the signed SHA-256 again and made executable; any failure leaves the old file. An app installed from the `.deb` never replaces files: it offers the download. On Windows an app installed from the per-user MSI (`OpenAgents.exe` in `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Programs\OpenAgents`) stages the new MSI only after its header and the signed SHA-256 pass, checks the SHA-256 again when **Restart to update** is chosen, and hands it to Windows Installer only after the window exits; a copy from the `.zip` offers the download, and a development build never checks. Only Authenticode-signed Windows packages are published to `desktop/windows`; unsigned ones go only to a test prefix. `OPENAGENTS_UPDATE_MANIFEST_URL` only changes where the manifest is read; the compiled keys still decide. | Mac on 2026-09-29 ([#9972](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/9972)); Linux on 2026-09-30 ([#10025](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/10025)); Windows on 2026-09-30 ([#10027](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/10027)). Model boundary: the MSI's own Authenticode signature is checked by Windows when it runs, not by the updater. | `a_tampered_manifest_is_refused`, `a_downgrade_is_refused`, `a_linux_manifest_is_only_for_linux`, `a_linux_install_takes_its_own_format`, `an_appimage_replaces_itself_with_the_signed_build`, `a_failed_replacement_leaves_the_appimage_alone`, `a_deb_install_is_offered_the_download`, `the_release_key_signs_what_the_app_accepts`, `the_release_key_signs_a_linux_manifest_the_linux_app_accepts`, `a_windows_manifest_is_only_for_windows`, `a_windows_install_is_recognized_by_where_it_runs`, `only_an_msi_header_is_accepted`, `an_msi_install_stages_the_signed_msi_and_a_zip_is_offered_the_download`, `the_release_key_signs_a_windows_manifest_the_windows_app_accepts` in `crates/openagents-desktop/src/update.rs`; the `BUILDINFO` check in `scripts/desktop/sign-manifest-windows.sh` |

## Desktop theme

| Invariant | Status | Checked by |
| --- | --- | --- |
| The desktop app paints in the person's Theme setting (`app.theme` in the settings file): `system` by default, which follows the system's light or dark appearance and reads as dark when the system does not say, `light` (Coder Light), or `dark`. Every window surface reads its colors from the seam; the Verse's 3D scenes keep their art, the slides keep the deck's own look, and the pairing QR code stays black on white. Only the window reads that appearance (`event_loop.system_theme()` in `rust_native_desktop::window`, on opening, `ThemeChanged`, and coming to the front) and hands it to the app (`App::system_appearance`); the app resolves its scheme once at its theme seam (`openagents_chat_app::visual::set_scheme`), its views read colors from `visual::current()`, and the window asks for the appearance of the theme the app returns. Nothing that lays out or paints reads the system's appearance. Light values come from the shared token table (`oa-tokens`, the same values as the web's `data-theme="light"`); the dark look is unchanged; a token change is a visual change and updates the pinned snapshot. Terminal UIs (Coder's terminal interface, `coder-terminal`, OpenAgents Terminal) stay Coder Noir only. | New on 2026-09-30 ([#10022](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/10022)) as dark only. Relaxed on 2026-10-08 ([#11028](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/11028), the Apps SDK UI adoption plan's phase 4): Coder Light is offered by choice. Default became `system` on 2026-10-08 (#11028) once every desktop surface painted from the seam; the phones do not set the seam yet and keep painting dark. Model boundary: on a Mac a system change made while the app is in front is picked up when the window next comes to the front. | `the_default_theme_is_dark_and_only_the_window_reads_the_system`, `the_openagents_tokens_match_their_snapshot` in `crates/rust-native-desktop/src/theme.rs`; `the_shell_stays_dark_and_the_same_under_every_motion_setting` in `crates/openagents-desktop/src/main.rs`; `the_theme_switches_to_light_and_back` in `crates/openagents-desktop/src/settings_shell.rs`; `the_light_look_is_coder_light_and_the_dark_look_is_unchanged`, `a_scoped_scheme_stays_on_its_thread_and_ends_with_the_scope` in `crates/openagents-chat-app/src/visual.rs`; `a_missing_or_broken_section_is_the_defaults` in `crates/openagents-chat-app/src/preferences.rs`; `the_light_palette_is_the_tables_light_side`, `the_choice_follows_the_system_unless_the_person_overrides_it` in `crates/oa-tokens` |
| The phone apps paint dark unless the person chooses otherwise: Account > Appearance is `dark` by default (the shared `Preferences` default), `light` (Coder Light), or `system`, saved as the `theme` file in the app's state directory. Only the hosts read the phone's appearance (iOS: the screen's `userInterfaceStyle`; Android: `uiMode`'s night bits) and report it to Rust (`system_appearance`); Rust resolves the scheme once (`openagents_mobile::appearance`, which sets `openagents_chat_app::visual::set_scheme`), its Rust Native views read `visual::inks()`, and the hosts paint their own chrome, color scheme and status bar from the packet's `appearance` palette. The dark look is unchanged. Terminal panes stay Coder Noir in both themes. | New on 2026-10-08 ([#11028](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/11028)). Model boundary: host surfaces not yet converted (the Verse, Wallet, Gym and Playtest screens and the sheets on iOS) stay dark in both themes; on Android a theme change mid-session rebuilds the shell, chat, Gym, Wallet and Account, and other views take the new colors when next built. | `the_choice_persists_and_system_follows_the_phone`, `the_light_palette_is_coder_light` in `crates/openagents-mobile/src/appearance.rs`; `the_theme_is_saved_and_system_follows_the_phone` in `crates/openagents-mobile/src/tests.rs`; `the_inks_follow_the_scheme` in `crates/openagents-chat-app/src/visual.rs` |
| Reduced motion, from the system (macOS Reduce motion, the Linux desktop portal or GNOME animations, Windows Show animations) or the person's Reduce motion switch in Settings, stops non-essential animation: decorative motion durations are zero and the Grid on the Verse page draws one still frame per showing. Reduced motion never changes a static view's pixels. | New on 2026-09-30 ([#10022](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/10022)). | `reduced_motion_zeroes_decoration_and_keeps_what_is_essential`, `the_persons_switch_or_the_system_reduces_motion` in `crates/rust-native-desktop/src/theme.rs`; `reduce_motion_draws_one_still_frame_per_showing` in `crates/openagents-desktop/src/backdrop.rs` |

## Desktop accessibility

| Invariant | Status | Checked by |
| --- | --- | --- |
| A screen reader reaches a Rust Native desktop window only through the input a person already gives it. `rust_native_desktop::access` builds the AccessKit tree from the validated view as laid out, and a request becomes an activation resolved against the current view (a click's or Enter's), a Tab focus move the application allows, a press and release at a control's on-screen bounds in a surface, or the select-all and typed text a composer takes. A disabled button, text, and a control that is off screen admit no action, and no request carries an intent, a node the view does not have, or a destination of its own. Nothing is built or sent until a screen reader asks. | New on 2026-09-30 ([#10024](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/10024)). | `requests_become_the_input_the_window_already_handles`, `a_screen_reader_reads_the_sidebar_the_replies_and_the_composer` in `crates/rust-native-desktop` (`access`); `a_screen_reader_writes_and_sends_a_message`, `a_screen_reader_opens_another_chat_from_the_sidebar` in `crates/openagents-desktop` (bin, `access_tests`). A live VoiceOver and Orca pass is an owner check (`NEEDS_OWNER.md`), a model-boundary exception. |

| Invariant | Status | Checked by |
| --- | --- | --- |

A plugin's status comes only from records `nostr::eval_ext::parse_publication` verified (`records.json`, written by `live_route_map_records`), matched by its package's publisher and slug; a result signed for another publisher's package of the same slug is not its result. | New on 2026-10-01 ([#10085](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/10085)). | `the_route_map_sources_match_the_router` in `crates/coder/tests/route_map_sources.rs`; `every_route_of_the_router_is_on_the_map`, `the_catalog_plugins_have_their_real_statuses`, `the_plugin_ladder_reads_the_records` in `crates/openagents-chat-app` (`route_map`) |
| This person's route counts are counted on their computer from their saved chats' typed judgments (`Command::Routes`, read-only, which reads the store without keeping the turns) and never leave it: the map shows them and nothing sends them anywhere. The Map page exists only while it shows: opening it builds it, and leaving it drops it, so no other page pays for it. A next step on the map never runs by itself: a tap puts a message in a new chat's composer unsent, copies a command, opens a page, or opens Settings. | New on 2026-10-01 ([#10085](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/10085)). | `the_map_counts_this_persons_routes_locally`, `the_footer_opens_the_map_and_leaving_releases_it`, `a_chat_step_drafts_a_message_unsent` in `crates/openagents-desktop` (bin, `route_map_shell_tests`); `next_steps_become_effects` in `crates/openagents-desktop/src/route_map_tests.rs` |

## Pay front

`openagents pay serve` (`crates/x402::front`) is the one process that holds
the receiver wallet, the replay store, and the settlement hook for many
priced routes ([central receive and splits](docs/payments/2026-10-02-central-receive-and-splits.md)).

| Invariant | Status | Checked by |
| --- | --- | --- |
| A payment hash buys one call: every `402` carries one invoice, bound to the request hash, in both x402 (`PAYMENT-REQUIRED`) and the HTTP `Payment` scheme (`WWW-Authenticate`, whose id HMACs the body digest, method, and URL), and either proof is consumed in the one replay store under `network:payment_hash`, so a proof is refused on another route, on another body, and in the other scheme. After a valid proof the settlement hook runs before the route; a hook error answers 503, runs nothing, and releases the key so the same proof can be presented again. The hook therefore records each executed payment exactly once. | New on 2026-10-02 ([#10186](https://github.com/OpenAgentsInc/openagents/issues/10186)). Releasing the key on a hook error departs from the Lightning charge draft's "consumed even if delivery fails", which still holds once the hook has recorded the payment. Model boundary: the hook must be idempotent per payment hash, because a crash between a successful hook and the response leaves the key consumed with the record written. | `two_routes_share_one_replay_store_across_both_schemes`, `a_refusing_hook_never_executes_and_the_proof_survives`, `serves_two_routes_over_a_socket`, `lnget_built_credential_is_accepted` in `crates/x402/tests/front.rs` |
